# DRIFT — Decisions Log
*Standing-authority decisions with rationale, newest first. Forks that went to the director are marked ⚑. PARKed ideas live at the bottom. Canonical home: repo `/docs/` from Cycle 01 onward.*

---

**D-165 · 2026-08-17 — COMBINE MAKES THE THING NOW, ALL OF IT; AND RETIRING A SURFACE TOOK TWO INVARIANTS WITH IT.**

**THE HALF-RULE IS CLOSED.** [[D-164]] gave shelter and storage a real build from the slate and left every hand-held outcome bumping a plan version — *"You refine your plan for X"* — so a crate went up and a spear did not. Every known outcome is now MADE on the spot: `Game.MAKERS` routes each recipe id to its own SHIPPED craft function, unchanged, so nothing about making an axe moved except which surface asks for one. Measured on the real surface: `tools.spear false -> true` with **plan version 1 -> 1**, `spent wood 3, blade 1, fibre 2`, tap `combine:spear:made`.

**TWO SHAPES, ONE VERB, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS ONLY WHERE THE THING LANDS.** Placed outcomes ask WHERE — arm a siting, spend nothing, build on the tap that picks the spot. Hand-held outcomes ask nothing at all, because there is no question: a spear goes in your hands and your hands are where you are. A confirm step there would be ceremony. Both draw through `drawIntoHands`, so an open crate feeds a spear exactly as it feeds a shelter.

**THE BUILD PANEL COULD NOT BE DELETED, AND THE DEPENDENTS WERE CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** All eight craft rows are gone, with `buildItemMarkup` and `raftMarkup`. What survives, survives because it has nowhere else to be: **KNAP** is a ONE-slot recipe and staging wants two materials, so it can never reach the slate — and sharp blades gate the axe and the spear, so deleting its button would strand the tree. **SLEEP ROUGH** exists only there; the circle's `sleep` verb wants a shelter to aim at. The refuge line is a reading, the hints are the teaching half of the pivot, and **MEND** is genuinely redundant (the shelter's own circle carries it) and kept only because removing a working control was not this batch's ruling.

**THE SHELTER IS A SHELTER, AND NOTHING NEEDED RESTRUCTURING TO MAKE THAT TRUE.** Asked whether the tier model needed rework: **no**. There is no ladder — `ShelterState` is one structure with `built`, `durability` and named `defects`, and `shelter` is a single recipe id. "Lean-to" was a word in the prose, never a rung, so "improvements make THIS shelter better" is already the only thing the model can express. A pure display fix in three places: the name, the migrated-save label, and the refuge sentence. A test asserts no recipe name anywhere still reads "lean-to".

**AND RETIRING THE ROWS COST TWO THINGS, WHICH ARE NAMED HERE RATHER THAN DISCOVERED LATER.**

**FIRST, [[D-160]]'s RULING IS GONE, NOT RELOCATED.** That batch ruled that a recipe you have earned keeps its row when the materials run short, showing the shortfall instead of vanishing. It does not transfer: slate chips exist only for materials genuinely in reach, so a survivor with no wood cannot stage wood and therefore cannot see the spear AT ALL. The panel used to show it as `Wood 0 / 3`. Two rulings in tension, this one implemented as directed, and the loss recorded in `PANEL`'s own comment where the check used to be.

**SECOND, THE HARNESS IS BADLY OUT OF DATE, AND THE SCHEDULE RULE IS WHY NOBODY SAW IT.** Thirty-five references across the device harness drive selectors that no longer exist — `try-combine-btn` (8, dead since [[D-163]]) and ten craft buttons (27, dead as of this batch). Twenty checks across six sections are red on main, and **the product is correct in every one of them**: they assert surfaces that were deliberately retired. They went unseen because active-hours discipline means only the sections being touched are ever run, so a retirement's blast radius is invisible until something runs the rest. That is a real hole in the process and not merely a to-do: the rule that keeps runs cheap also keeps their consequences hidden.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (`Game.MAKERS` and the hand-held branch of `onCombine` in `src/body/game.ts`; `recipeCost` in `src/brain/experiment.ts`; the craft rows' removal from `src/body/hud.ts`; the `Shelter` rename; shipped in this batch).

**NO SAVE MIGRATION.** No state shape changed — the makers are the shipped ones and write the fields they always wrote. The migrated-save blueprint LABEL changed, which is a string in a v-migration and not a shape.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1461 passed / 3 skipped** across 80 files, +17. Two earlier reds were my own concurrency — a unit run against a HELD bench lock, caught by `the bench mutex` test itself — and the suite is clean with the bench free. **DEVICE: MAKES 19/19**, plus PANEL, SLATE and MERGE re-run together so a neighbour cannot drift unseen. **FAIL-THEN-PASS, both changes:** restoring the tier name fails on `expected 'Lean-to frame' to be 'Shelter'`; removing the maker lookup returns the exact original defect — `plan version 1 -> 2` with `tools.spear` unmoved, cost `1/1/0` instead of `3/1/2`, and the tap reading `combine:spear:invented`. **LIVE:** the push SHA and served-SHA gate in this batch's report.*

**NAMED, NOT FIXED:** the thirty-five stale selector references and the twenty red legacy checks, which want a batch of their own; and the [[D-160]] affordance, which wants a ruling.

**D-164 · 2026-08-16 — THE LAST TWO THINGS WITH THEIR OWN WAY IN JOINED THE SLATE, AND A CRATE STOPPED BEING SOMETHING YOU STAND NEXT TO AND CANNOT USE.**

**SHELTER AND STORAGE WERE ALREADY ORDINARY RECIPES; WHAT THEY HAD WAS A PRIVATE DOOR.** They sit in `matchPool` like everything else and have appeared in the slate — named when demonstrated, anonymous when not — since [[D-163]]. What made them special was the ENTRY: a hold on open ground opened a card that asked WHAT and WHERE at once, so a survivor had to already be standing somewhere buildable before the game would admit crates existed. That is the catalogue problem wearing a gesture. The pile names the thing now, and the tap that follows places it.

**THE ENTRY QUESTION, ANSWERED AND WITH A REASON THAT IS NOT ONLY TASTE.** Asked whether slate visibility should still require standing somewhere placeable: **no**, and not merely for consistency. If an anonymous slot appeared and disappeared as the survivor walked, they could triangulate which unknown was the shelter by stepping on and off buildable ground — **a location-gated slate is an identity leak**. So shelter and storage list wherever you are, and the world's veto is spoken at the placement step instead of silently hiding a slot. `openSiteCard` is deleted and the Build panel's two rows with it; `PANEL` now asserts their ABSENCE while earned, so quietly restoring them reads as a contradiction rather than a harmless extra.

**A PLACED OUTCOME COMMITS IN TWO BEATS, AND THE FIRST ONE IS FREE.** Combine arms a siting and spends nothing; the tap that picks the spot runs the geometry veto FIRST, then charges, then builds through the same `placeAtSite` the card always used. A refused spot RE-ARMS rather than cancelling — being made to walk back through the pack for aiming badly is a punishment for aiming. Measured: `"Tap where the crate should stand."` with `wood 14 -> 14`, then `built true at (-0.0, 95.2)` with `wood 14 -> 9`.

**AND THE FIRST CUT CHARGED TWICE, WHICH A CHECK CAUGHT INSIDE ONE RUN.** It called `makeChosen` as well, so a crate costing 5 wood took 6 — one per staged material for an "invention", then the recipe price for the build. On a SECOND crate, whose plan is already held, that is a surcharge for nothing at all. Combine only ever offers outcomes already demonstrated, so there is no invention to pay for: the build IS the act. **The loose assertion missed it** — `spent < before` is true of 6 as well as 5 — and only the exact-cost check bit. Both paths now assert the precise figure.

**AND A BOX YOU ARE STANDING AT IS NOW A BOX YOU CAN REACH INTO.** `reachFor` merges held and stored into ONE count map, which the existing gate, slate and charge consume unchanged — no second combine system. Spending is HANDS FIRST, BOX SECOND, in both paths: it matters only when a material is split, and hands-first means a survivor who empties a crate into a build ends up carrying less, never mysteriously more. The gate's refusal changes wording with the reach (*"in hand"* versus *"in the box"*) so nobody is told to look in the wrong place. The SLATE is deliberately untouched by any of it and a test says so: the slate is about KINDS, the reach about COUNTS, so `unknownCount` still equals `matchPool.length` with the box wide open.

**`buildStorage` AND `buildShelter` READ INVENTORY AND NOTHING ELSE**, so rather than teach them — and every future builder — about a second source, `drawIntoHands` moves what is needed out of the crate first and then builds exactly as before. Which is what a person does: you take the timber out of the box before you raise the frame. Measured, with empty hands: `box wood 20 -> 12, stone 20 -> 16, fibre 20 -> 17` — 8/4/3, a shelter's price and not a penny of surface tax.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (`PLACED_OUTCOMES`/`isPlaced`, `reachFor`, `spendFromReach`, `drawIntoHands`, `placedCost` in `src/brain/experiment.ts`; the siting mode and `placeFromSlate` in `src/body/game.ts`; shipped in this batch).

**NO SAVE MIGRATION.** The reach is computed per combine from live state and stored nowhere, which is also why [[D-011]] is untouched: there is no field an absence path could advance, and a test asserts the reach never leaked into the save shape.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1444 passed / 3 skipped** across 79 files, +23, including D-011's four property tests and Law 95 re-asserted with the box open. **DEVICE: 24/24** in a new `MERGE` section plus the updated `PANEL`, `--only` targeted, no full sweep. **FAIL-THEN-PASS, both changes:** forcing `withStorage` false turns the box refusals back to *"in hand"* and inventions back into refusals; restoring the double charge makes the shelter cost `9/5/4` against a wanted `8/4/3`. **LIVE:** the push SHA and served-SHA gate in this batch's report.*

**NAMED, NOT FIXED — and it is the top candidate for the next list.** Combine on a HAND-HELD known outcome still only refines the plan (*"You refine your plan for X"*); the Build panel is what turns plans into objects. So a crate now builds from the slate while a spear does not, which will read as broken the first time it is met. The fix is to make Combine make the thing for hand-held outcomes too, which retires most of what is left of the Build panel — a larger change than this batch was scoped for, and one that should be ruled on rather than assumed.

**D-163 · 2026-08-16 — THE PILE TELLS YOU WHAT IT COULD BE BEFORE YOU SPEND IT, AND FIRE STOPPED WAITING FOR DARK.**

**FIRE'S SCAFFOLD NO LONGER ASKS WHAT TIME IT IS.** The condition was never in `fireIsKnown` — it was `discovery.ts`'s torch route, whose need read `isNight || warmth < low`, so the *thought* was available only to a survivor already cold or already in the dark. That is a defensible model of NEED and a bad model of DISCOVERY: a person holding a stick and a handful of dry fibre at midday can work out what they are for, and making them wait for dusk is the game withholding an idea until its cue. The need is now unconditional; `makings` still gates it on wood AND fibre genuinely in hand, and a suspicion is still not a row — `revealedInPanel` wants `demonstrated`, which only making one can give. Law 130's own comment records the superseded trigger rather than being quietly rewritten, and the prompt changed with it: *"The dark is closing in"* would have been a lie at noon.

**AND THE CRAFTING SURFACE STOPPED ANSWERING AFTER THE FACT.** The old flow staged a pile, committed, and *then* said something — three message branches deciding retrospectively what the survivor should have been told. The slate replaces all of it: every outcome a pile could produce, listed live, updating on every chip, before anything is spent. Known outcomes are named and selectable; unknown ones are a grey, inert slot.

**LAW 95 IS HELD BY THE TYPE, NOT BY THE RENDERER, AND THAT IS THE LOAD-BEARING DECISION.** An unknown outcome contributes to `unknownCount` — an INTEGER — and to nothing else. No id, no name, no domain, no ordering. **A body cannot leak an identity it was never handed, however carelessly it renders.** Every previous version of this rule lived in a sentence somebody had to remember not to write, and this log has three decisions' worth of evidence about how that ends. Measured on the real DOM: two anonymous slots rendering as `"?" [class=quiet slate-slot unknown type=button disabled aria-disabled=true]`, byte-identical, `distinct renderings: 1`. Planting a leak required widening the brain contract AND changing the markup — and both the unit suite and the device check caught it, the latter through `data-leak=storage` in the ATTRIBUTES rather than the text.

**TWO VERBS, BECAUSE THEY ARE TWO INTENTIONS.** Combine commits to a thing you know. Discover commits to finding out what a grey slot is. The old flow made those one press with a question in between, so wanting to experiment meant first being offered something you already knew and then declining it. Which unknown Discover lands on is NOT decided by the new code: `resolveRecipe`'s undiscovered-first tie-break already answers that and gives the same answer the generic path gave — this adds a door, not a rule. Witnessed end to end: `plans [storage] → [storage, stonehammer]`.

**WHAT WAS REUSED AND WHAT IS NEW**, since the question was asked directly. Reused unchanged: `matchPool`/`recipesMatching`, `resolveRecipe`, `makeChosen`'s guards, `announcementFor`. New: `combineSlate`, `discoverWith`, the slate UI, `onCombine`/`onDiscover`. **Deleted:** `onTryCombine` — 82 lines of verb-circle staging, checked first for whether the debug hook still needed it (it does not; `runtime.tryCombine` calls the brain directly). No save migration: the slate is derived and no persisted shape changed.

**TEMPORARY, AND WIRED SO IT CANNOT BE FORGOTTEN.** `COMBINE_ALWAYS_SUCCEEDS` makes both verbs succeed every time, by explicit direction, with the failure question parked. It is EXPORTED, and the three tests that assert failure are `skipIf(COMBINE_ALWAYS_SUCCEEDS)` rather than deleted or blind-skipped — flipping one constant restores the behaviour and its checks in the same edit. **Stated plainly because it was mis-stated to me:** this is the ONLY caller of `transformOnFailure`, so Law 128's failure-transform is DORMANT, not "intact for other paths" — there is no separate axe-wear consumer; `matterWear` has exactly this one. `matter.ts`, its save field and its tests all stay and come straight back.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (`combineSlate`/`discoverWith`/`COMBINE_ALWAYS_SUCCEEDS` in `src/brain/experiment.ts`; the torch route in `discovery.ts`; the slate in `src/body/hud.ts` and its wiring in `game.ts`; shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1421 passed / 3 skipped** across 78 files, +12 for the slate including Law 95 asserted over six different piles. **DEVICE: 23/23** in a new `SLATE` section, `--only` targeted, no full sweep. **FAIL-THEN-PASS, both changes:** restoring the torch route's clock fails on `daylight still withheld the idea`; leaking an identity into the grey slot fails 5 unit cases and 2 device checks. **LIVE:** the push SHA and served-SHA gate in this batch's report.*

**FUTURE, NAMED NOT BUILT:** material icons in place of names (cosmetic); and discovery-difficulty weighting by complexity — a spear easier to work out alone than fire, fire easier than a steam engine — which is a real skills/knowledge-weighting system, unscoped and deliberately untouched here.

**D-162 · 2026-08-16 — A TAP ON NOTHING DOES NOTHING AGAIN, AND THE CHECK THAT SAID SO WAS PROVING NOTHING.**

**THE RULING REVERSES THIS BRANCH'S OWN FIX, AND THE ARGUMENT IS KEPT RATHER THAN DELETED.** [[D-153]]'s reasoning was that silence on bare ground was a [[D-042]] breach — every other outcome in `onTap` acts or explains itself, and eight opening taps answered with nothing reads as a dead game. That reasoning stands on its own terms and was still wrong for this game: a turn and a chime on EVERY tap that hits nothing is the whole beach answering back, and aimless taps are most of the early game. Feedback that fires on the null case is not feedback, it is noise with a rationale. So `faceToward` and `cues.play(CUES.target)` are gone from that path, and the comment above it now carries both the argument and the override — so the next reader finds the reasoning already answered instead of rediscovering it and re-applying it.

**THE COUNTING STAYS, AND WAS NEVER IN DISPUTE.** `markGroundTap` and the `empty-ground` breadcrumb are how the miss rate is known at all — the number whose absence cost three sessions of guessing — and both are silent by construction. Being measured is not the same as being answered; only the answer went. Witnessed separately from the silence, because on screen "silent" and "not counted" are the same picture: eight real taps on a patch the SHIPPED pick path confirms is empty took `trace.groundTaps` from 3 to 11 and left eight `empty-ground` breadcrumbs, while `rotY` held at 3.1416 and nothing was said.

**AND THE CUE CHECK WAS VACUOUS UNTIL THE PLANT CAUGHT IT.** With the reverted `cues.play(CUES.target)` planted straight back in, the turn check went red and *the cue check stayed green* — `runtime.lastCue` reads `lastCuePlayed`, a field exactly two call sites assign by hand, so it has never reflected `Cues.play` at all. Shipping on that run would have left the audible half of the ruling with no witness whatsoever. `Cues.play` now records every REQUEST at the top of the method, **before** the `!this.ready` early return: headless audio is frequently never ready, so "did a sound come out" is green either way, and what a ruling about feedback needs to see is whether the game ASKED. Exposed read-only as `cuePlays()` / `forgetCuePlays()` ([[D-075]]). Re-run against the same plant: `cues requested: [target, target, target, target, target, target, target, target]`.

**`faceToward` IS KEPT THOUGH NOTHING CALLS IT**, behind a `@ts-expect-error` that `noUnusedLocals` genuinely requires — verified by removing the directive and watching `TS6133` fire. `onBuildFire` and `onBuildShelter` both place by `this.facing`, so aiming by looking is a real affordance this game may want back on a DELIBERATE gesture. What the ruling forbids is spending it on the null case.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (the empty-ground branch in `src/body/game.ts`; the cue-request log in `src/body/audio.ts` with its `runtime` hooks; the `GROUND` device section — all shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1411/1411** across 77 files, unchanged by design: this is a body-layer ruling and the brain's counting is untouched. **DEVICE: 6/6** in a new `GROUND` section, `--only` targeted, active hours, no full sweep. **FAIL-THEN-PASS:** restoring the turn and the cue fails BOTH player-facing checks — `rotY 3.1416 -> 1.8275` and eight `target` requests — while the counting checks stay green, which is exactly the separation the ruling draws.*

**D-161 · 2026-08-16 — THE PILE HAD NO PIXEL, THE COLD HAD NO VOICE, AND THE SIX-ZONE RULE HAD BEEN OVERRULED.**

**THE ZONE COUNT WAS A SUPERSEDED SPEC, NOT A BUG.** v0_7 §9 named six fixed access zones, and [[D-157]] deliberately made two of them conditional — the hub must not name a backpack nobody owns or a crate nobody built. The check still asserted the old six and had been red on main since that ruling shipped. It now asserts what was actually ruled: the four body zones, a carry row that is honest about whether it is a pack or your arms, and no Storage row until one exists. Measured: `Active hand | Support hand | Belt | Pockets | Backpack`.

**"DROPPED ITEMS STILL VANISH" WAS RIGHT, AND HAD BEEN ROOT-CAUSED ONE LAYER TOO SHALLOW.** The brain was correct and always had been: `dropAll` writes a stack at the player's feet, `pruneDropped` is online-only so [[D-011]] holds, save v20 migrates the array. **THREE separate body-layer pieces were missing, and each one alone was enough to produce the report:**

1. **No mesh.** `state.dropped` had zero lines of render code anywhere. The pile existed in state and had no pixel.
2. **No tap target.** `worldCandidateAt` had no `dropped` kind — so `verbs.ts`'s `dropped` target and its `pick-up` verb, both shipped with the feature, were **unreachable through the world**. A reachability proof that bypasses the surface, [[D-114]]'s exact class.
3. **No walk target.** `pendingTarget` had no `dropped` case and ended in a catch-all `return { x: POND.x, z: POND.y }` — so a tap that finally resolved correctly walked the survivor **to the pond** and did nothing on arrival.

The third is why the earlier fix read as working: state was right at every step, and the player watched their wood disappear. **That fail-open default is the defect behind the defect** — silent mis-routing of any target nobody remembered to wire — so the pond is now named explicitly and an unlisted kind returns null to be refused out loud ([[D-042]]).

**AND THE COLD COULD KILL YOU WITHOUT EVER SAYING A WORD.** Illness has had a five-rung grammar since the Medicine Slice whose first two rungs cost nothing and SAY something. Cold had the identical five-rung shape in `thermalStrain` — and it was read in **exactly one place in the whole codebase**, to decide whether wet plus hypothermic seeds a chill. The only cold signal a player ever got was a HUD bar changing colour, while health drains 6/hour from the moment warmth hits zero. So the rungs are mapped onto illness's rather than invented: `chilled` is `unsettled`, `freezing` is `ailing`, and empty warmth is where the diagnostic voice belongs — which is why `coldStage` reads WARMTH and not the strain band, since that band calls 12 and 0 both "hypothermic" and the difference between them is the whole fair-challenge line.

**HONEST LIMIT, RECORDED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER:** cold's warning rungs are free of HEALTH cost, not of all cost. `impairmentOf` has always charged `cold` 0.5 and `hypothermic` 1.0, where `illnessImpairmentShare` returns 0 until `feverish`. Closing that is a balance change, not a grammar one, and is not what this did.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (`DroppedView` and the `dropped` target/pending/walk path in `src/body`; `coldStage`/`coldSymptom` in `src/brain/thermal.ts` with its watcher in `session.ts` and `announceCold` in `game.ts`; all shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1411/1411**, +6 for the cold grammar including a sweep of every warmth asserting nothing costs health before something has spoken; the D-011 offline-death property re-run green. **DEVICE: 20/20** in a new `ITEMS` section plus the corrected zone check, `--only` targeted, active hours, no full sweep. **FAIL-THEN-PASS, both fixes:** removing the disclosure sentence fails the cold suite on `chilled said nothing`; removing the mesh turns four ITEM 1 checks red at once and reproduces the report exactly — `stacks 1` in state, `enabled false`, tap `resolved to "null"`, `wood 0 -> 0`. **LIVE:** the push SHA and served-SHA gate in this batch's report.*

**D-160 · 2026-08-16 — WHAT YOU HAVE EARNED ALREADY OUTLIVES YOUR MATERIALS; WHAT WAS MISSING IS THE TRIPWIRE.**

**ASKED TO CONFIRM BEFORE CHANGING ANYTHING, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT NOTHING NEEDED CHANGING.** `revealedInPanel` is `atLeast(ladderFor(state, id), 'demonstrated')`, `ladderFor` reads `state.blueprints`, and there is no inventory term anywhere in that chain. Measured across all eleven recipes: `known+rich` true, `known+EMPTY` true, `unknown+rich` false, every one. The row that renders on a shortfall is `buildItemMarkup`'s ordinary one — `x / n` gates marked `unmet`, a *"from driftwood on the sand…"* source hint, and a **disabled "Not enough yet"** button. The only thing that removes a row is `!item.revealed`.

**PROVEN THE WAY THE RULING WAS WRITTEN, on the real panel rather than on the predicate.** A stone hammer was worked out through the staging UI — no granted blueprint — and then the inventory was stripped to nothing. The row stayed, reading `Wood 0 / 2`, `Stone 0 / 3`, button disabled and saying *"Not enough yet"*. All ten earned recipes then survived an empty inventory together, each showing its own shortfall; and with full pockets and nothing demonstrated the panel listed **no recipe at all**, which is the scope boundary in the other direction.

**SO THIS BATCH SHIPS THE TRIPWIRE, NOT A FIX, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** "True by construction" is exactly what stops being true the first time someone adds an inventory term to a gate — the shape [[D-150]] and [[D-158]] both wore. The unit case sweeps quantities 0, 1, 2, 5 and 99 per recipe and fails if any of the five disagree, so the failure surfaces as *"gated on how much is held"* rather than as a player noticing a row went missing. Planting that defect into `reveal.ts` turns `torch` false at zero materials and returns `[false, true]` from the sweep; restoring returns 4/4.

**AND ONE CHECK WAS VACUOUS BEFORE IT WAS RIGHT.** The first device run reported `Shelter:0short` and a Law 95 leak on `Shelter`. Neither was real: the panel's first `.build-item` is the F3 refuge report, whose head also reads "Shelter" and which has no gates by design, so a title match found it before the buildable row. Recipe rows are the ones carrying an `<h2>`; the selector now says so. Recorded because a green run on that selector would have proven nothing about the Shelter row at all.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (no product change — the mechanism is the instrumentation: `tests/panel.test.ts` and the `PANEL` device section, both shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), docs-integrity. **UNIT: +4**, including the quantity sweep and both halves of the scope boundary. **DEVICE: 8/8** in a new `PANEL` section, `--only` targeted, active hours, no full sweep. **FAIL-THEN-PASS:** an inventory term planted into `revealedInPanel` fails the sweep on the exact assertion. **LIVE:** the push SHA and served-SHA gate in this batch's report.*

**D-159 · 2026-08-16 — 13 + 10 IS THE SAME PILE AS 14 + 13, AND GETTING A HAMMER IS THE DESIGN WORKING.**

**THE EXACT QUANTITIES, ON A FRESH INCOGNITO SAVE, ON PRODUCTION.** `2ab45d9`, wood 13 and stone 10, no plans held, no crate built, `experimentCount` 0, zero journalled dead ends — the save's own defaults, with only the two counts written. Staged through the real DOM, the staging surface said: *"You have not worked these out yet, and there is more than one thing here. Put them together and see?"* **The disclosure clause is present at these quantities.**

**QUANTITY IS NOT A CODE PATH, AND THAT IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN LUCKY.** `recipesMatching` assigns material KINDS to slot tags through `assignable` and never reads a count; `canExperimentWith` gates only on `inventory[m] > 0`; the costs (`storage` 5 wood + 3 stone, `stonehammer` 2 + 3) are charged AFTER the naming branch returns, not before it. So `matchPool(['wood','stone'])` is `[storage, stonehammer]` at every quantity the gate admits, and 13+10, 14+13 and 1+1 build the identical sentence — asserted as an equality rather than three separate matches.

**AND RESOLVING TO ONE REAL OUTCOME IS CORRECT.** The probe followed the survivor's own click through: attempt 1 produced the stone hammer, floated as *"Stone hammer — you see how it works"*, and cost 1 wood + 1 stone. Attempt 2 on the same pile then offered `Stone hammer` **plus** *"Try something new"* — and produced storage. That is `resolveRecipe` step 2 preferring undiscovered outcomes, which exists precisely so a tie's loser stays reachable, doing exactly what it was written to do. **Law 95 forbids listing two products nobody has worked out**, so the surface cannot name them; what [[D-158]] added is the one thing it CAN honestly say — that a choice exists — and the second attempt is where the other half is actually collected. A result is not a silent choice; the silence was the defect, and it is gone.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (no product change — this batch ships INSTRUMENTS, and they are the mechanism: `tools/pile-13-10.mjs`, a device check at the director's exact quantities, and a unit case asserting 13+10 and 14+13 build the same string. All three shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1401/1401**, +1 asserting 13+10 and 14+13 produce the same string. **DEVICE: targeted 19/19**, `--only=BRANCH`, active hours, no full sweep. **LIVE:** the quoted run above against `2ab45d9`. **FAIL-THEN-PASS at the product level:** removing the `validCount` arm from `namingQuestionFor` fails the 13+10 case specifically.*

**D-158 · 2026-08-15 — THE BRANCH WAS RIGHT; WHAT IT LEFT OUT WAS THAT IT WAS CHOOSING FOR HIM.**

**MEASURED FIRST, ONE CASE AT A TIME, ON THE DEPLOYED BUILD — and the branch does not collapse.** Driven through the real DOM on a clean context against production (`5af3f26`), each case takes its own branch and says its own sentence:

| pile | plans held | message | branch |
|---|---|---|---|
| 14 wood + 6 sharp stone | `[spear]` | *"You are trying to make a fire-hardened spear. Go ahead?"* | **NAMED** ✓ |
| 14 wood + 13 stone | `[storage, stonehammer]` | *"You know more than one way to use these. Which are you making?"* — options `Storage crate` \| `Stone hammer` | **LIST** ✓ |
| 14 wood + 6 sharp stone | `[]` | *"You have not worked these out yet. Put them together and see?"* | **GENERIC** ✓ |

**SO THE REPORTED SYMPTOM IS NOT A COLLAPSED BRANCH — IT IS THE GENERIC BRANCH BEING RIGHT AND INCOMPLETE.** The director staged 14 wood + 13 stone with neither plan known, which is genuinely case 3, and the generic line is the correct branch for it. What he actually caught is the half that line never said: **that pile makes TWO things.** `matchPool(['wood','stone'])` is `[storage, stonehammer]`, and `resolveRecipe` then picked one of them on his behalf with no sign the other had ever been possible. His words — *"options are multiple not one in sequence"* — describe a real choice being made behind a generic prompt, and he was right to distrust it.

**THE FIX IS A FACT ABOUT THE PILE, NOT A CATALOGUE OF PRODUCTS.** A pile with more than one possible outcome now says so: *"You have not worked these out yet, and there is more than one thing here. Put them together and see?"* Law 95 is untouched — neither product is named, and a test asserts the message never contains "storage", "crate" or "hammer". What changes is that the survivor knows a choice exists and that trying again may find something else, which is exactly what `resolveRecipe`'s undiscovered-first tie-break will actually give them. A pile with only one possible outcome does not claim otherwise.

**AND FOUR COPIES OF THE SAME NARROWING BECAME ONE.** The exact-arity pool — the three lines deciding which recipes a pile is *about* — had drifted into four places in this file. `matchPool` is now the single answer, and both `heldMatches` and `hasUnknownRival` read it. The staging surface has to ask that question twice for different reasons (which are HELD, and how many there are ALTOGETHER); the second is the one the generic branch was missing.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `matchPool` and the `validCount` arm of `namingQuestionFor` in `src/brain/experiment.ts`; `tools/branch-probe.mjs`; shipped in this batch).

**WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT PROVE.** Every message above is quoted from the DOM of the deployed build, not from a brain hook and not from a local preview — the gap that hid three earlier rounds. The new unit case fails against its own plant with the exact prior sentence. What it does not prove is that no OTHER pile takes a wrong branch: the claim is bounded to the piles named here, plus a mutual-exclusion test asserting no message can ever satisfy two branches at once.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1400/1400 across 75 files**, +6 asserting the MESSAGE per case rather than that a question happened. **DEVICE: targeted, per the active-hours rule** — `branch-probe` against production, quoted above; no full sweep. **LIVE: see the push SHA and the served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-157 · 2026-08-15 — THE AUTO-COMMIT WAS ALREADY FIXED; WHAT HE SAW WAS AN OLDER BUILD, A RAW ID, AND A HUB NAMING THINGS HE DID NOT OWN.**

**THE THIRD REPORT OF "CRAFTING STILL AUTO-COMMITS" DOES NOT REPRODUCE ON THE SHIPPED BUILD, AND THIS IS THE EVIDENCE.** The brief required ruling out deployment before touching code, and then required not trusting this round's own verification either. So the crafting path was driven **through the real DOM** — open the pack, pick two chips, press the button — on a **genuinely clean browser context**, against **the live site the director actually loads**, which reports its own stamp as `06990bf`:

- **14 wood + 13 stone**: circle appears, plans `[]` before and after, storage not built. `COMMITTED WITHOUT ASKING: false`.
- **wood + sharp stone**: identical. Nothing minted, nothing built, nothing spent.

**THE REPORT MATCHES THE PREVIOUS BUILD EXACTLY, INCLUDING ITS SECOND HALF.** Under `efa6512` (D-155), `needsNaming` was `heldMatches(...) >= 1` — so a pile with nothing held committed silently, and a pile with ONE held plan asked with exactly one option. That is both of his observations, in order, from one build: the first trial builds without asking, the second offers a single named outcome. On `06990bf` neither happens. **The most likely explanation is that he tested between the push and the deploy completing** — the served page did not become `06990bf` until 18:21 — and incognito does not help with that, because the gap is on the server, not in his cache.

**WHY EVERY PREVIOUS CHECK MISSED THE REAL DEFECT.** Every check ever written for this feature called `__drift.tryCombine` — the BRAIN, through a debug hook. Not one of them opened the pack, picked chips and pressed the button, so anything between the button and the brain was invisible to all of them. That gap is now closed: `tools/craft-ui-probe.mjs` drives the DOM on a clean context and can be pointed at the live URL, and the new device section does the same.

**AND DRIVING IT THAT WAY FOUND WHAT HE ACTUALLY SAW.** `blueprintNameFor` covered **six of eleven** recipes; every recipe added after it was written fell through to `default: return recipeId`. So the staging circle offered a position labelled **"spear"** — lowercase, an internal id showing through as a product name — and the same for `backpack`, `raft`, `fishingline` and `net`. His words were *"offered spear as the ONLY option"*, and the option really was labelled `spear`. Five names added; a test now walks `allRecipes()` so a switch nobody is obliged to extend cannot go stale again.

**THE HUB NAMED THINGS THAT DID NOT EXIST, AND IT WAS THE WHOLE CLASS.** `loadoutView` built its `zones` array unconditionally, so a fresh survivor was shown a **Backpack** row and a **Storage** row alongside Belt and Pockets. Audited per the brief: of the six, only two are POSSESSIONS — the others are body positions a survivor always has, which is why belt and pockets are correct to show. The two are fixed differently on purpose. **The carry row always exists**, because the materials have to be somewhere visible — hiding it would hide the whole inventory — so what changes is its NAME: without a pack it reads *"In your arms"*, the backpack recipe's own words and the same correction [[D-154]] made to the carry icon. **The storage row simply goes**, because there is no box and an empty row for a crate nobody built is a claim with nothing behind it.

**A TEST HAD LOCKED THE PHANTOM IN.** `loadout.test.ts` carried one named *"storage reads empty before a crate is built, never as a phantom container"* whose assertion was the opposite of its name — it required the empty row to be present. An empty row IS the phantom. Rewritten, along with a fixture called `fullyEquipped` that owned neither a pack nor a crate.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: five names in `blueprintNameFor`; the `arms` zone and the conditional storage row in `src/brain/loadout.ts` with its label in `src/body/hud.ts`; `tools/craft-ui-probe.mjs`; shipped in this batch).

**WHAT RULES OUT THIS BEING ANOTHER FALSE PROOF.** Three things, and they are different in kind from what was done before. The crafting claim is measured **against the live production URL**, not a local build — so a stale `dist` cannot flatter it, and indeed the local preview was stamped `efa6512` while the live site was `06990bf`. It is driven **through the real DOM** rather than a debug hook, which is the specific gap that hid this for three rounds. And both fixes **fail against their own plants with the director's exact strings** — `labels [spear]`, and `rows [… Backpack | Storage]` for a survivor owning neither. What this does NOT rule out is a defect on a path nobody has thought to drive; the honest limit of the claim is that the two piles he named, on the build he is served, do not commit without asking.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1394/1394 across 74 files.** **DEVICE: targeted verification only, per the active-hours schedule rule** — the new section 34/34, fail-then-pass both legs, plus the standalone UI probe against production. No full sweep. **LIVE: see the push SHA and the served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-156 · 2026-08-15 — THREE ITEMS, THREE VERDICTS: STAGING ALWAYS ASKS, THE FIRST CRATE PAYS, THE SHELL STAYS.**

**THE PROCESS CHANGED, AND THIS ENTRY IS THE FIRST UNDER IT.** The director now approves a list of two to four items before a session starts, tests each personally in minutes afterwards, and gives verdict before the next list is made. Anything else noticed along the way is NAMED, not fixed. What is recorded here is exactly the three items and nothing beside them.

**1 · NEVER AUTO-COMMIT — THE GATE ONLY EVER FIRED FOR PLANS ALREADY HELD.** `needsNaming` read `heldMatches(...).length >= 1`, so a first-time pattern went straight to a finished product. Measured on the director's own case before touching anything: **stone + wood on a fresh life returned `invented — "You work out how it fits: Stone hammer."` with no ask at all.** [[D-151]]'s P0-C was real and covered only half the game — the half where the survivor already knew the answer. "Never build silently" has to mean never, so the gate is now `recipesMatching(materials).length >= 1`: staging anything that MAKES something asks first.

**WHAT THE QUESTION MAY SAY IS WHERE LAW 95 LIVES, and it is the only thing that varies.** Two or more held plans → the list names every one of them and the question points at it. One held plan → the attempt is named outright, in the director's own words. **Zero held plans → the ATTEMPT is named and the OUTCOME is not** — *"You have not worked these out yet. Put them together and see?"* — which promises nothing, hides nothing, and hands over no catalogue. Naming the attempt is not naming the product, and that distinction is what lets one surface serve all three cases instead of two mechanisms pretending to be one.

**THE BLAST RADIUS WAS SIXTEEN TESTS ACROSS EIGHT FILES**, every one of them driving `tryCombineWith` and expecting a commitment. They now do what a player does — stage, read the question, answer it — through one shared helper (`tests/helpers/confirmed.ts`) rather than the round-trip being retyped eight times. Three of them asserted the OLD law outright ("an unknown pattern still resolves"); those are rewritten with a note saying what they used to claim.

**2 · THE BACKPACK — (a) VERIFIED, (c) BUILT.** A genuinely fresh survivor has none: `tools.backpack false`, and on screen the carry affordance draws an ARMFUL, not a pack — read off computed style rather than presence, per the standard [[D-154]] set. The new half is (c): **the first crate a survivor opens always holds one.** `trace.cratesOpened` is the fact that makes "first" mean a life rather than a crate; every later crate is an ordinary crate. Additive, so a save written before this field merges in at zero and its owner's next crate is still their first — which is both the kind answer and the correct one. Guarded on not already owning one, because `tools.backpack` is a boolean and a second pack is not a thing.

**3 · THE COCONUT SHELL — IT WAS CONSUMED WHOLE.** `eat` decremented the stack and returned nothing, so the husk a survivor is plainly still holding stopped existing at the moment they drank from it. `vessel.ts` has described a *"coconut-shell cup"* since the water slice, which is the game already saying the shell is a thing; now it is one. `shell` is a new `MaterialKind` — the compiler named all eight tables that had to answer for it, which is exactly why those tables are exhaustive `Record<MaterialKind, …>` types. It is structurally inert on purpose: a by-product, not a new economy, and what it is FOR is a later question than this one. The grant is written at the one call site rather than in a by-product table, because a table with one row is a system pretending to be general.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `needsNaming`/`namingQuestionFor` in `src/brain/experiment.ts` and the option list in `src/body/game.ts`; `trace.cratesOpened` and the crate grant in `src/brain/state.ts`; the `shell` material across types, tune, materials, evidence, matter and the HUD label; shipped in this batch).

**NAMED, NOT FIXED — candidates for a future list.** **(i) A crate is an AXE node** (`needsAxe: true`), so the guaranteed pack is gated behind owning an axe, which is gated behind a knapped blade and a stone hammer. As a *reliable early-game* second path that is worth the director's eye: it may be later than intended. **(ii) SLICE 2C's tab-leak check** remains undiagnosed at seven passes to two failures, carried from [[D-155]] with its new diagnostic in place and not yet triggered. Neither is touched here.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1393/1393 across 74 files**, +14 locking the three items. **DEVICE: fail-then-pass, both legs run** — planted, the section reproduces all three reported behaviours including the director's own string (`invented — "You work out how it fits: Stone hammer."`, `foundBackpack false`, `shell 0->0`); restored, 18/18. **TARGETED VERIFICATION, NOT A FULL SWEEP, AND SAID PLAINLY.** The three items were witnessed by their own section at 18/18, and item 1's blast radius reached two further device sections — `D-063` and `THE MAKER DOOR`, whose helper drove the pre-ruling player path — which return **37/37** once that helper answers the staging question as a player now must. A full sweep was attempted three times and did not complete: the first reached 281 checks before the SYSTEM KILLED IT, and two further attempts were killed within a minute, with the polling shells themselves taking SIGKILL. Memory was healthy on the last attempt (1.7 GB free, commit 11.5 of 30.6 GB), so this is not [[D-152]]'s pressure and is not diagnosed. It is named for the night batch rather than chased in an active hour, per this session's own schedule rule. **LIVE: see the push SHA and the served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-155 · 2026-08-15 — THE GAME LIED, BUT NOT WHERE IT WAS ACCUSED: A SUCCESS THAT DID NOT COST WHAT IT SAID.**

**THE REPORTED LINE IS HONEST, AND THAT IS THE FIRST FINDING.** The director's report was that a failed axe attempt prints *"the blade lost its edge"* while no material actually changed. Measured through the real verb rather than read off the source: `transformOnFailure` genuinely fires, `matterWear.sharpblade` genuinely moves 0 → 1 → 2, the wear is genuinely persisted (`save.ts` carries it through both the migrate and the merge), and the blade genuinely **breaks on the third failure**, taking a real unit out of the inventory. The text and the mutation are structurally coupled — the sentence cannot be printed without the transform having returned — so the claim is true.

**WHAT IS WRONG THERE IS LEGIBILITY, NOT TRUTH.** `matterWearPerUnit` is 3, so the first failure moves a counter with **no rendering anywhere** — `matterWear` appears nowhere in `src/body/` — and the inventory is unchanged. The survivor is told the blade lost its edge and can verify nothing. A true sentence that is unfalsifiable at the moment it is said is, from the chair, indistinguishable from a lie. `isNearlySpent` does reach the Vitals panel as a Wear line, but only at two-thirds gone, which is the SECOND failure. Named here rather than retuned: moving `matterWearPerUnit` or surfacing wear per-material is a design call, not a defect fix.

**AND THE MEASUREMENT FOUND THE REAL LIE STANDING NEXT TO IT.** A successful invention read `state.inventory[a] -= 1; state.inventory[b] -= 1;` — the FIRST TWO staged materials — while the comment above it said *"the materials"* and `canExperimentWith` accepts **two to four**. The axe is wood + blade + **binding**. Across six successful inventions the probe recorded wood 40 → 34 and blade 40 → 34 with **the fibre sitting at 40 the entire time**. The survivor stages three things, is told they made something, and one of the three is quietly still in the bag. **That is the game lying about a state change**, and it is not the one that was reported.

**THE SHAPE IS THIS PROJECT'S OLDEST.** The pair-only assumption from [[D-063]] survived the widening to four inputs because the widening happened in the GATE and nowhere else — a law enforced in one layer and enforced nowhere. A loop over the staged set is safe at any arity: the gate already rejects duplicates and already requires every material to be in hand, so no stack can be driven negative.

**THE SWEEP, AND WHY IT IS SHAPED THE WAY IT IS.** "Check every narrative line" is not mechanisable — most lines are instructions or refusals, and those claim nothing. What IS mechanisable is the class the defect belongs to: *a message announcing a change, against the change actually made*. So every manufacture verb is now driven for real and its inventory movement is compared against **its own recipe's declared slot amounts**, read from `recipes.ts` rather than typed into the test, so a retune moves the test with the game. **All seven pass** — torch, axe, spear, backpack, stone hammer, shelter, storage — plus knapping's single slot and the raft's three including the float. **The prototype path was the only liar**, and it is fixed.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: the consumption loop in `src/brain/experiment.ts`; a `makeChosen` debug hook mirroring the shipped `tryCombine`; `tests/item4-honesty.test.ts` and a device section; shipped in this batch).

**NOT RETUNED, DELIBERATELY.** The wear-legibility finding above is left as a report. The director asked whether the transform fires or the text must go; it fires, so neither remedy applies, and the third option — making wear visible before it costs a unit — changes the felt difficulty of every failed attempt in the game. That belongs to whoever owns the fair-challenge grammar, not to a defect pass.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1379/1379 across 73 files**, including 14 new checks that drive every craft verb against its own declared costs. **DEVICE: fail-then-pass, both legs run** — planted, the section reports `fibre 30 -> 30` and goes red; restored, `wood 30->29, blade 30->29, fibre 30->29`. The honesty check stays green against the plant, correctly: that half was never broken, and a guard that flipped with the defect would be measuring the wrong thing. **Confirming full sweep: 533/537**, with the ITEM 4 section green inside it — `wood 30->29, blade 30->29, fibre 30->29`.

**THE FOUR REDS ARE BOTH KNOWN CLUSTERS, AND NEITHER IS THIS BATCH'S.** Three are [[D-152]]'s radio clock drift, recognisable to the character: the broadcast window is hour 5.0 and the sweep reached it at **5.4** with `heard []`, and that section returns 21/21 standalone on the same build. The fourth is SLICE 2C's tab-leak check, which now stands at **seven passes to two failures** across recorded sweeps — the first failure was mine (a Build panel left open upstream, fixed in [[D-154]]) and this one is not explained. Nothing in this batch touches tabs, panels or the loadout, and a tab switch passes `reopening: true` so it cannot itself trigger the spoken refusal that moves `failedInteractionTaps`; the shape to look for is a tap that MISSES the tab and lands on the carry button beneath it, which would call `openLoadout` with `reopening: false` against an open panel. **It is not diagnosed, and it is not being labelled flaky.** What it now has is the thing it lacked both times: the check records which tap landed and what the game actually SAID, so the next occurrence names its own cause instead of printing a bare count. That diagnostic was added after this sweep began, so this run's detail line is still the old bare one.

**LIVE: see the push SHA and the served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-154 · 2026-08-14 — THE BENCH AUDIT, AND THE ONE PREDICATE BEHIND THREE REPORTS.**

**ITEM 0(a) — THE FRESH SAVE IS REAL, AND THAT IS THE UNCOMFORTABLE ANSWER.** A genuinely clean browser context — a profile that has never seen the origin, which is what incognito is — was booted and diffed field by field against the harness's own `startFresh()`. Across tools, torch, inventory, blueprints, fire, shelter, storage, radio, raft, capacities, skills, knowledge domains, nodes, illness, injuries, loadout and trace: **identical**. Only `gameHoursElapsed` and `trace.activeMs` differ, and both are artefacts of the two boot paths sleeping for different lengths. So the four failures in the director's hands are **not** explained by a stale fixture at boot. The bench started from the right state and missed them anyway, which moves the blame to what it ASSERTS.

**ITEM 0(b) — THE BENCH RENDERS, AND HAS NEVER ONCE WITNESSED A RENDER.** Both halves need saying. It can see: a real GPU (`ANGLE (Intel UHD Graphics, D3D11)`, not SwiftShader), and the presented frame carries **57 distinct colours across 40,755 sampled pixels, none of them black**. But of **544 checks, exactly ONE compares pixels — and it is a DOM button**. Zero assert on rendered 3D pixels. All twenty-one uses of `meshInfo`/`screenOfMesh` rest on `enabled` or a projected coordinate, and the gap was proven on live meshes: `caveBluff enabled=true onScreen=false`, `n_wr3_housing enabled=true onScreen=false`. **`enabled` means "in the scene graph and not disabled". It does not mean a player can see it.** [[D-150]]'s spear fix — swapping `TOOL_IDS` for `meshInfo().enabled` — was an improvement and is still not a visibility witness.

**AND A PROBE OF MINE THAT LIED FIRST.** My initial render check read the canvas with `createImageBitmap` and reported one colour and zero non-black pixels — "nothing is rendering". That would have been catastrophic and it was **wrong**: WebGL canvases default to `preserveDrawingBuffer: false`, so the buffer is empty by readback, and the suite's own 200-300 KB screenshots disprove it outright. Corrected to the compositor path before reporting.

**ITEM 0(c) — 39 OF 544 CHECKS ASSERT WORDS WITH NO STATE IN THE CONDITION**, shipped as `tools/bench-audit-text.mjs` so the set regenerates. The number is an **upper bound and is labelled as one in the tool's own output**: it is regex over JavaScript, and reading the flagged rows found real false positives — `tools.axe === migrated.tools.axe`, `JSON.stringify(inventory) === …`, `activeHand === 'axe'`, `tapTargetAt === 'raft'` all assert state while looking textual. Two classifier corrections took it 60 → 49 → 39. A further portion are legitimately ABOUT wording — Law 145's sensations, Law 95's refusal to name a product — where text is correctly the whole subject.

**THE FINDING NOBODY ASKED FOR, AND THE ONE WITH THE WIDEST BLAST RADIUS: `editSave` WAS WINNING A RACE, NOT DOING A JOB.** The harness parks on a "blank" page to edit the save with the game not running. That path had no file behind it, so **the dev server answered it with a 200 and the entire index.html** — SPA fallback — and the blank page **booted a second copy of the game**, which loaded the pre-edit save and wrote it back over the mutation on the way out. Measured in a standalone reproduction of the exact sequence: **the edit was destroyed 10 times out of 10.** With a real file at `public/__smoke_blank.html` it survives **10 out of 10**, and the probe confirms no game boots there. A fixture that silently fails to apply is the worst failure a device suite can have — every check downstream tests the wrong state and can pass vacuously — so `editSave` now **verifies its own edit** and any that did not take are named at the summary. That guard needed one correction of its own: it first compared the whole live state and flagged three fixtures that had applied perfectly, because energy, warmth and the clock are supposed to drift; it now compares only the scenario fields a fixture exists to set.

**HOW FAR THIS REACHES, SAID PLAINLY RATHER THAN FOLDED AWAY.** `editSave` is the fixture mechanism for a large share of this suite — every scenario that stocks an inventory, sets a vital, plants an illness or stands a structure goes through it. On the evidence it did not always lose (fixtures visibly applied all through these runs, and my standalone reproduction losing 10/10 while the suite plainly got edits through means the outcome turned on timing I have not fully characterised). What that leaves is a bench where **an unknown subset of fixture-dependent checks may, on any given run, have been asserting against unmutated state and passing on it** — which is indistinguishable from passing honestly, and is the precise mechanism by which a "device-proven" claim reaches the director's hands and fails. It is a live candidate for some portion of this project's unexplained intermittency: [[D-152]]'s three "flaky" panel checks, [[D-153]]'s quarry cluster, and the radio's clock drift were each root-caused to something else, but every result predating this fix that rested on a fixture is now weaker evidence than it looked. Nothing is being retro-invalidated on suspicion; what IS true is that the suite could not previously tell the difference, and now it can.

**ITEM 1 — ONE PREDICATE, THREE SYMPTOMS, EXACTLY AS THE DIRECTOR HYPOTHESISED.** `revealedInPanel` read `SURVIVAL_BASIC.has(id) && suspicionFor(id).suspected`, and `suspected` is `needFelt && every making present` where `hasHandled` is literally `inventory[m] > 0`. The torch's need is `isNight || cold` and the game opens at hour 18, so **one stick and one strand, four seconds off the beach, with `blueprints: []` and `torch.owned: false`, inserted a manufacture-ready Torch row** — Law 216 breached precisely. The same boolean is the last line of `fireIsKnown`, which is why [[D-150]]'s fix changed nothing the director could feel: **I moved the question one layer down to a function that asked the same thing.** Measured before the fix: 1 wood + 1 fibre → Torch row present; 9 wood → `"Build fire" shown=true` with nothing ever invented.

**THE SCAFFOLD IS NOT LOST — IT MOVES TO WHERE A SCAFFOLD BELONGS.** Law 113 asks that survival basics not be gated behind blind experiment, and a scaffold is a PROMPT, not a product. `panelHints` already skips anything revealed and carries the route's own words for anything suspected, so the survivor holding wood and fibre is still told *"The dark is closing in, and you are holding something that burns."* They are pointed at the experiment; they are not handed the answer. Both laws hold, and the only change is that the ROW must be earned.

**THE THIRD SYMPTOM WAS A PICTURE, NOT A PREDICATE.** `tools.backpack` is false from arrival onward and no Backpack row is ever revealed — and a **backpack was drawn in the corner of the screen the whole time**, because `addCarriedButton` painted one unconditionally. The affordance now draws what the survivor actually has: an ARMFUL without a pack, in the recipe's own words — *"Carry properly instead of in your arms"* — and the pack the moment one is owned. The 2026-07-27 reasoning that made this a drawn object rather than a word still stands; it was simply drawing the wrong object.

**TEN CHECKS HAD LOCKED THE DEFECT IN AS CORRECT — SIX UNIT, FOUR ON DEVICE**, which is why the bench never caught it: `reveal-sweep`'s behavioural half, two Law 113 cases, the hint-exclusivity case, `fire-law130`'s scaffold case, and one of my own from D-150. Each is rewritten to the post-216 law rather than deleted, and each carries a note saying what it used to assert — a test that encoded a defect is worth remembering, not erasing. The hint-exclusivity case keeps its exact claim (teach once, then stop) and only changes its FIXTURE, reaching "revealed" the way a survivor actually does. The device half surfaced only in the full sweep: three SLICE 2B checks asserting that cold-plus-makings hands over the fire route, and my own D-150 P0-A check whose fixture reached "can build" through materials alone. Each is rewritten the same way, and the SLICE 2B craft-it-end-to-end case keeps its exact intent while reaching the row the way a survivor really does.

**AND TWO DEFECTS OF MINE IN THE HARNESS, BOTH CAUGHT BY THE SUITE INSIDE ONE RUN.** My new SLICE 2B block opened the Build panel and never closed it, and the very next section caught it: `openLoadout` refuses OUT LOUD when a panel is already open (which is [[D-152]]'s own fix), a spoken refusal is a counted failed tap, and SLICE 2C's no-leak check went 9 -> 10. Downstream of that the quarry could not be walked to at all — 94 m from a 2.5 m reach — because an open panel blocks movement, so one missing line printed as four failures. **That is precisely the leftover-modal class I root-caused two sessions earlier and then committed myself**, which is its own small lesson about who these rules are for. Separately, my ITEM 1 check scraped row HEADINGS and passed in isolation while reading `rows [ | | ]` in the full sweep; it now asserts the row's BUTTON, which is what "offered" actually means and what SLICE 2B already did.

**ONE CHECK IS NOW DIAGNOSABLE THAT WAS NOT.** The fail-loud tree tap went `0 -> 0` and could not say why: unlike the quarry and storage blocks it never asserted that the survivor REACHED the tree, and out of reach a tap resolves to empty ground — which since [[D-150]] deliberately does not count a failed interaction. So a slow bench and a genuinely silent tap produced the same red. It asserts its own precondition now, the same shape [[D-152]] gave the settings cluster.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `revealedInPanel` in `src/brain/reveal.ts`, `fireIsKnown` in `src/brain/state.ts`, the carry affordance in `src/body/hud.ts` and `index.html`, `public/__smoke_blank.html`, and the fixture guard plus `bench-audit*.mjs` in tools; shipped in this batch).

**WHAT IS NOT BUILT, NAMED RATHER THAN IMPLIED.** Items 2 through 9 — the P0-C staging surface, the carry/hand render path and the narrowed reachability invariant, **the game lying about a failed axe attempt**, construction targeting, the tap-feedback revert, the radio's first contact, illness drawing health, and the two confirm-first questions — are **untouched**. Item 0 ran first because the brief made it sequential, and what it found reshaped item 1 and cost real time to establish honestly. Item 4 in particular outranks most of that list and should lead the next batch: a game that misreports its own outcomes is worse than one missing a feature.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: 1365/1365 across 72 files**, six of them rewritten from the superseded law. **DEVICE: before-and-after on a genuinely fresh incognito life**, plus a new 6-check ITEM 1 section at 15/15 asserting only VISIBLE surfaces — rows on screen, computed-style buttons, displayed icons — per item 0's own standard. **Confirming full sweep: 529/534**, with all three of this batch's late corrections verified in it — the fail-loud tree tap green at `0 -> 1` with its new reach assertion reading 1.21 m, both P0-A checks green on the corrected fixture, and all six ITEM 1 checks green including the learned case now asserting the row's BUTTON.

**THE FIVE REDS ARE ONE CLUSTER, AND THEY ARE NOT THIS BATCH'S.** All five are the D-050 block failing behind `panel death visible`: the survivor died mid-section, the death overlay covered the debug button and the Look button, and the tap log reset to zero. The evidence that this is the bench and not the change is direct — **the previous full sweep passed those same five checks on byte-identical product code**, differing only in three harness-side corrections that touch neither warmth nor that section. This run logged **45 stalls over 60 s, the worst 2219 s and 1739 s**; at 150 real seconds per game hour a half-hour stall is roughly twelve game hours of vitals draining with nobody playing, which is [[D-152]]'s documented mechanism and is enough to kill a survivor standing still. Recorded as shipped-with rather than resolved: the cluster is explained, not eliminated, and it will keep recurring until the bench itself is healthy.

**LIVE: see the push SHA and the served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-153 · 2026-08-13 — THE QUARRY CLUSTER: A PROJECTION THAT INVENTED AN ANSWER, AND A CHECK THAT NEVER LOOKED AT WHAT IT WAS TAPPING.**

**THE LAST "FLAKY" LABEL IN THE FILE, AND IT WAS WRONG TOO.** Three checks — the quarry is repeat-minable, the quarry depletes once its pool is spent, and D-070's spent seam staying spent — were carried as `measuredIntermittent` at a recorded 8-in-10, with the hypothesis *"screen-projection fragility ... suspect camera state at projection time, not the projection maths"*. Half right. **It IS camera state, and the maths had no guard for it.**

**THE ROOT CAUSE, IN ONE LINE OF BABYLON.** `runtime.projectToScreen` called `Vector3.Project` with no check that the target is in front of the camera. That function does the perspective divide unconditionally: for a point BEHIND the camera the w term goes negative, the divide flips sign, and it returns a confident-looking coordinate that is pure nonsense. The recorded failures read `pt=3265,3433`, `pt=2002,1480` and `pt=-1279,1617` **on a 915×412 viewport, with `dist: 2` — the survivor standing two metres from the node.** Nothing was wrong with the arithmetic. **It was asked a question with no answer and made one up**, which is this project's oldest recurring shape, and `screenOfMesh` had the identical hole beside it.

**THE HARNESS ALREADY KNEW THE SYMPTOM AND HAD PATCHED THE WRONG END.** `tapWorld`'s own comment says *"a touch dispatched off-screen produces NO pointer event, so the gesture silently does not happen while the helper reports success"* — and the bound-check that comment introduced was applied at that ONE caller and never at the source. So every other caller stayed exposed, and this one paid for it for sessions. Both projections now return **null** for anything behind the camera: a helper that says "I cannot aim at that from here" instead of aiming at a pixel that does not exist.

**AND THE SECOND HALF, WHICH IS WHY IT LOOKED RANDOM.** The quarry block approached the node and then tapped it, and **never faced it** — the only aimed check in the file that does not call `faceNode`. It inherited whatever heading the approach happened to end on. Face the node and the projection is on screen; end facing away and the node is behind the camera. **Whether a run passed depended on which way the survivor happened to be looking**, which is not a ratio to be recorded, it is a missing line. The block now faces the node before the first tap and again between taps, because a check that establishes its aim only for the first of three is two thirds luck.

**ONE CAUSE, THREE FACES — the same shape as the settings cluster the session before.** When the taps landed nowhere the pool was never spent, so the two depletion checks reported `available=true pool=4` as a *consequence* and were never independently broken. All three are **promoted back to plain `check()`s**: they have no ratio because they have a cause. Both `*_RECORD` objects stay in place as history, because what they recorded is worth remembering — two checks carried a failure rate for sessions while the thing failing was a third check upstream of both, and the detail line said so on every single run.

**FAIL-THEN-PASS, MADE DETERMINISTIC RATHER THAN LEFT TO LUCK.** Deleting the `faceNode` call alone would restore the old behaviour and leave the outcome to chance, and a plant whose failure depends on luck proves nothing on the run where it gets lucky. So the plant faces the survivor deliberately AWAY and removes the guard, and reproduces the reported signature exactly: **`stone now 0`, `pt=-669,-10697 onCanvas=false`, `pending=none`, `hold[idle(stone 0) …]`, `pool: 220`.** Restored, the same section reads `stone now 12`, `available=false`, `pool=0`.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `isInFrontOfCamera` guarding both projection hooks in `src/body/game.ts`; `faceNode` before and between the quarry taps, and three checks promoted, in `tools/smoke.mjs`; shipped in this batch).

**WHAT THIS IS NOT.** Both projection hooks are debug-surface instruments — `__drift.screenOf` and `__drift.screenOfMesh` — so this is a **test-instrument defect, not a player-facing one**. It is still squarely a reliability finding: it manufactured reds that looked exactly like game defects and consumed diagnosis across several sessions. The distinction is stated because [[D-152]] recorded me getting an attribution like this wrong one session earlier, and the correction is only worth anything if it changes what I claim next time.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: ran** — 1365/1365 across 72 files, untouched by this batch, which is expected: the defect is in the body's debug surface and in the harness, not in the brain. **DEVICE: fail-then-pass, both legs run** — the planted face-away reproduces the exact recorded signature, and the restored build mines 12 stone and empties the seam. **Confirming full sweep: 526/526, the first wholly clean sweep in this stretch** — and it ran on the worst bench yet recorded, with [[D-152]]'s new verdict reporting **31 stalls over 90 s, the worst 2351 s**. A suite that passes completely while the machine pages for thirty-nine minutes is the actual measure of what removing the luck was worth. **LIVE: see the push SHA and the served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-152 · 2026-08-13 — THE BENCH DOES NOT LEAK; IT STALLS. AND THE THREE "FLAKY" CHECKS WERE NEVER FLAKY.**

**ITEM 1 — WHAT IS DEGRADING, MEASURED RATHER THAN DESCRIBED AGAIN.** The previous session logged three wildly different sweep speeds, a spurious felling red, a clock-drift red and a four-minute navigation timeout, and every claim about WHY rested on watching a log file grow. **The bench had no instrument at all**, so the first work was building one: every section timed, and the page measured at each boundary for heap, DOM nodes, event listeners and documents. Those four separate the hypotheses that actually differ — a harness leak shows as documents and listeners climbing and never returning, a game leak shows as heap and nodes climbing while documents stay flat, and machine contention shows as time varying with **no memory signal at all**.

**THERE IS NO LEAK, IN EITHER LAYER.** Across a 2 h 8 m sweep of 47 sections: heap **52.0 → 54.9 MB**, DOM nodes **2105 → 1123 (down)**, listeners **64 → 62 (down)**, node RSS **72.0 → 47.8 MB (down)**, documents 4 → 8. Quarter by quarter the page grows mildly and bounded — heap 32.8 → 39.8 MB, nodes 2571 → 3845 — and nothing runs away. **And the bench got FASTER as it ran**: median gap between measurement points fell **60.5 s → 18.4 s → 24.2 s → 16.1 s** across the quarters. Progressive degradation predicts the exact opposite, so both leak hypotheses are dead on their own evidence rather than on an argument.

**WHAT ACTUALLY HAPPENS IS STALLS, AND THE MACHINE IS THE CAUSE.** Twelve gaps over ninety seconds in one run; the worst **1781 s — 29.7 minutes** — and an 1083 s second. Sampling the machine every twenty seconds through the same run: physical memory **7.9 GB total**, free oscillating **277–2122 MB**, and commit charge sitting at **94–95% of its limit continuously**. During the stalls specifically: commit **28,777 MB** against a limit that started at **28,672 MB**, free memory down to **580–812 MB**. Windows grew the pagefile mid-run — **commit limit 28,672 → 32,636 MB, pagefile 20,611 → 23,709 MB** — and a pagefile grow is a synchronous disk operation that every allocating process waits on. **Chrome navigating is exactly such a process, which is what a four-minute navigation timeout IS.**

**ATTRIBUTION, PRECISELY, BECAUSE "EXTERNAL" IS A CLAIM AND NOT AN EXCUSE.** Total commit **24,759 MB across 790 processes**. The sweep's own share — Chrome 2147 MB across 22 processes plus node 1081 MB across 12 — is **3228 MB, about 13%**. The rest is other software on a developer's desktop, including 5741 MB of Claude processes across 16. So the bench is not filling this machine; **the machine is already full, and the sweep is the allocation that tips it over.** One cause, three symptoms that each looked like a game defect: the felling budget that ran out, the radio's scheduled hour drifting from 5.0 to 5.4, and the navigation that never returned.

**THE HARNESS CANNOT FIX THE MACHINE, SO IT NOW DECLARES ITS OWN CONDITIONS.** A run that stalls prints **"BENCH STALLED DURING THIS RUN — TIMING-SENSITIVE REDS ARE SUSPECT"** with the count and the worst offenders, beside the number everyone reads. `STALL_MS` is 90 s: far past the slowest deliberate wait in the file (a 30 s `approach` budget) and far short of the multi-minute stalls that actually occur. This is what stops the next paging stall from being diagnosed as a game defect for a third time.

**ITEM 2 — "FLAKY" WAS THE WRONG WORD, AND IT HID A DETERMINISTIC DEFECT FOR SESSIONS.** The Look button, the Copy-debug-info button and the copy confirmation were carried as `measuredIntermittent` at a recorded 8-pass-in-10. They are not intermittent. **They fail together, always, and only when a previous section leaves a panel open** — the detail line said so on every failure and nobody read it: `panels=[panel backpack loadout visible]`. The storage interaction opens the loadout hub, the hub is modal, and the section that follows tested the Look button through it. `not-found` was never a timing signature; **it was an occlusion signature**, exactly as the director suspected.

**PROVEN BY SEPARATING THE PRECONDITION FROM THE SUBJECT.** Run alone with a clean start, all three pass — twice. Run in the full sweep, the stray panel IS present (`stray on arrival [panel backpack loadout visible]`), and with the panel cleared first all three pass there too. Two consecutive earlier sweeps, with the stray panel present and no cleanup, failed all three. The harness now clears any stray panel and **asserts the precondition as its own check** before testing the button, and the three are **promoted back to real `check()`s** — they have no timing component left to be intermittent about. `PANEL_CLUSTER_RECORD` stays in place as the record of what was believed and for how long, because that is the part worth remembering.

**AND A CORRECTION I OWE, FOUND BY MY OWN CHECK REFUSING TO CONFIRM ME.** I fixed `openSettings`'s `if (runtime.panelOpen) return` — a silent refusal — and called it a player-facing bug: *tap Look with the inventory open and nothing happens*. **That claim was wrong.** A real, occlusion-respecting tap reports `refused: occluded` — the modal covers the button, so no hand can reach the guard in the ordinary case. My first two probes asserted the refusal and "passed" it while proving only that nothing had been clicked, which is vacuity in the opposite direction. **The branch is reachable, but elsewhere**: `guardPanelLock`'s own header names the freeze class where `panelOpen === true` with **nothing visible on screen**, and it names Settings explicitly. In that window — up to a full second before the backstop recovers — the button is not covered, the tap lands, and it returned in silence. The fix is right; the reason I gave for it was not.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `sampleBench`/`markSectionStart` and the stall verdict in `tools/smoke.mjs`, the panel precondition and three promoted checks, and `explain` on the two silent panel guards in `src/body/game.ts`; shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. **UNIT: ran** — 1365/1365 across 72 files, unchanged by this batch, which is the point: neither item touches the brain. **DEVICE: 525/526 on the confirming full sweep**, all three formerly-"flaky" checks green with the stray panel observed and cleared in the same run; the single red is `net::ERR_INTERNET_DISCONNECTED`, a dropped connection and not a product failure. The quarry cluster remains genuinely variable across runs (FLAKY-PASS then FLAKY-FAIL) — real timing shape, unlike the settings cluster, and left labelled honestly rather than swept in with it. **LIVE: see the push SHA and the served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-151 · 2026-08-13 — THE LAST FOUR P0 ITEMS: the attempt is named, the fever is felt in the feet, practice reaches the act, and the receiver can be found.**

**P0-C — AND THE RULING, TAKEN LITERALLY, SOFT-LOCKS THE INVENTION TREE. That is the finding, and the shipped reachability test is what proved it rather than an argument.** [[D-147]]'s P0-1 stopped the game arbitrating between TWO known answers and drew its line at two, because one match was "nothing to ask about". The director's ruling is that one is not silence's excuse either: staged materials matching a plan the survivor HOLDS must name the attempt and wait. So the gate drops to one — `needsNaming`, beside `knownMatches` rather than replacing it, because *"which of these two?"* and *"are you making this?"* are different questions in different words. **But "name the held plan and wait" also means a pile you know one answer for can only ever BE that answer** — hold the storage plan and wood+stone is storage forever, with the stone hammer behind it unreachable. `tests/combine-reach.test.ts` went red on `fishingline` the first time this ran: a recipe walled off behind a plan the survivor already had. P0-1 never hit this because at two-known it fell through to `resolveRecipe`, whose second tie-break deliberately prefers what you have NOT made. Dropping the threshold removed the only door invention had. **So the question gets a second answer — the named plan, or "try something else"**, which runs the discovery path exactly as before. It leaks nothing and cannot: the option is a REFUSAL, not a product. It says nothing about what else these make or whether anything does, and it is offered only when a rival genuinely exists, so a fully-known pile asks a plain yes-or-no rather than dangling a door with nothing behind it.

**Law 217 is locked rather than assumed.** `applyLearningEvent` already fired before the success/failure branch, so both outcomes return information to the technique — it was correct, and it was believed rather than checked, so it now has a test that fails if a failed attempt ever stops teaching.

**P0-D — "THE LINE SHOWS; THE BODY IGNORES IT", AND THE LINE WAS RIGHT.** Illness was wired — into `impairmentOf`, which is an ENERGY term, so a fevered survivor paid more per hour out of a ledger nobody can see and **walked at exactly the pace of a well one**. The whole felt cost was a sentence. `speedScale` in the body is a product of multipliers that each return 1 when their condition is absent — load, water, dive — and illness was simply not on that line; it is now, by the same rule, scaling `walkSpeedMps` at use and never touching the constant. **Both warning rungs stay genuinely free**: `illnessCosts` is false through `unsettled` and `ailing`, so the survivor is told twice, in sensations, before anything is taken, and the cost begins at `feverish` — the rung whose own readout says *"It is costing you now."* Before this, that sentence was not true. Floored at `illnessSlowestMultiplier`, so a fever slows a run and never quietly ends one.

**P0-E — GROWTH REACHED THE SIMULATION AND NEVER REACHED THE PLAYER.** Capacities were not inert; they were spent on the wrong currency. `swimEfficiencyOf` bought off part of the swim's ENERGY, `demandRelativeToCapacity` scaled walking DEMAND, and **carrying read no capacity at all** — `loadTolerance` was developed by hauling, displayed on the panel as "Carrying", and consulted by nothing. Every one of those is a slower drain on a bar, which is precisely the *"hide the number and change nothing observable"* build v2.8 rewrote Law 201 into **Law 234** to fail. So each act now pays out in pace or in ease: carrying expresses practice as EFFECTIVE WEIGHT, so both shipped load curves pick it up from their own existing shapes rather than through a fourth factor nobody could trace; walking and swimming join the multiplier line. `airCapacityOf` was the exception all along and is deliberately UNCHANGED — more air is felt the moment you are under — and it has a test now so that if dive ever goes flat, the coverage claim fails out loud instead of quietly.

**A ZERO-CALLER FOUND IN PASSING, NAMED RATHER THAN WIRED.** `staminaCeilingFor` raises a reserve ceiling from `endurance`, is unit-tested, and **has no production caller anywhere** — because the stamina reserve it shapes was never built. Wiring it would be a new system, which this batch is not for. It is named in `body.ts` so the next reader finds it in one grep instead of rediscovering it.

**P0-H — THE RECEIVER WAS NEVER GATED WRONG. IT WAS UNFINDABLE.** `salvageReceiver` has worked since [[D-124]] and still does; `wr3` is "the instrument housing, off the bow" in `radio.ts`'s header, in the TUNE comment, and **nowhere a player can see**. All six wreck parts drew as the same plate and rib, differing by a rotation angle — so the one rung of ENDING E03 standing in the world was a one-in-six blind guess, 296 m offshore, past a hull that wounds you. Nobody found it because there was nothing to find it BY.
