Law 26's standard is that the world tells you: the housing is a shape in the water now, with a glass face — the only bright value anywhere on the wreck — and it names itself in the evidence grammar the journal and the boat already use when it is worked. No new verb, no marker, no menu.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `needsNaming`/`heldMatches`/`EXPERIMENT_CHOICE` in `experiment.ts`, `illnessSpeedMultiplierOf`, `effectiveCarriedKg`/`walkEaseOf`/`swimSpeedEaseOf`, and the housing in `entities.ts`; shipped in this batch).

**HELD, EXPLICITLY NOT BUILT:** the overall running experience total. It collides with Laws 202/209 and the fork stays with the director. Wave 1 stays shut.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — typecheck, purity (56 brain files), tune-mirror, docs-integrity. A duplicate import that vitest tolerated and `tsc` did not was found and fixed by running BOTH; passing tests are not a typecheck. **UNIT: ran**, 1365 across 72 files, +21 new. **DEVICE: fail-then-pass, both legs run** — six defects planted, **nine checks red**, including the planted P0-C silently consuming wood 20→19 and charging exactly an attempt's 6 energy, and a fevered survivor covering 4.81 m against a well 4.75 m. The four checks that stayed green against the plant are GUARDS by construction — the free-rungs line, the no-strand floor, the depth fixture and the sibling contrast — and pass either way by design rather than by vacuity. **Two of my own checks were rejected before they could lie**: a swim comparison run entirely on dry sand (depth 0), and a guard against it written as `a && b ? c : d`, which evaluates to `d` on land and so was itself vacuous. **DEVICE, CONFIRMING FULL SWEEP: 523/524**, the new section inside it and all thirteen of its checks green. The single red — a tree that would not fell on the second try in a `fell → gather → fell` interleave — was **bisected rather than waved off, and rather than blamed on the nearest suspect**: re-running from an earlier section on the SAME build returns `fell1 true, gather true, fell2 true`. It is a 34-second budget in a sweep that ran at roughly half speed, which is the same family as [[D-150]]'s radio clock drift. Every change in this batch makes felling strictly EASIER — a lighter effective load and a lower energy multiplier — so there was no mechanism by which it could be the cause. A defensive guard added while chasing it is kept, and its comment says plainly that it is not known to have fixed anything, because a guard that quietly takes credit is how a real cause goes unlooked-for. **LIVE: see the push SHA and the served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-150 · 2026-08-12 — THE DEVICE VERDICT ON `ae0f62d`: the counter was not counting failures, the spear was never drawn, and the cave had no inside.**

**THE TELEMETRY'S HEADLINE NUMBER DOES NOT MEAN WHAT IT SAYS, AND THAT IS THE LARGEST FINDING.** `failedInteractionTaps: 25` was read as twenty-five failed taps. `markFailedTap()` is reachable from exactly ONE place — `explain()` — so the counter does not count taps at all; **it counts calls to `explain()`, and roughly fifteen call sites were using `explain()` to announce things that had just SUCCEEDED.** *"You break the surface and breathe." "The net is set. Leave it to soak." "You wrap it tight. The bleeding stops."* — [[D-148]]'s own bandage verb reported its success through the failure counter, with the DENIED cue playing over it. An unknown share of those 25 were the game working. `say()` now carries success on the same channel with no count and no denied cue; six verified sites are converted, `explain()` still counts so no genuine refusal silently stops being counted, and **the remaining sites are owed and named rather than swept**.

**AND THE EIGHT OPENING TAPS WERE NOT MISSES.** They are `empty-ground`, the last branch of `onTap` — and it was the ONLY outcome in that method that did nothing: no cue, no hint, no visible change, and no telemetry, because `empty-ground` never reached the counter either. The comment above it has read *"just look there"* since FIX 1 and **nothing ever looked anywhere**. A survivor lands on a beach with nothing in reach, taps the sand, and the game answers with total silence eight times. That is [[D-042]] breached in the one branch nobody counted as an interaction — *silence is never a legal outcome, and "never mind" is still an outcome*. The gesture now does what it always claimed: the survivor TURNS to face the point, which is real, costs nothing, cannot nag, and is useful, because `onBuildFire` and `onBuildShelter` both place by `this.facing`. **`trace.groundTaps` counts them from now on.** No migration: `hydrate` merges `{ ...fresh.trace, ...old.trace }`, and there is now a test that loads a save with the field deleted rather than a belief that it would work.

**P0-B — THE TENTH INSTANCE, AND THE FIRST FOUND INSIDE THE HARNESS.** The spear has had a recipe, a craft function, a verb, a Build row, a `TOOL_IDS` entry and a hand slot since Drop 1, and **not one line of render code**. [[D-147]]'s check passed 17/17 because it witnessed the LIST and the PANEL: `spear:right` printed over a character holding nothing. `PlayerView` now carries a shaft and a knapped point across the back — two-handed, so not on a hip beside the axe — under the same rule the axe set: no equip step, owning it is wearing it. **The check now reads `meshInfo('spearShaft').enabled` and the projected point INSIDE the viewport.** That second half matters: the first cut asserted only `Number.isFinite`, and it stayed GREEN against the planted defect at `y = -35`, thirty-five pixels above the screen. *Finite is not in frame.*

**P0-F — D-142 FIXED THE OTHER HALF.** D-142 closed the cave from OUTSIDE, with a ring of obstacles, and declared it done. The Director was reporting the material. `caveBluff` is a cylinder with default culling, so it is a one-way surface: stand inside — which the mouth exists to allow — and every wall is simply not drawn. **Nothing was transparent; the inner faces never existed.** Culling off, and witnessed from inside: the survivor 0.00 m from the cave centre, camera 1.31 m, `twoSided true`.

**P0-A — THE THIRD HYPOTHESIS, AND THE PREVIOUS FIX WAS HALF-APPLIED.** Not a seeded flag and not a migration: **the HUD never consulted the gate.** `paintHud` read `fireIsKnown(state)` and then `visible: state.inventory.wood > 0` — and the comment directly above it describes fixing this exact bug while leaving that clause in place. Compounding it, `suspicionFor(state, 'torch')` flips `suspected` at **one wood and one fibre**, with the need felt from the first second ashore. One stick and one strand, and the button read *"Build fire (4 short)"* at 0.34 h on a life with no deaths. The HUD now asks `canBuildFire`, whose own comment says *"May this be OFFERED to the player? Knowledge and matter both."* **The planted run reproduced the Director's string exactly.**

**P0-G — the fire's bed gain was created, set once, and dropped**, so a fire sounded identical from beside it and from across the island. The gain node is kept and ramped by distance, and the authored volume drops 0.26 → 0.16. **What the bench can and cannot see is stated rather than implied:** audio never decodes headless, so the gain node does not exist and reading it returns null forever — the curve is proven in a unit test where it is pure, and the device check proves only that the mixer was TOLD, with a falling factor. Two honest halves rather than one confident whole.

**THE QUESTION C1 ASKED — did the Director use the bandage and the boiled water — WAS UNANSWERABLE, AND NOW ISN'T.** Neither `vessel.ts` nor `injury.ts` wrote anything to the trace, the debug export dumps only the trace, and a DOM button press never reaches `recordTap`. A shipped verb going completely unused was indistinguishable from one working perfectly. **`trace.woundsBound` and `trace.sipsBoiled` now count the act itself.** Their silence in the last report is not evidence either way and is not treated as any.

**REPORTED, NOT TUNED, BECAUSE THE BRIEF ASKED FOR A VERDICT AND NOT A CONSTANT:** `realSecondsPerGameHour` is 150, so the Director's session was **9.9 game hours** and `msToFireLit` was **8.5 of them**. Building IS lighting — `markFireLit` fires inside `onBuildFire`, there is no separate ignition — so that 86% is time-to-gather 5 wood and 1 fibre, against an opening in which the first eight taps answered with nothing. **Node regrowth is 48 h deadfall, 60 h coconut palm, 72 h rock, against initial densities of 2, 2 and 3 — five to seven times longer than the whole session**, so within one sitting they are single-use, which is what 0/3, 0/2, 0/2 records. `salvage 2/10` is the D-051 beach spawner working: eight were claimed and consumed.

**AND ONE MORE THING P0-A TAUGHT, FOUND BY MY OWN CHECK FAILING IN THE SWEEP AND PASSING ALONE.** The torch route's need is `isNight || warmth < warmthLowThreshold`, so **a warm survivor in daylight is not offered fire however much wood they carry** — Law 113 exactly as written. My second fixture left warmth at 90, passed standalone (a fresh run opens at night) and went red hours into the full sweep, which would have read as the fix being wrong. Both fixtures now pin the need so the checks isolate the MATTER half, which is the half P0-A is about. It also finishes the Director's story: **the button they saw at 0.34 h was during the first night, when the need is genuinely felt** — so the report was of a real defect arriving through a real gate, not of a flag stuck on.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `empty-ground` in `src/body/game.ts`, `say()` beside `explain()`, `PlayerView`'s spear, `CaveView`'s material, `paintHud`'s gate, the fire bed's gain, and four new trace counters; shipped in this batch).

**NOT BUILT, NAMED RATHER THAN IMPLIED:** P0-C (never auto-commit, the Director's crafting ruling), P0-D (illness with a felt cost), P0-E (growth grammar for carry/walk/swim/dive) and P0-H (the radio's first contact) are **untouched**. Four fixes done properly with fail-then-pass beat eight done on faith, and this session's whole subject is checks that passed without witnessing anything. Wave 1 stays shut regardless.

**D-149 · 2026-08-12 — v2.8 FILED; the FOURTH identifier collision, and the tiebreak that ends the series. One project name, everywhere.**

**THE COLLISION.** *Design Bible v2.8* declares **Laws 201–235**. The v2.7 amendment sheet, filed one day earlier at [[D-145]], already held **201–206**. Six numbers double-assigned inside twenty-four hours. This is the **fourth** identifier collision on this project — ENDING E03 against CAPABILITY E03, AGENT C1 against ITEM C1, two documents both called v2.7, and now two claims on 201–206.

**THE RULING. v2.8 keeps 201–235; the amendment sheet vacates.** Three of its six laws are ABSORBED into v2.8's own stronger versions and **deleted outright** — not carried, not renumbered — and three SURVIVE, renumbered, text otherwise unchanged.

**AND THE TIEBREAK IS NOW STANDING, written down for the first time after three ad-hoc rulings:**

> **When law or identifier numbers collide across two documents, the SMALLER document renumbers. Never the larger.**

Six laws moving beats thirty-five moving. It is a rule about total disturbance to canon, **not seniority** — the older document does not win by being older and the newer does not win by being newer, which is what made each of the first three a judgement call. It sits beneath the rule the first three already established: *a document version number is a register too, and only one document may hold one.* Had that been honoured, the third collision would not exist either.

**THE THREE ABSORBED — use v2.8's wording from here on, in every document and every brief:**

- **was 201** *Growth is embodied and never numeric* → **v2.8 Law 234**, *Progression must be felt blind*. Stronger because it is a **test rather than a prohibition**: the old law forbade showing numbers, which a build can satisfy by hiding the bar and changing nothing observable. v2.8 fails that build — the growth must be perceivable in the act, without opening a menu. Same standard as Law 26.
- **was 202** *No composite score may exist* → **v2.8 Law 202**. Stronger because it **replaces rather than only forbids**, naming the eight channels progress does travel through. The number coincidence is not a collision: it is the same subject, which is part of why absorption beat renumbering.
- **was 205** *A diagnosed failure raises understanding* → **v2.8 Laws 209 + 230**. Stronger because the pair **separates two claims the single law had fused**: 209 fixes experience as evidence that cannot be spent, and 230 sets the transfer bar the old law never set — repetition at the same bench proves consistency and nothing more.

**THE THREE SURVIVORS.** **Law 236** (was 203) — a tracked score with no producer is a defect; **still uncovered by v2.8**, which legislates what progression must feel like and never that a tracked quantity must be raisable, so the seven inert `adaptation` scores stay a defect rather than a to-do, and this remains the **eighth instance** of the zero-caller class. **Law 237** (was 204) — the manufacture graph is a superset of the shipped game; **the TORCH is a standing defect under it**, in shipped code and reachable through the fire's own circle (`light-torch`, `src/brain/verbs.ts`) and in no node of the graph — and the newly filed item catalogue **confirms it independently**, listing "Simple torch" on its own *Canon Gaps* sheet. **Law 238** (was 206) — seed lines and tamed animals are inheritable across the Cycle.

**THE PROJECT NAME. There is one, and it is THE FIRST NIGHT.** "RUSTED" is struck from all three filed documents: the v2.8 Bible's header line, and the title cells of both workbooks — *Executive* and *v2.8 Progression* in the crafting model, *Summary* in the item catalogue. This hardens what [[D-071]] already ordered case-by-case (*"strip RUSTED and any Godot-specific assumptions wherever any section is later adopted"*) into a rule that needs no adoption event to apply. **It also half-closes a standing Director fork** — the parked "Godot RUSTED workspace" question in `handover_challenge_sprint.md` can no longer be a naming question, only a trust-in-external-build-claims question.

**NOT DONE, AND COUNTED EXACTLY: nine already-filed artifacts still carry the old name.** Six markdown documents label the project with it — v2.5, v2.6, and the v0.9 / v0.10 / v0.11 / v0.12 weaves, as *"Project: THE FIRST NIGHT / DRIFT / RUSTED"* or *"Internal codename: DRIFT / RUSTED"* — and **two already-filed workbooks carry it in their own title cells**, the v2.6 body-water model and the v2.7 civilization-weave model, which a sweep of every part of every filed workbook found and which no one had looked for before. The ninth, `handover_challenge_sprint.md`, is the **only one that should keep it**: there the word is the subject of an open Director fork, not a label, and striking it would erase the question. So the debt is **eight**. This pass was scoped to the three arriving files and I did not widen it, because a filed document is byte-identical to its source by construction and editing filed canon in place is a larger call than a filing session takes unilaterally. **The ruling says "everywhere, no exceptions", so this is a real gap — owed, dated, and now enumerated rather than estimated.**

**AND v2.8'S OWN STATED PRODUCTION TRUTH IS ALREADY FALSE — corrected here rather than edited into the Director's document.** Its header reads *"Production truth: Designed, not implemented or Director-device accepted."* That was true when written and is not true now. **Law 216** (*the pattern book remains empty*) and **Law 220** (*slot count represents controlled relations*) are **PARTLY SHIPPED as of [[D-147]] / `97886bd`**: the pattern book ships empty and fills only by attempt, and P0-1 closed the last door by which the resolver picked a named product on the player's behalf — **the game now asks instead of choosing**, device-proven. Law 220's two-relation start is likewise the shipped state, with the third staging position gated on a physical enabler rather than on experience, which is §3.2's ruling and now v2.8's law. **The document body is untouched by design**: one document per version number means a filed document is not silently amended, so the correction lives here, where corrections are citable.

**Class: DESIGN-BINDING** — no mechanism ships from this entry. What binds immediately is the numbering (236–238, and the three absorbed numbers unusable), the tiebreak rule for the next collision, the single project name, and the production-truth correction. The build order is **unchanged**: P0-3, P0-5, W-HEARTH, A-BOAT-B1 and A-DIVE's shore station remain next, and v2.8 introduces no new build order this pass.

**D-148 · 2026-08-12 — WAVE 0 PART TWO: the bandage heals, and the water boils. Four verbs, four reachability proofs.**

**P0-2 / A-BANDAGE — CLOSED, AND THEY WERE ONE VERB.** `bindWound` has existed since Drop 2 with a verb-circle route and no surface on the tab that READS the wound. Worse, the Vitals tab said *"Bleeding — bind it with fibre at the shelter"* while `canBindWound` has never had a location term: the prose sent a bleeding survivor on a walk they did not need and gave them no way to act when they arrived. A `bind-btn` now sits in the Injuries row, routed to the SAME `doBindWound` the shelter's circle already uses — one verb, not a second implementation beside it — and `bindBlocker` supplies the reason so the readout can never again describe a requirement the rule does not have. **Device-proven at 90 m from the shelter**: bleeding 2 → 0, fibre 6 → 5.

**AND THE PROSE DEFECT WAS FOUND BY ITS OWN FAIL-THEN-PASS.** Planting *"You would need to be at the shelter."* back into `bindBlocker` left the suite GREEN — nothing tested it at all. Four checks now do, including one that sweeps the sentence for any mention of a place and one that binds successfully from three positions across the island.

**THE THREE WATER RUNGS — BUILT FROM THE FILED MODEL, NOT GUESSED.** Every prerequisite is read off `the_first_night_body_water_work_manufacture_model_v2_6.xlsx`, sheets "Water Craft Tree", "Treatment Matrix" and "Water Sources":

- **W1 · C-COCONUT-CUP** — coconut + a cutting edge, and the blade is a tool USED rather than spent, because the sheet's operations are open/clean/stabilize and none of those eats a knife. It holds more than the flask a survivor might FIND, which is what makes it worth the coconut.
- **W2c · C-FOUND-PAN** — recovered recognised cookware in wreck-era metal, scarce by construction because `metal` has exactly one source and it is 115 m out. It REPLACES the cup rather than stacking, and keeps water already boiled.
- **W2a · C-COCONUT-BOILER** — the boil itself, gated on the matrix's own ancestry: *"Fire + viable vessel + observed boil"*. `boilRefusalFor` names which of the four is missing.
- **P-CLEAN-WATER** — treated water as a QUANTITY, not a flag, because the matrix's qualification is *"completed boil + clean cooling/storage"*. Drinking it costs no illness; drinking at the pond still does, and that difference is the reward for building the rung. A pond is the register's own *"representative illness source"* at microbial risk H, and a rolling boil is exactly what takes biological risk out — **and nothing else**, which is why the refusals exist rather than a boil that quietly makes the sea drinkable.

**THE WHOLE CHAIN IS DEVICE-PROVEN, one real gesture per rung.** A hold on the water offers *"Open a coconut"* and a real press makes the cup (coconut 2 → 1, blade untouched); a real press fills it (2 raw); a hold on the fire offers *"Boil water"* and a real press turns 2 raw into 2 clean, saying *"It comes to a rolling boil, and holds it. Whatever was in it is dead."*; and a real tap on the Vitals row drinks it — thirst 44.3 → 69.3, clean 2 → 1, **illness severity 0**.

**[[D-011]]:** every function runs inside a verb; `reconcile` has no water term. Proved over four hours, two days and a month, in both directions — an absence cannot take treated water and cannot hand any over. Schema **v31 → v32**, vessel migrating in ABSENT.

**AND THE POND'S OWN CIRCLE IS UNREACHABLE AT ITS CENTRE — a real finding, not a fixture quirk.** `fp-pond` is authored at EXACTLY `POND.x, POND.y`, so a hold on the middle of the water resolves to the fishing spot and opens `[cast-line, set-net, spear-fish]`. The pond's own verbs — drink, fill, and now the cup — never appear there. Four filtered runs read this as *"no circle opened"* and I chased the gesture through two rewrites and a removed walk; **the full sweep printed the segments and named the cause in one line.** The lesson is the filter's own: a filtered run that fails early never reaches the print that would explain it, and the confirming pass is not optional — the same sweep also caught the tap-log check going red with an EMPTY detail string, blind in exactly the way this session kept paying for. The rung is proven by holding six metres out, which is still the pond; **that the centre is taken by the fishery is worth a ruling and is recorded here rather than worked around silently.**

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `src/brain/vessel.ts`, the pond and fire verbs in `src/brain/verbs.ts`, `bindBlocker` in `src/brain/injury.ts`, and their surfaces in `src/body/game.ts` / `src/body/hud.ts`; shipped in this batch).

**NOT BUILT, named rather than implied:** P0-3 (dropped items surfaced and recovered) and P0-5 (the journal's first contact) remain root-caused and unbuilt. W-HEARTH, A-BOAT-B1 and A-DIVE's shore station are untouched. `drift_design_vs_shipped_audit_v2_7.xlsx` was ordered a third time and is **still not on disk** under any prefix; the v2.7 amendment sheet was already filed at [[D-145]] and is byte-identical to its source.

The single red is `UPKEEP 4b`, which passed in all three prior full sweeps and passes when its own section is re-run: the tap-landing family, and its detail line now carries whether the tap LANDED — `tap true` on the re-run — so the next occurrence can say which. **AND THE FIRST BISECT OF IT WAS RUN AGAINST THE WRONG SECTION** — `--only="A1"` matched the shelter-construction block, not the entropy block that owns UPKEEP, and returned a confident 29/29 for a check it never executed. A filter that matches SOMETHING is not a filter that matched the RIGHT thing, and a green total is not a witness unless the check you are asking about is named in it. **LIVE: see the push SHA and the deploy's served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-147 · 2026-08-11 — WAVE 0 PART ONE LANDS: the choice, the spear, the felt illness. Merged, not rebuilt.**

**THREE DIRECTOR-CONFIRMED DEFECTS, AND ALL THREE ARE ONE SHAPE** — a system that works perfectly and cannot be reached, seen or felt. None was a missing feature. The branch `wave0/p0-brain` was merged as preserved at `8780f79` per the order; everything after it is body wiring, tests and device proof.

**P0-1 — THE GAME STOPS CHOOSING.** 5 wood + 5 stone silently built a crate when the director wanted a hammer: both match, both were known, and `resolveRecipe` picked. When staged materials satisfy more than one pattern the survivor ALREADY knows, the game now **offers the choice** through the shipped radial circle — two positions, no third, no new interaction system. **Being asked costs nothing**, proven on device: wood 9 → 9, stone 9 → 9, nothing built while the question is open. **Discovery is untouched**: a survivor who knows neither thing is still inventing, still sees property hints and never a menu of named products, because the offer is filtered to recipes they already hold blueprints for.

**AND `makeChosen`'s GUARD WAS WRONG ON ITS FIRST DAY.** It checked the choice against `knownMatches`, which returns nothing below two — so naming the thing you meant was REFUSED whenever the pile was unambiguous, and three shipped tests caught it at once. Naming what you are making is legal whenever the recipe matches the pile and the survivor knows it; **ambiguity is what makes the game ASK, not what makes an answer valid.**

**P0-4 — THE SPEAR, AND THE NINTH INSTANCE.** It kills a boar, so it exists; it was absent from `TOOL_IDS`, and that list is the only thing reaching the pack readout and the equip path. Owned, usable, invisible — [[D-114]]'s class in its purest form, after `craftSpear`, `makeBackpack`, `submergedDepthM`, `fishRingSpent`, the trace tap, `manuals()`, `BOAT_LADDER` and the boat's undertuned technique route. On device it now shows as a chip and a real tap puts it in the hand, two-handed, leaving the support hand free.

**THE INVARIANT IS WRITTEN FOR THE LIST, NOT THE SPEAR** — and it found three more names before it found its shape. Not every tool is HELD: a backpack is worn, a line and a net are deployed. Those are legitimately outside `TOOL_IDS` and legitimately visible, because each has a Build-panel row. So the rule is the union: **every tool the model can own must be reachable EITHER by hand OR by panel.** The spear was in neither. Adding a tool now forces that decision instead of allowing it to be forgotten. My first probe asked `revealedInPanel`, which gates on the LADDER and answered false for every non-held tool — the catalogue, not this survivor, is what decides whether a panel can show a thing.

**P0-6 — ILLNESS IS FELT BEFORE IT IS NAMED.** The goal line only ever spoke an illness once it was COSTING, so both free warning stages were silent and the director drank pond water and felt nothing. `illnessSymptom` gives the two free stages a sensation — *"Your stomach turns over, and does not settle."* — read off the screen on device with **no panel open**, and swept to prove it never says "bad water", "dysentery" or "illness" (Law 145: symptoms as evidence, never labels). Watched centrally on `Session` rather than per cause, because drinking happens in a VERB and not on the tick — a hook beside the chill term would have missed the one actually reported.

**THE BREADCRUMB IS WHAT CRACKED THE LAST FAILURE.** A pick that resolved correctly looked identical to a pick that never happened: circle gone, nothing built, hint unchanged. `chose:stonehammer:invented` in the tap trail settled it in one run — the choice had worked end to end and **my check asserted the wrong consequence.** Combining yields a BLUEPRINT; building the object is a separate verb. The claim was corrected to the director's own — the game must act on the HAMMER and leave the crate alone — which is what is now asserted.

**AND THREE SHIPPED TESTS ENCODED THE OLD LAW.** Once a survivor holds both plans wood+fibre makes (the torch and the backpack share that gesture), the pile is a question — so tests that looped attempts never saw a failure again. Rewritten to attempt normally and NAME the torch once the pile becomes ambiguous, per [[D-113]]'s precedent: rewritten to the new law rather than deleted, so a silent revert of P0-1 still fails there.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `knownMatches`/`isAmbiguousToPlayer`/`makeChosen` in `src/brain/experiment.ts`, `TOOL_IDS` in `src/brain/loadout.ts`, `illnessSymptom` in `src/brain/illness.ts` with `watchIllness` on `Session`, and their surfaces in `src/body/game.ts`; shipped in this batch).

**NOT BUILT, and named rather than implied:** P0-2 (the bandage verb from Vitals), P0-3 (dropped items surfaced and recovered) and P0-5 (the journal's first contact) are root-caused and unbuilt. The four half-built nodes and the three water rungs of the filing manifest's Part Three are untouched. Wave 0 part two.

**D-146 · 2026-08-11 — THE HANDOVER WAS MIS-NAMED, NEVER MISSING. Absence asserted from a grep on a guessed filename.**

**THE RECORD IS CORRECTED.** `handover_challenge_sprint.md` was ordered twice and reported 404 twice. It was never absent. The source exists as **`the_first_night_handover_challenge_sprint.md`**, carrying the `the_first_night_` prefix that every Bible, weave and model document in this project uses. Both prior orders searched the bare name, found nothing, and reported the document as lost. [[D-145]] filed it under the ordered name; this entry names the CAUSE, because the filing fixed an instance and the cause is what will otherwise repeat.

**THE CAUSE: ABSENCE ASSERTED FROM A GREP ON A GUESSED FILENAME.** The search term was constructed from how the document was referred to in prose, not from how it was written to disk. A grep that finds nothing proves the pattern did not match — it does not prove the thing does not exist, and the gap between those two sentences is the whole defect. Two orders' worth of "retire it formally, the source no longer exists" rested on it.

**SAME CLASS AS THE THREE COLLISIONS, IN A FILENAME.** [[D-081]] separated three vocabularies that had collided on the word OPERATIVE. [[D-138]] required every E-code to name its register after `E03` meant two unrelated things. [[D-145]] ruled one document per version number after two documents both claimed v2.7. This is the fourth: **a short identifier used as though it were unique and canonical when it was neither**. A filename is an identifier like any other, and the prose name and the disk name were two different registers all along.

**THE RULE THAT FALLS OUT OF IT.** A document is ABSENT only when a search that could not have missed it comes back empty — by content, by prefix-insensitive stem, or by directory listing. Never on a single exact-name grep. This is [[D-097]] clause (e) pointed the other way: an edit must witness its landing, and **an absence must witness its own search**.

**Class: OPERATIVE** — the correction and the search rule govern immediately; the mechanism is the filed document at [[D-145]] plus this record of why two orders missed it.

**STILL GENUINELY ABSENT:** `drift_design_vs_shipped_audit_v2_7.xlsx`. Ordered again this pass and searched again by stem (`design_vs_shipped`, `audit_v2_7`) across the repository, Downloads, Desktop and Documents — nothing, under any prefix. It is not on disk, and unlike the handover it is not hiding behind a name. Owed.

**D-145 · 2026-08-11 — CANON FILING PASS: v2.6 and v2.7 filed, the v2.7 version collision ruled, and one-document-per-version made standing practice.**

**SEVEN FILES FILED, each verified byte-identical against its source and each grepped at ORIGIN after the push per [[D-097]]'s clause (e).**

To `docs/reference/`: `the_first_night_design_bible_v2_6_body_water_work_and_survival_manufacture.md`, `the_first_night_design_bible_v2_7_civilization_weave_and_second_life.md`, `the_first_night_bible_v2_7_amendment_sheet_laws_201_206.md`, `drift_canon_filing_manifest_and_build_brief.md`, and `handover_challenge_sprint.md`. To `docs/reference/model/`: `the_first_night_body_water_work_manufacture_model_v2_6.xlsx`, `the_first_night_civilization_weave_model_v2_7.xlsx`.

**THE HANDOVER WAS NEVER MISSING — IT WAS MIS-NAMED.** Ordered twice and 404 twice, and reported here rather than retired: the source exists as `the_first_night_handover_challenge_sprint.md`, carrying the project-wide `the_first_night_` prefix that every Bible and weave document uses. Both prior orders searched for the bare name. Filed under the ordered name, `handover_challenge_sprint.md`, so the citation that has been failing now resolves. **It is NOT retired**; there was nothing wrong with it but a prefix.

**ONE FILE COULD NOT BE FILED.** `drift_design_vs_shipped_audit_v2_7.xlsx` **does not exist** anywhere reachable — a wide search across the repository, Downloads, Desktop and Documents returns nothing under that name or under `design_vs_shipped`. Named as absent rather than substituted: the nearest thing present is `drift_bible_vs_shipped.csv`, which is a DIFFERENT artefact and is not filed in its place. It is owed.

**AND ONE PLACEMENT CORRECTED.** The v2.6 model was filed to `docs/reference/` earlier in this same session under the previous order; this pass moves it to `docs/reference/model/`, which is where every model belongs and where the v2.5 model already sits.

**THE VERSION RULING.** *"Civilization Weave and Second Life"* is **canonical v2.7** and holds **Laws 163–200**. C1's *"Skills, Salvage, Forge and Depth"* is **WITHDRAWN IN FULL**; its additive content survives as **Laws 201–206** in the amendment sheet filed above. **Nothing may cite Laws 163–200 against the withdrawn document** — those law numbers belong to the Civilization Weave alone.

**THE STANDING RULE, and this is the THIRD register collision.** **ONE DOCUMENT PER VERSION NUMBER.** Two documents both claiming v2.7 is what produced this ruling, and the pattern is now unmistakable: [[D-081]] separated three vocabularies that had collided on the word OPERATIVE; [[D-138]] required every E-code citation to name its register after `E03` meant two unrelated things; and a version number is the same kind of short identifier used as though it were unique. A version is only unambiguous if exactly one document bears it.

**Class: OPERATIVE** — the ruling and the rule govern immediately; the mechanism is the filed set itself plus the citation discipline it establishes.

**NO DESIGN WORK IN THIS PASS**, per the order. Wave 0 — six defects, four half-built nodes, three water rungs — is briefed in the filing manifest's Part Three and starts next session. The brain-layer work that was in flight when this order landed is preserved OFF MAIN on branch `wave0/p0-brain` at `8780f79`, unshipped and unwitnessed, the same preservation [[D-108]] used for `resolver/one-body`.

**D-144 · 2026-08-10 — DROP 6, "THE READOUT": what the body knows, made perceivable. One grammar, not eight patches.**

**SIX FINDINGS SAID THE SAME THING IN DIFFERENT WORDS.** The game develops the survivor and never tells them: mastery speeds the swing, breath lengthens the dive, capacities climb — all of it shipped, all of it invisible. `src/brain/readout.ts` is the single answer, and every surface reads from it rather than deriving its own version. Six surfaces each deriving their own reading is how a screen comes to disagree with the hands, which is what `refugeReport` was called *"the liar"* for.

**IT ADDS NO STATE AND NO MECHANIC.** Every reading is a pure derivation from models that already ship — `masteryFor`, `airCapacityOf`, `standingOf`. No XP, no tree, no counter, no field, no migration. A test sweeps the module's exports for anything that could grant, award, spend or level, and another asserts that reading it never mutates the thing it reads.

**THE COMPARISON IS AGAINST THE DAY THEY LANDED, which is what makes it sayable without storing anything.** `masteryFor` is exactly 1 at the innate floor by construction, so *"what would this have cost me on day one?"* is a derivation rather than a memory. No previous-best field, no running average.

**WORLD FIRST (Law 26), and witnessed as such.** The act reads differently before any panel confirms it: felling a tree says *"1.3 seconds faster than your first — steadier with it now"* **with no panel open at any point**, and surfacing says what the lungs have become. Silent until the gain clears `readoutNoticeableSeconds`, so the first few swings say nothing and the one that has genuinely got easier says so — the same rule that keeps `holding` silent in the dive.

**CONCRETE, NEVER NUMERIC (Bible v2.3).** Experience rewards INFORMATION GAINED, so what a survivor is told is a change they can feel and a band they can see themselves moving through. On device the Skills tab reads *"With the axe [practised] — 2.9 seconds faster than your first"* with a bar at 80%, and *"On and under the water [practised] — 8.5 seconds longer under than your first breath"* at 87%. A survivor who has done nothing reads *"No steadier than the day you washed ashore"* at 0%. **No score reaches the screen**, and a check sweeps the rendered panel to prove it.

**THREE REAL BUGS, EACH CAUGHT BY THE THING BUILT TO CATCH IT.** (1) The breath baseline was ZERO, so a survivor who had never dived was told they could hold their breath 1.1 s longer — `freshCapacities()` starts every capacity AT the innate floor, and zero is a body that has never existed. The unit suite caught it on its first run. (2) The hands reading was passed the ALREADY-MASTERED hold as its baseline and multiplied the discount in a second time, understating the saving to 0.85 s — under its own one-second threshold, so a practised feller was told nothing. The device check caught it, and the boulder note firing throughout is what proved the wiring was live and the arithmetic was not. (3) My anti-drift test then passed the FRESH survivor's hold into the corrected contract — my own misuse of the rule I had just written.

**THE BOULDER IS SLOW ON PURPOSE, AND THE SENTENCE SAYS SO HONESTLY.** Measured before writing a word: one node, 2 stone a swing, inexhaustible, **5.5 s by hand against 3.0 s with a stone hammer**. So the hammer is an ACCELERATOR, never a gate, and the note says *"Slow work by hand — it gives, just grudgingly. A stone hammer would halve it, and the face never runs out."* A test forbids the words "required" and "need a", because the false sentence would send a survivor away from a face working perfectly well for them.

**A READOUT NEEDS ITS OWN WITNESS.** It is SHOWN as a hint, which is the right surface and the wrong instrument: the standing-hint system legitimately replaces it moments later, and the first device check read an empty string off a readout that had fired perfectly. `runtime.lastReadout` is read-only ([[D-075]]) and keeps the readout's own words — the same lesson `runtime.lastCue` learned for the crash's audible half at [[D-141]].

**IT TAKES ITS TONE FROM [[D-143]].** An inert iron wedge bought real excitement in the director's own play; a readout that says *"2.9 seconds faster"* rather than a number is the same bet — that a survivor engages with a concrete, felt thing far harder than with a score.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `src/brain/readout.ts`, the world-first announcements in `src/body/game.ts`, and the Skills tab's readout rows; shipped in this batch).

**DEFERRED, named rather than implied:** the FIRST-CONTACT nudges for the boat and the shelter site (the brief's item 4) are NOT built — they are evidence-led copy work on two existing surfaces and want their own pass rather than a tail-end addition to this one. No numeric XP economy and no skill tree, per the brief: that fork is the director's, and nothing here forecloses it.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity (55 brain files), docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1297/1297 across 68 files**, including 12 new readout checks. **DEVICE: ran. DROP 6 16/16**, including the reachability proof — a real tap through to Skills, words on the rendered page — and the world-first line landing with no panel open. `READOUT 6` carries [[D-142]]'s cave fix onto the device: pressed at the side, the survivor now stops at 4.77 m of centre where the old collider let them reach 1.94 m, three metres inside solid rock. **LIVE: see the push SHA and the deploy's served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-143 · 2026-08-10 — THE INERT IRON WEDGE WORKED. Atmosphere earning engagement, validated in the director's own play.**

**THE REACTION WAS EXCITEMENT, THEN CONFUSION — and both were the design.** The rusted iron head from the Junk & Flavour Catalogue ([[D-131]]) does nothing mechanically: it yields `{}`, holds no note, moves no ladder, and answers the hundredth tap exactly as it answered the first. In the director's own playtest it produced real excitement on being found and real confusion on turning out to be inert. **That is the intended reaction, on content that is mechanically empty** — which is the whole claim the catalogue was built to test.

**IT VALIDATES LAWS 65-69's REPRESENTATIVE APPROACH.** A family opens with one honest representative rather than a catalogue, and the representative earns the family. Six objects that do nothing bought a genuine emotional response — so atmosphere is load-bearing rather than decoration, and it earns engagement as hard as a mechanic does. Worth recording because the cheap conclusion from "the player was confused" is to make the object DO something, and doing so would spend exactly the thing that worked.

**IT SHOULD SHAPE THE TONE OF WHAT FOLLOWS**, and it shaped [[D-144]]'s directly: a readout that says *"2.9 seconds faster than your first"* rather than a number is the same bet — that a survivor engages with a concrete, felt thing far harder than with a score.

**Class: OPERATIVE** — the finding governs tone from here; its mechanism is the catalogue already shipped at [[D-131]] and unchanged by this entry.

**D-142 · 2026-08-10 — PART 0 TRIAGE: the save question answered from the code, and the cave made genuinely solid.**

**FRESH VERSUS CONTINUED SAVE — ANSWERED FROM EVIDENCE, AND THE EVIDENCE IS DETERMINISTIC.** The director's save is not in this repository and his device cannot be read from here, so the question was settled the only way that actually decides it: by what the two paths DO. `createInitialState` ships `backpack: false` — a fresh castaway has none and must make one ([[D-113]]). `migrateV18toV19` ships `backpack: old.tools?.backpack ?? true` — a pre-v19 save migrates in **already carrying one**, deliberately, because taking a tool out of a hand mid-run is the one thing a migration must never do.

There is therefore **no path by which a fresh run produces a backpack**. A report of the survivor already having one identifies a CONTINUED, correctly-migrated save and nothing else. The two findings that turned on it are expected behaviour: **closed, no fix needed**, and the gate on the rest of the batch is discharged rather than assumed away. Three tests now lock the discriminator so the next person reads it instead of remembering it, and `schemaVersion` in Copy Debug Info settles any future instance in one look.

**THE CAVE WAS GENUINELY NOT SOLID, AND THE NUMBER IS 1.94 METRES.** Walking a survivor in from each bearing and resolving collision every step — which is what the game does, and is not the same as sampling points — the collider that shipped gave: mouth **1.94 m** of centre, side **1.94 m**, side **1.94 m**, back 7.14 m. The bluff's base is 9.6 m across. So a survivor could walk in from either SIDE and stand **three metres inside solid rock**.

**ONE CIRCLE COULD NEVER FIX IT.** Cover the mass and the player is walled out of the one place they are trying to reach — [[D-051]]'s unminable quarry in new geometry, and the reason the offset existed at all. Shrink it and the rock is a ghost. `ringObstacles` is a pure primitive — a wall of overlapping blocks with the mouth's sector left out — so the shape is provable without a renderer, and `ringIsContinuous` states the overlap as arithmetic so a retune cannot open a seam silently. After: side **5.54 m**, back 5.54 m, and the mouth still admits the survivor to the centre.

**MY FIRST PROBE WAS WRONG AND SAID SO LOUDLY.** It SAMPLED points at each distance and asked whether `pushOut` moved them, which reported the new wall as letting everything through — a point dropped at the cave's centre is inside no block and is left alone. Teleporting is not walking, and a collision model only means anything against motion. The suite keeps the old collider so the defect stays reproducible rather than remembered.

**THE BOULDER WORKS — checked, not assumed.** One node at (38, −34): 2 stone a swing, repeatable and inexhaustible, **5.5 s by hand against 3.0 s with a stone hammer**. It is a legibility item rather than a defect, and it carries a correction: **the hammer is not required, it is an accelerator.** The honest sentence is *"slow by hand, faster with a hammer, and never used up"* — see [[D-144]], which ships it.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `ringObstacles`/`ringIsContinuous` in `src/brain/movement.ts` and the cave's wall in `src/body/entities.ts`; shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity (54 brain files), docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck. **UNIT: ran. 1285/1285 across 67 files** — 8 new cave-wall checks including the old collider's defect reproduced as its own test, and 3 backpack-gate checks. **DEVICE: carried into [[D-144]]'s run**, where `READOUT 6` presses the survivor at the bluff's side on a real device and stops them at 4.77 m of centre. **LIVE: pushed as `99c692d`.***

**D-141 · 2026-08-10 — DROP 3B(i): THE APPOINTMENT. The island's first deadline, and the storm's own clock extracted to run it.**

**A NEW SITE, AND NOT THE WRECK.** The Wreck ([[D-124]]/[[D-125]]) is a permanent structure in open water, worked over and over as the tide shifts it. This is a forest crash on LAND, and it is the opposite kind of thing: it exists for a few days and then it does not. Nothing here touches `state.wreck`, its nodes or its instability — a test asserts that working the site moves neither. The only thing the two share is the material economy, which is the point: wreck-era metal, cable and glass are what falls out of the sky as well as out of the sea.

**TRAJECTORY, NOT A SPAWN ROLL.** `CRASH_SITE` is authored inland on the bearing the wreck already lies on — that line continued past the shore — so the smoke, the site and the direction it came down on cannot disagree.

**THE WORLD TELLS YOU FIRST (Law 26), AND A DEVICE PROBE CORRECTED ME ON HOW.** I first asserted the column was on screen from the beach unprompted. It is not: at the camera's resting pitch nothing above the horizon is in frame — [[D-135]] measured the same thing looking for the far island — and the foot of the column reads y = −129 in a 412-tall frame. So the announcement is what the brief actually describes: something **HEARD** coming down (a heavy impact, needing no gesture at all), and then smoke when you look up. `runtime.lastCue` is new so the audible half is witnessable rather than asserted.

**SIX STAGES, RAIN'S OWN GRAMMAR:** none / sighted / standing / fresh / picked-over / overgrown. **TWO FREE STAGES** before anything is at stake, and `standing` — the preparation window — is the longer of them, because reading the sky has to be worth more than reacting to it. Once overgrown it is **GONE FOR GOOD**: a terminal stage, no second crash, no reschedule.

**NO THIRD SCHEDULING SYSTEM, per the brief's explicit instruction.** The storm already owned *"scheduled at a game-hour, staged, one boundary per call"*. That shape is now `staged.ts`, and BOTH run on it — the storm's 32 checks pass unchanged on the extracted primitive. Extended, not paralleled.

**WORKING IT NEITHER SHORTENS NOR EXTENDS THE WINDOW.** That is what makes it a deadline rather than a resource bar: a survivor cannot buy time by hurrying.

**ABANDON IS A LEGITIMATE ANSWER**, and `abandonCost()` returns zero with a test on it — because "no penalty" is exactly the kind of absence somebody later softens with a small one. A survivor who reads the smoke, weighs a day's walk against what they already have, and stays home has played the loop correctly and loses nothing.

**AND A LATE ARRIVAL IS TOLD IT IS LATE.** The device run caught the overgrown site falling through to a generic *"Nothing to do there."* — throwing away the emotional payload of a deadline. The scar stays a tap candidate while gone, so the game says the one true sentence: *"The forest has closed over it."*

**[[D-011]], STRICTER THAN ANY HAZARD BEFORE IT.** `advanceCrash` runs on the online tick alone and `reconcile` has no crash term, so the window cannot open, run or close while the game is closed — **missing it while away is structurally impossible.** Deliberately unlike the storm, which an absence ENDS: a storm you were not there for is weather you did not stand in, but an appointment you were not there for would be a deadline missed for not playing. A month offline leaves it untouched with the whole window still to be had. Schema **v30 → v31**.

**WRECKFALL IS ALWAYS EMPTY OF THE LIVING** — arrival canon, swept by a test over every authored line.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `src/brain/crash.ts` and `src/brain/staged.ts`, `CRASH_SITE` in `src/data/world.ts`, `advanceCrash` on `Session`'s online tick, and the site and column in `src/body/island.ts`; shipped in this batch).

**HELD, NOT BUILT:** Drop 3B(ii), the pressure pass, per [[D-140]] — nothing here guesses at hunger, thirst or the night curve.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity (54 brain files), docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1274/1274 across 66 files**, including 23 new crash checks with **seven planted defects each proven red and restored**. **DEVICE: ran. FULL SWEEP 475/475 — zero graded failures, zero console errors** — with all 12 APPT checks green. The reachability proof is a real walk in from the shore (65 m) and a real tap on the wreckage. The two FEEL COURT reds carried since [[D-137]] did not recur, and neither did the tap-landing family from [[D-139]]'s sweep: this is the first fully clean sweep in the series. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `d642d6c`.*

Drop 3B(i) resumes as the actual next item; Drop 3B(ii) (the pressure pass) is deliberately held on the director's own play. Same shape as [[D-097]]'s clause (e) and [[D-082]]'s "local is not live": the failure worth writing down is the one that looked exactly like success at the time.

**D-139 · 2026-08-10 — DROP 5, "THE STATIC": a voice on the air, and it is not for you. One rung of ENDING E03.**

**REGISTER NAMED per [[D-138]]** — ENDING E03 ("A Voice in the Static"), not the CAPABILITY register's E03 ("Defended homestead"). One rung of it, not the ending.

**THE RECEIVER COMES OUT OF THE WRECK, and that was a choice between two offered sources.** `wr3` has been *"the instrument housing, off the bow"* since [[D-124]] authored the six parts, and already yields `glass` and `wiring` — the set is the thing that housing was built to hold. It arrives through the SHIPPED hold, so there is no new verb for the taking and the crossing already gates it. The boat was the alternative and was refused: [[D-135]] caps her at stage B0 with no restoration mechanics, and hanging salvage on her hull would have implied exactly the work that cap exists to prevent.

**IT CANNOT TRANSMIT, AND THAT IS ENFORCED RATHER THAN INTENDED.** No send function, no key, no aerial, no power-out term, and no field on `RadioState` a control could bind to. A unit test sweeps the module's exported surface for anything that could send; a device check sweeps the RENDERED panel's buttons, because if a send control ever appears it will appear on the surface a player touches, not in a type.

**POWER IS THE COST.** One salvaged cell, no generator, no solar, no bench. Listening burns it; a flat cell switches the set off and says so rather than leaving a dead button to be discovered.

**RECEPTION IS CONDITIONAL, NEVER A MENU.** Traffic exists at three authored hours (05, 14, 22) and nowhere else, and weather degrades it through Rain's own `rainIntensity` ([[D-133]]) read directly — there is no weather term in this module. Tuned so the storm's two FREE warning stages leave the band alone and only its costed stages black it out: a warning stage that took the air away would make a free stage expensive, which the fair-challenge contract forbids.

**NOBODY ANSWERS, and the suite enforces the prose as hard as the mechanics.** Two fragments were rewritten because the suite's own second-person guard caught them: *"Advise you stand off"*, and *"we have you"* — the second addressed to Kestrel rather than the listener, but a listener reads it as theirs for a beat, and that beat is the softening this drop must not make.

**THE JOURNAL CARRIES IT.** A heard call sign writes as `topic: null` — the shipped shape for a plain observation, which is exactly what it is: nothing a successor could build from, just the fact that at that hour somebody was talking. It inherits the journal's condition, legibility floor, light rule and succession for free. v0.11 §10.6's *"physical evidence they left behind"*, as a consequence of what already ships rather than as a system of its own.

**FAIR CHALLENGE ON THE CHARGE ECONOMY, measured like the storm's asymmetry.** A survivor who listens at the scheduled hours hears all three fragments for about a fifth of the cell; one who leaves it running hears fewer and spends the lot. The test compares signals-per-unit-charge and requires better than 2×.

**[[D-011]] STRUCTURALLY.** `advanceListening` is the only function that spends charge or catches a fragment and it runs on the online tick alone; `reconcile` has no radio term. An absence spends nothing (the offline-death half) and delivers nothing (the offline-GIFT half), and switches the set off. Save schema **v29 → v30**; the receiver migrates in ABSENT, because salvaging it costs a crossing.

**MY OWN FAIL-THEN-PASS FOUND TWO REAL GAPS IN THE SUITE.** Three of six planted defects survived the first round. One plant was a no-op of mine; the other two were genuine. A drain planted in `afterAbsence` passed every D-011 check, because they all call `reconcile` directly and `afterAbsence` lives in `Session.start` and `Session.resume` — **[[D-129]]'s exact defect, one drop later.** And the *"a flick of the switch hears nothing"* check derived its dwell from the constant it was guarding, so setting that constant to 0 made the flick zero-length and the plant passed. Both closed; all six then proven red and restored.

**AND THE FULL SWEEP CAUGHT A DEFECT IN THE FIXTURE.** `STATIC 2` read *"owned true -> true"*: THE WRECK section works wreck parts hundreds of checks earlier and had already salvaged the set, so the claim — that a real hold HANDS IT OVER — was unobservable. It passed standalone because standalone nobody had been to the wreck. The fixture now states the world it needs rather than inheriting one.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `src/brain/radio.ts`, `SIGNALS` in `src/data/world.ts`, `advanceListening` on `Session`'s online tick, and the Inventory-tab surface; shipped in this batch).

**DEFERRED, named rather than implied:** no transmitter, no two-way, no mast, no authentication, no extraction, no ending fires. One representative thread of ENDING E03, not the 32-ending catalogue. No second cell, no recharging, no direction finding, and nothing that turns a bearing into a destination.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity (52 brain files), docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1251/1251 across 65 files**, including 25 new radio checks with **six planted defects each proven red and restored**. **DEVICE: ran. Full sweep 460/463 with all 13 STATIC checks green**, then re-witnessed **120/120** from THE WRECK onward after the fixture fix, with `owned false -> true`. **THREE REACHABILITY PROOFS, one per new verb** ([[D-114]]'s class, named non-negotiable in the brief): a real hold on the housing hands over the set; a real tap switches it on; a real tap writes the call sign into the journal. The two remaining sweep reds are the established tap/approach-landing family — the quarry setup never moved, and `UPKEEP 4b`'s tap landed on a Build hint — at **1.06 GB free**, under [[D-116]]'s ~2 GB condition, and both upstream of a section that runs last. **The two FEEL COURT reds carried from [[D-137]] did NOT recur**, which corroborates that characterisation. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `ae73071`.*

**D-138 · 2026-08-10 — THE E-CODE COLLISION: every citation names its register (C1, ratified hazard).**

**`E03` MEANS TWO UNRELATED THINGS.** In the ENDING register (v0.6/v0.7) it is *"A Voice in the Static"*. In v2.3's CAPABILITY register it is *"Defended homestead"*. Nothing distinguishes them at the point of citation, so a bare `E03` in a brief, a ledger entry or a source comment is ambiguous by construction — and the reader who resolves it wrongly is reading about a radio when the text meant a palisade.

**FROM NOW ON EVERY CITATION NAMES ITS REGISTER EXPLICITLY: `ENDING E03` / `CAPABILITY E03`.** Bare `E03` is not a citation. This is standing practice, not a one-off correction.

**[[D-081]]'S COLLISION, ONE REGISTER OVER, and it is recorded that way on purpose.** That ruling separated three vocabularies after **OPERATIVE** was found carrying a law's effectivity class and a Ch.10 plan section's standing at the same time. The lesson generalised then and it generalises now: *a short code is only unambiguous inside the register that issued it*, and this project has two registers that both start at E01. Naming the register is the same fix as naming the vocabulary — the citation carries its own namespace instead of relying on the reader's.

**Class: OPERATIVE** — the practice governs immediately; its mechanism is the citation form itself, applied from Drop 5 onward — the first work to cite an ENDING code in anger, and the reason the collision was found now rather than after it had misled somebody.

**D-137 · 2026-08-09 — F1 ROOT-CAUSED. Another section was zeroing the counter it measured against — and the absence clock was asserting that Chrome boots instantly.**

**THE LAST CARRIED ITEM, AND IT WAS DETERMINISTIC ALL ALONG.** `F1 — no input went missing during the feel section` compared `trace.failedInteractionTaps` against a value captured **fourteen sections upstream** at the fail-loud check. In between, the D-063 INPUT SAFETY section opens with `editSave('state.trace.failedInteractionTaps = 0;')` — it deliberately zeroes the counter to measure its own delta. F1 compared a pre-reset number against a post-reset one and reported a **DECREASE**.

**A DECREASE CAN NEVER BE THE DEFECT THIS CHECK EXISTS TO CATCH.** Input going missing makes that number go UP. Every red F1 has ever produced was definitionally not the thing it guards, which is precisely why chasing it never found anything, across many sweeps.

**THREE THEORIES WERE MEASURED AND KILLED BEFORE THE RIGHT ONE, and two of them looked strong.** (1) **Persistence** — `markFailedTap()` does increment in memory with no persist, and a device probe confirmed `live=1 persisted=0` immediately after a failed tap. But `pagehide`, `blur` and `visibilitychange` all persist, and the same probe watched the value survive the reload intact. No defect. (2) **Permadeath** — the sweep really does kill the survivor between the two reads, and the successor is built from a FRESH state ([[D-105]]). But `closeSurvivor` carries `trace` forward in full, under its own comment: *"Instrumentation is about the SAVE, not the survivor. A death is an event in it."* The counter does not reset on death. Wrong. (3) Only then did reading **every writer of the field** find the one that zeroes it.

**THE FIX IS WHAT THE CHECK ALWAYS CLAIMED TO DO.** F1 takes its baseline at the top of its OWN section. Nothing is relaxed — the bar is still *not one more swallowed tap* — and it can no longer be moved by any section before it. D-063 drops the reset, which **it never needed**: it reads its own `tapsBeforeClose` three lines later and compares a delta, and its own check passes unchanged on the natural baseline (19 → 19). The cross-section binding is gone from the hoisted list entirely.

**THE SAME BLINDNESS WAS INVOLVED, as [[D-136]] predicted it would be.** A bare count cannot tell a swallowed gesture from a mis-resolved one from a fixture that moved the number underneath the check — the gap `runtime.tapTrail` was added for. F1 now prints the trail beside the numbers, so the next reader gets the cause off the failure instead of building a probe to find it.

**A SECOND INSTRUMENT DEFECT, FOUND BY THE CONFIRMING SWEEP.** *"The absence advanced the clock at the tuned rate"* read 1.67 game hours on a fast machine and 2.07 on a slow one, and failed the slow one. `goAway` rewinds `savedAtMs` and reloads, and `Session.start` diffs `nowMs` against it — so the real **BOOT TIME lands on the survivor as elapsed time** too. That is the model working: the survivor really was away that long. What was wrong is a check comparing against the NOMINAL four minutes, which quietly asserts that Chrome boots instantly. Measured boots this session: 49.7 s, 54.2 s, 61.3 s, **90.8 s**. `goAway` now records the absence it actually produced and the check compares the RATE against it, **tightening the band from ±0.2 to ±0.05**. [[D-128]]'s clock-stamping class, in the one helper that must NOT stamp because simulating elapsed time is its whole job.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: F1's same-section baseline, the removed reset in D-063, and `lastAwayRealMs` in `goAway` — all in `tools/smoke.mjs`, plus `tests/harness-invariants.test.ts`; shipped in this batch). **NO PRODUCT CODE CHANGED:** `git diff 7fe3d2a -- src/ index.html` is empty.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity, docs-integrity, tune-mirror (79 of 82 values), typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1226/1226 across 64 files**, including five new harness-invariant checks with **five planted regressions each proven red and restored** — the zeroing returning, F1 reverting to the upstream binding, the baseline removed, `goAway` not measuring, and the nominal comparison coming back. **DEVICE: ran TWICE, both full sweeps. F1 GREEN in both** (`32 → 32` and `20 → 20`), which is a condition it had never met; the measured absence check green in-sweep at *"1.96 game hours against 1.96 expected — 4 min rewound + 54.2 s of real boot"*. The fail-then-pass on it is stark: reverting one line makes the same run report **FAIL at "1.93 game hours against 1.93 expected"** — the old check calling a correct game wrong. **TWO REDS REMAIN AND ARE CHARACTERISED RATHER THAN CLAIMED FIXED**: both FEEL COURT movement measurements (perpendicular travel; slide reversals 4 against a bar of `<4`). They passed on IDENTICAL product source at [[D-136]] and **cannot be bisected in isolation — the block is skipped in a filtered run**. The machine measured **1.45 GB free**, under [[D-116]]'s recorded ~2 GB condition, and the run took roughly three times D-136's wall time. Newly observed, not attributable to this batch, named rather than absorbed. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `f4ff574`.*

**D-136 · 2026-08-09 — THE PANEL WAS PRESSING ITS OWN BUTTONS. Two long-carried defects root-caused, and one fix closed seven checks.**

**WRECK 6 — RULED, THEN HOISTED.** The director's ruling: **a medical store IS a primary action.** [[D-125]] left this check red on purpose because whether it was one is a design call and not the builder's; it has now been made. The Sickness row **LEADS** the Vitals tab whenever the sickness is pressing — [[D-053]]'s own precedent, where Rest went to the top of the Build panel because *"a primary action a tired player is hunting for should not need a scroll to be seen"*. An ill survivor hunting for their medicine is that player exactly.

**CONDITIONAL, AND THE CONDITION IS THE ACTION.** The row leads only while there is something to DO about it, so a well survivor still reads their body in its natural order rather than being greeted by a Sickness heading saying *"Well"*. That hoists an ACTION to where it can be seen, which is what the precedent did; it does not reorder a readout. Confirmed with the **SAME check, unedited**: `button true (ok)`, where it had read `off-screen` since D-125. Fail-then-pass: forcing `illnessLeads = false` restores `off-screen` exactly.

**FIX 5 — ROOT-CAUSED, AND IT WAS NEVER ABOUT THE PACK.** Carried and re-confirmed as *"not this session's fault"* across many sweeps without ever being diagnosed. **Two independent causes were stacked**, and the first is a real player-facing defect that had shipped the whole time.

**CAUSE 1 — THE PANEL PRESSES ITSELF.** Every panel is `position: absolute; inset: 0` — full screen — and is created **DURING** the tap that opens it. The browser then dispatches that same touch's compatibility `click`, and by the time it lands the panel exists underneath it. A device probe measured it: tapping the pack opened the Backpack and the stray click hit `.growth-btn` at that pixel, **switching it to Skills before the thumb had left the glass**. `.panel.loadout` then stopped resolving, the harness's close missed, and the still-open full-screen panel swallowed every later tap in the column. *Two pixels higher and it presses Build.* The existing `pointerdown` guard cannot help: the panel did not exist when `pointerdown` fired, so there is nothing to stop propagating.

**`panel()` NOW ARMS** — a panel ignores HIT-TESTED input for its own 300 ms fade-in. `element.click()` is direct dispatch and unaffected, which is the right line to draw: a stray click is a hit on a surface nobody aimed at; a programmatic press is somebody naming the button.

**THE FIRST ATTEMPT AT THIS FIX DID NOTHING AT ALL**, and the probe's own readout printed `pe=none` while the panel went on pressing itself. `#ui button { pointer-events: auto }` is **more specific** than a container rule, so guarding the container guarded nothing. The shipped rules carry the `#ui` id to outrank it, and `tests/panel-guard.test.ts` locks that specificity, the button coverage, the arming call, and the fact that the window IS the fade-in — `panelArmDelayMs` is **compared against the CSS transition** rather than asserted to match it.

**AND IT WAS SEVEN CHECKS, NOT ONE.** The storage-box cluster went with it: *"the box opens promptly"* read **6881 ms** and now reads **265 ms**, and all four storage checks plus the fail-loud tap check turned green. The box panel had been opening and immediately pressing its own Close — exactly as the registered intermittents' recorded hypothesis had guessed and nobody had been able to confirm.

**CAUSE 2 — A BOAR WANDERS INTO THE FIXTURE.** A boar outranks every other target by design ([[D-109]]). `boar1` lives 42 m from the FIX 5 fixture, **but boars roam**, and by the time that section runs in a FULL SWEEP it has often reached the survivor: the first tap targets the boar, the survivor walks off, and the rest of the column samples the ground they used to stand on. Standalone the run is short, the boar is still home, and the check passes. **That is the entire standalone-passes / sweep-fails history nobody could explain.** The fixture now sends boars home — the same staging the section already does for the shelter and crate, and where an absence puts them anyway — and a new setup check FAILS LOUD if the survivor is not genuinely alone.

**THE INSTRUMENT THAT MADE BOTH VISIBLE.** `runtime.tapTrail` exposes the tap breadcrumbs read-only ([[D-075]]). `lastTapOutcome` returns the newest crumb, so a gesture that never reached `onTap` serves the previous answer again and reads as fresh — a probe built on it cannot tell *"the gesture missed"* from *"it landed and resolved wrongly"*, which is exactly the distinction FIX 5 and `JUNK 4c` had both been unable to make. The coordinates settle it. FIX 5's detail line now prints the trail; **the assertion is untouched, and it was green before the line was added.**

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: the `.arming` guard in `panel()` plus the `#ui .panel.arming` rules in `index.html`, the hoisted sickness row in `vitalsBody`, and `runtime.tapTrail`; shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity (51 brain files), docs-integrity, tune-mirror (64 refs, 79 of 82 values), typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1221/1221 across 63 files**, including six new panel-guard checks with **five planted defects each proven red and restored**. **DEVICE: ran. 450/451** on the served bundle, zero console errors. **Both carried items green IN THE FULL SWEEP**, which is the condition neither had ever met. Fail-then-pass on both against their **exact carried signatures**: removing the guard restores `0:ground 20:ground 40:ground 70:ground 120:ground`; planting a boar on the fixture reproduces it and the new setup check names it at 0.1 m. The single remaining red was `F1`, root-caused the next day at [[D-137]]. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `7fe3d2a`.*

**D-135 · 2026-08-08 — DROP 4, "THE PULL": THE WAY HOME, VISIBLE. A boat on the beach, two ways to read her, and a second route that shipped unreachable.**

**THE PROMISE, MADE PHYSICAL (Laws 124–125).** A broken fishing boat at stage **B0** — beached, hull-holed, engineless — at (14, 100) on Spawn Island, fifteen metres from where the survivor washes ashore. A **separate vessel from the raft, on home ground**: found by an ordinary shore walk, needing nothing to reach. Transom cut square where an engine was unbolted, seams open, rot along one gunwale, and a hole low in the port side that is **real geometry rather than paint**, for the same reason the cave mouth is near-black geometry — an opening reads as an opening because it is darker than everything round it, at any distance and any light.

**INSPECTION REVEALS QUESTIONS, NEVER A PARTS LIST.** A tap gives back observations and open questions through the same affordance layer everything post-pivot uses. Understanding does not buy answers — it buys **better questions**, and the difference is ORDER: *"the hull comes first"* is knowledge and is still not a shopping list. Guarded by `namesAFinishedAnswer` (the junk catalogue's own sweep, [[D-131]]) **plus a narrower net aimed at this drop's own temptation**: no line may name fibreglass, resin, tar, petrol, an outboard or a fastener. A positive control proves the guard is awake.

**TWO ROUTES TO ONE UNDERSTANDING, COMPOSED AND NEVER SUMMED (Law 125).** The MANUAL is a dry-bag on the shore carrying `topic: 'boat'`, so `ladderFor` answers for it **with no new ladder branch** — the same found-content channel the Journal ([[D-068]]) and the far island's traces ([[D-126]]) already share, stopping at `conceptually-suspected` because reading is not doing. The HANDS are `navigationSeamanship` technique: a survivor who has built a raft, crossed open water and worked a hull has learned to read one. `boatUnderstanding` returns the **HIGHER** of the two, never the total — stacking would quietly make "do both" the optimal play and turn two paths back into one long one. Neither is mandatory, and the two sites are far apart so that finding one is not finding the other.

**THE SECOND ROUTE SHIPPED UNREACHABLE, AND THE TEST THAT LET IT THROUGH IS NAMED.** `boatSeamanshipTechnique` was **34**, on a basis line claiming the domain is trained by *"the raft, the crossing, the wreck and all three fishing methods"*. Fishing trains nothing — it never reaches `gatherNode` and has no `recordTrying` of its own ([[D-130]]). The domain's real producers, counted, are four call sites totalling **twelve events**; headroom decay puts 34 at **twenty-seven**, i.e. three full regrow cycles of the wreck AND the dive site. The route existed and could not be reached — **[[D-114]]'s zero-caller class, in my own drop**. Retuned to **14**, the eighth event. The assertion that let it through was *"between the floor and the ceiling"* — true of every number in that range, including ones the game cannot produce — and is replaced by one that **drives the shipped producers** (`recordTrying` for the raft and the crossing, then `gatherNode` over all six wreck parts) and crosses the threshold for real.

**SCOPE CAP, HELD AND ARGUED RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** No restoration mechanics, and deliberately **not even the first repair step the brief left open**. Every candidate — patch the hull, bail it, drag it clear — needs a stage between B0 and B1, a material cost and a visible change to the object. That is a restoration system with one entry in it, and a system with one entry is the hardest kind to remove later **because it looks finished**. So the boat does not change this drop; what changes is the SURVIVOR. `BoatStage` is the single-member type `'B0'` — a type already listing B1 and B2 would be a promise this drop cannot keep.

**NO SAVE MIGRATION, AND IT IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN LUCKY.** The boat adds no key to `GameState`; every function in `boat.ts` is a pure reading of state that already existed. `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays **29**, and a test asserts the key set is unchanged across reading the manual.

**THE ABSENCE SPEAKS, AND IT SPEAKS TO EVERYONE.** The handler read `route ?? boatWorkBlocker()`, which was exactly backwards: the route note exists only for a survivor who has come to understand the hull, so the fallback meant **the one person who now knows what she needs — and is therefore likeliest to reach for a repair verb — was the only one never told there isn't one**. The uninformed got the warning and the informed got a compliment. Both lines are said now, and two device checks hold it.

**THE VISUAL SENTENCE, CORRECTED BY A SCREENSHOT.** Her heading is the bearing to the far island, and the sight line from where she lies **leaves Spawn Island and never returns over it** — geometry, so it is provable in the unit suite. But she shipped as a **5.6 m box under a comment calling her "the largest made thing on this island"**, and a device frame from sixteen metres settled it: she read as a crate. Now **7.6 × 2.6 × 1.6 m** — an inshore boat a person could work alone. `runtime.meshSizeM` is new so that a claim about SIZE has an instrument other than a screenshot somebody remembers to take, and `BOAT 7b` would have gone red on the dinghy.

**`check-tune-mirror.mjs`'S VALUE HALF HAD NEVER RUN — 81 keys, zero comparisons.** Its pattern was built by interpolating the key into a **template literal**, so `\s` and `\d` were eaten before `RegExp` saw them and it compiled to `^s*<key>:s*([-d.]+)s*,`. That matches nothing in `tune.ts`, so `if (!m) continue` fired for every key and the loop **never compared one value to another**. It printed *"none drifted from src/data/tune.ts"* over 81 keys it never read. Found because this drop retuned a value, left the harness mirror stale at 34, and was told the mirror was clean. Fixed, and it now prints how many values it **actually compared** and fails outright at zero — because "none drifted" out of zero comparisons is the sentence it spent its whole life saying. Same family as [[D-097]]'s clause (e) and the two prior regexes that matched nothing: *a pattern that matches nothing fails quietly and looks like nothing happened.*

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `src/brain/boat.ts`, `BOAT`/`MANUALS` in `src/data/world.ts`, `buildBoat` in `src/body/island.ts`, and the repaired `tools/check-tune-mirror.mjs`; all shipped in this batch).

**DEFERRED, named rather than implied:** B1+ restoration entirely (its own future maritime slice — do not read this as close), any use of the boat as a vehicle or platform, fuel and engines as materials, and a second document beyond the one manual.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran.** purity (51 brain files), docs-integrity, tune-mirror (63 live refs; **78 of 81 values genuinely compared for the first time**), typecheck, production build. **UNIT: ran. 1215/1215 across 62 files**, including 24 new boat checks with **six planted defects each proven RED and restored green** — routes summed, a parts list in the informed reading, a route made mandatory, an unreachable tap gate, the 34 threshold restored, and the boat moved behind the hill. **DEVICE: ran. 441/450** on the served bundle, zero console errors, full output in `.smoke/run-drop4.log`. **DROP 4: 10/10**, including the reachability proof — walk in from out of range, tap the hull with a finger, read the answer off the screen — and the visual sentence. Of nine failures, `FIX 5` and `WRECK 6` are the carried known reds; **the other seven cascade from the storage box opening 6.3–6.9 s late, reproduced on a STASHED BASELINE containing none of this work**. That leg's own section cannot be bisected standalone — it crashes without a predecessor's fixture — so it is characterised rather than proven. **LIVE: witnessed.** `origin/main` `72f3f97` → `fbca4c7`, deploy green, and `deploy-witness.mjs` reports local = origin = **served `fbca4c7`**.*

**D-134 · 2026-08-08 — MARITIME 3d, ROOT-CAUSED. The check was never flaky, and the load theory was exactly backwards.**

**WHAT IT ACTUALLY WAS, MEASURED BEFORE IT WAS THEORISED.** The fixture starts the survivor at `swimSpentEnergy + 6` and waits for the swim to close that gap. At `swimEnergyDrainPerGameHour` (70/gh) over `realSecondsPerGameHour` (150), that gap takes **12.9 REAL SECONDS**. The poll was `60 × 120 ms` — **7.2 seconds of sleep**. It gave up roughly five seconds early, **deterministically**, on any machine where a `page.evaluate` round-trip cost less than 214 ms. So it PASSED on a loaded machine, where slow round-trips stretched sixty iterations past thirteen seconds, and FAILED on a healthy one. Three sessions read that as a load artifact — including two of mine ([[D-131]], [[D-132]]) — and **the causality was exactly inverted**. Nothing about it was intermittent; the machine was the variable, in the opposite direction to the one assumed.

**INSTRUMENTED, NOT DEDUCED — AND THE INSTRUMENT LIED TWICE FIRST.** The first probe wrote `localStorage` on a **live page**, whose autosave overwrote the fixture before the reload. The second navigated to a made-up path as a "dead page" — but `vite preview` serves the SPA fallback, so **that was a second live game**, ticking and persisting over the edit. Only the third, using the harness's own request interception to answer the blank path with a real empty document, showed the truth: the fixture lands perfectly (energy 17.83, gh 8.003, pos 0,150) and SPENT arrives at **13.2 s**. *A probe that cannot tell "the fixture failed" from "the game behaved" is the Vacuity Law applied to diagnostics* — [[D-066]] (a) turned on the instrument rather than the test — and it wasted two runs before the third asserted that its own fixture had landed.

**[[D-128]] DID NOT CAUSE THIS SO MUCH AS TRADE FOR IT.** It found the opposite failure — a headroom too small for the page-load window, so the survivor drained past SPENT into going-under before the first sample — and raised the headroom. That fix pushed the drain **beyond what the poll could wait for**. One timing bug swapped for its mirror image, **with nothing comparing the two numbers because they lived in different files**.

**AND [[D-128]]'S OWN FIX WAS NEVER SUFFICIENT FOR ITS OWN STATED CASE.** The comparison added here caught it on its first run: a 15-second boot — the figure D-128's note cites as the worst case — drains **7.0** energy, and the headroom was **6**. The original failure was made rarer and recorded as fixed. The fail-then-pass confirms both modes were live simultaneously: the shipped configuration failed GOING-UNDER on the demo run and LABOURING in the sweep.

**THE FIX.** Headroom **10**, which clears a 15 s boot with margin. The poll is a **60-second DEADLINE** rather than an iteration count — because a loop count silently encodes an assumption about round-trip cost, which is a property of the machine and not of the game. Both numbers live in `tune.ts`, where the TUNE-mirror check keeps the harness's copy honest, and the check reports the measured time-to-SPENT so a near-miss is visible **before** it becomes a miss.

**THE REGRESSION PROOF is what stops "believed fixed" and "actually fixed" diverging a third time.** Four checks in `tests/water.test.ts` compare the two numbers **directly**: the budget must exceed the swim by 2×, the headroom must exceed a worst-case boot, the window between the two failure modes must be comfortable, and **the harness must poll on a clock rather than a fixed loop** (a source assertion).
