They run in seconds, so the next retune of the swim cannot discover the mismatch two hours into a device sweep. The second of them found a live defect the moment it was written.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `swimSpentFixtureHeadroom` and `swimSpentPollBudgetSeconds` in `src/data/tune.ts`, the deadline poll in `tools/smoke.mjs`, and the four comparisons in `tests/water.test.ts`; shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran.** purity, docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, production build. **UNIT: ran. 1191 checks.** **DEVICE: ran**, and the fail-then-pass is against the **ACTUAL shipped mechanism** rather than a synthetic reproduction: the real harness section, on the real served build, reverted to the real `60 × 120 ms` poll and headroom of 6 — **RED**, `goal "You are going under."` — then restored — **GREEN**, `goal "You have nothing left."`. **31/31 on MARITIME three consecutive runs.** Full sweep **435/440 with MARITIME 3d PASSING**, which is the condition under which it previously passed for the wrong reason. Remaining reds are the established set — `FIX 5`, the absence-clock check, `F1`, `WRECK 6`. **`JUNK 4c` failed once and passes standalone twice**: a missed tap in a long run rather than a logic defect, and **its check cannot currently tell a missed tap from a wrong reading** — recorded rather than patched, since widening it would remove the only thing it asserts. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `72f3f97`.*

**D-133 · 2026-08-08 — RAIN & WET ESCALATION. Six stages, a hazard with nothing to face, and the fair-challenge grammar proven to generalise.**

**THE SECOND HAZARD FAMILY, AND THE POINT IS THAT IT IS SHAPED NOTHING LIKE THE FIRST.** The boar ([[D-109]]) established the fair-challenge grammar; what it could not establish is whether that grammar **generalises**, because a boar is a discrete opponent with a position — something to evade or fight. A storm is the opposite: **nothing to face, nowhere to dodge, no moment of contact**, arriving on a schedule nobody chose. If the same grammar works for both it is a contract. If it only worked for the boar it was a boar feature wearing a law's clothes.

**A CORRECTION TO THE BRIEF'S PREMISE, recorded rather than smoothed over.** This was scoped as reusing *"the existing rain flag and wet condition"*. The WET condition is real and is reused exactly. **THERE WAS NO RAIN FLAG.** `rain` existed only as a threat a refuge ANSWERS — `RefugeProfile.rain`, `caveRainAnswered: 1.0` — in a world where nothing had ever rained. Drop 3 built the cave's best statistic and no question for it to answer. So this does not reuse a rain flag; **it supplies the question half the vulnerability map ([[D-069]]) was already written to answer.**

**SIX STAGES, AND THE SIXTH IS NOT A TIMER.** PRECURSOR and WATCH cost **exactly nothing** and are deliberately the longest part of the run-up — reading the sky has to be worth more than reacting to rain. COMMITTED and IMPACT wet you, the second far harder than the first. AFTERMATH stops the rain; the cost is what is already on you. **CHANGED WORLD is not a stage, because a changed world does not end**: the storm worsens the two places rain attacks — the covering it falls on and the footing water stands around — and **leaves the LASHING alone, because this hazard is rain and not a cyclone**.

**NO PARALLEL THERMAL SYSTEM.** Rain never touches warmth. It raises `wet`, and `wet` reaches warmth through the shipped evaporative term — cashing the Maritime Slice's own note ([[D-121]]) that immersion *"arrives at warmth through `thermalWetLoss` and the existing heat balance, exactly like rain would"*. What a roof changes is **how wet you get, not what being wet costs**, which is why `netHeatFlowPerGameHour` needed no edit.

**THE TIE TO ENTROPY & MAINTENANCE IS ONE NUMBER.** `advanceStorm` passes `activeProfile`'s **already-degraded** refuge to the wetting rate, so a thinned thatch arrives as a smaller rain answer and `storm.ts` never learns that maintenance exists ([[D-132]]). Two systems meeting without either re-deriving the other — which is how the refuge report became "the liar" the first time somebody did.

**FIVE DEFECTS, THREE OF THEM MINE AND STRUCTURAL.** (1) `clearOnAbsence` scheduled the next storm off the **PRE-absence clock**, because it inherited `afterAbsence`'s position before `reconcile`: four hours away left the next storm overdue and it fired on the first tick back, so **the function written to stop a survivor walking into a storm they never stood in was delivering one**. Split across the span — ending before, rescheduling after. (2) **MY HEADLINE ASYMMETRY TEST WAS PASSING FOR THE WRONG REASON**: the "exposed" survivor stood at (400, 400) — 565 m out to sea, **SWIMMING**, pinned at `wetMax` before a drop fell, so every comparison against them measured immersion rather than weather. The device harness found it (`wet 100.0 → 100.0`), which is precisely the reachability gap [[D-090]] names. (3) `stormWetGainPerGameHour` **was set against nothing**: 55/gh chosen against the pond's 240 and against no drying rate at all — but `wetDecayPerGameHourSheltered` is 60/gh, so **any survivor at their shelter dried faster than the hardest rain could wet them**. Sound roof and holed roof both read +0.00 and the tie this stage exists to prove could not manifest. Now 100/gh, set against BOTH decay rates. And two of tuning: `shelterRainAnswered` at 0.85 left the cave a fifteen-point edge over a lean-to, erasing the one thing a cave is unambiguously better at in the very stage that gives it a question — set to 0.55 **by the cave rather than by the rain**; and `stormCommittedIntensity` at 0.35 netted +4.25/gh against drying, so *"the first of it comes down, fat and cold"* delivered a faint dampening.

**ONE PLANTED DEFECT STAYED GREEN, WHICH MEANT A TEST WAS NOT TESTING ITS OWN CLAIM.** The "exactly once" aftermath check inspected a flag on a path it never exercised twice. Rewritten to measure the **consequence** against a no-storm control, since the shelter weathers whether or not it is raining. [[D-066]] (a), caught by planting rather than by reading.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `src/brain/storm.ts` with `advanceStorm` on `Session`'s online tick; shipped in this batch).

**DEFERRED, named rather than implied:** the full weather-instrument and forecast system (no barometer, no storm-glass, no confidence model — nothing here predicts anything a survivor cannot see), wildfire and every other hazard family, the full hazard tables (cyclones, landslides, tsunami), rain as a world-visual (no particles, no sky change — the cues are words), and compound events firing simultaneously.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity, docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1187 checks, 32 new**, with **eight planted defects each proven red and restored** — offline rain, a costed warning stage, a roof that keeps nothing off, an aftermath paid every tick, rain attacking the wind's place, the maintenance tie cut, a lean-to as good as a cave, and the reschedule back on the wrong side. **DEVICE: ran. 436/440 with all 11 STORM checks green**, including both warnings reaching the screen in **different words through real stage TRANSITIONS**, and a holed roof measurably wetter than a sound one over the same span (+0.84 against +0.00). The four reds are pre-existing, and **`MARITIME 3d` was BISECTED rather than assumed**: it fails identically at the shipped [[D-132]] commit, standalone, at 2.30 GB free — so it is not load and not this slice. That bisection is what [[D-134]] then root-caused. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `726b3e8`.*

**D-132 · 2026-08-08 — ENTROPY & MAINTENANCE. Three named places, and the end of one bar.**

**THE DECAY HALF ONLY, ON ONE STRUCTURE**, which is where the gap dossier says to start. Spatial logistics is **DEFERRED WHOLE**: the homestead has too few placed structures to generate real hauling, and building a transport weave now would ship a system with nothing to move.

**WHAT THIS REPLACES IS A SHAPE, NOT A NUMBER.** The shelter has carried `durability: 0–100` since C05 — one bar, one rate, telling a survivor their home is at 63% of something. The dossier rejects that outright in favour of *"specific, named defects"* and a maintenance-debt model where *"deferred work changes cues and confidence, not a hidden damage-over-time tick"*. Both halves are built here.

**SHELTER LAW 4 — "A WALL IS A LOAD PATH, NOT A HIT-POINT TOTAL" — is what made it possible rather than aspirational**, because the vulnerability map ([[D-069]]) already worked that way. Drop 3 stopped asking *"how good is this shelter"* and started answering four separate questions. So a defect does not reduce a quality score; **it attacks ONE PLACE, and the place decides which answer gets worse**: the **LASHING** (the windward cross-brace) works loose over NIGHTS and takes WIND — and nothing else, because a racked frame is still a roof; the **THATCH** thins with WEATHERING and takes COLD — because a thin roof still breaks a gust; the **FOOTING** rots by the SITE — a shelter pitched on wet sand tells you so within a day, one in the scrub never raises it — and takes **BOTH**, because everything above it is standing on it. *That is the load path, and it is the reason the model is per-location rather than per-threat.* Three drivers, none invented: `timeOfDay().isNight`, elapsed game hours, and `waterDepthAt` at the shelter's own coordinates — the third making SITING a decision **after the fact**, arriving at a structure placed days ago.

**MAINTENANCE DEBT IS TRIPS, NOT A HIDDEN TICK.** Deferring accrues no secret number. It changes what the player is **told** — a named cue per stage, and a report that says what the shelter holds NOW against what it would hold sound, *which gap IS the debt stated* — and it changes the **work**: one wood mends ONE named place. `defectMendPerWood` is exactly `defectShowingAt`, so a showing defect clears in one visit and a failing one always takes two. **That equality is the constant being grounded rather than guessed**: written first as a flat 0.45, where a defect sitting on the failing threshold went straight to sound in one visit — the comment claimed two and the number delivered one, and its own test caught it.

**EXTENDED, NOT REPLACED.** `durability` is untouched and its C05/[[D-098]] tests pass unchanged; the named places sit alongside it and are spent first. One real refusal removed on the way: a shelter at FULL durability with a parted lashing previously **could not be mended at all**, because `canRepairStructure` gated on the bar alone.

**[[D-011]], AND STRICTER THAN THE BAR BESIDE IT.** `Session.advanceDefects` is the only thing that worsens a defect and it runs on the online tick alone — where `reconcile` **does** decay `durability` across an absence. The difference is deliberate: a defect degrades a **refuge answer**, so one that worsened offline would mean returning to a measurably colder shelter for having been away. The old bar predates the vulnerability map and only paused a flat bonus; this reaches the heat balance, so it gets the boar's treatment.

**THREE DEFECTS THE DEVICE FOUND, each after fixing the last.** The hold fixture stood **ON** the shelter, so the ray left the camera inside the frame and struck the ground behind it — `approach()`'s own lesson ([[D-127]]), relearned. And the D-011 check billed page-boot ticks to the absence **twice** before the bound was scaled off the absence's own game hours: 96.3 game hours away grew the lashing by 0.0067 against a floor of ~0.19 had it counted.

**SUBSTRATE, NOT THE LEDGER.** v0.14 L3's ratified pressure-transfer ledger needs somewhere to hang an entry: a named place, a named state, a named cause. `durability: 63` could never have carried one; three named defects at three named locations can. **The ledger itself is NOT built here and nothing pretends to be it.**

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `src/brain/upkeep.ts` with `advanceDefects` on `Session`'s online tick, composed into `activeProfile`; shipped in this batch).

**DEFERRED, named rather than implied:** the full ten-stage lifecycle (design / commission / inspect / modify / mothball / decommission / recover all absent — this is decay and repair only), spatial logistics and hauling ENTIRELY, the pressure-transfer ledger itself, named defects on any other structure (the storage crate keeps the plain bar), and inspection as a verb distinct from mending.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity, docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1154 checks, 33 new**, with **seven planted defects each proven red and restored** — offline decay, the load path collapsing back into one number, a report quoting a shelter that no longer exists, an answer driven below open ground, a percentage in the prose, wood going into the bar while the footing rots, and the full-durability refusal returning. **DEVICE: ran. 425/429 with all 11 UPKEEP checks green**, including both visible cues read off the **RENDERED meshes** rather than the state behind them. The four reds are the established set and none is new. Worth recording that `REGRESSION fell`, `F3` and `MARITIME 3d` all **PASSED** this run at 1.92 GB free having failed the previous sweep at 1.10 GB — which corroborated [[D-116]]'s ~2 GB condition as the cause rather than the code. **That corroboration was later shown to be wrong for `MARITIME 3d` specifically** — see [[D-134]], where the causality proved to be inverted. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `2cf5beb`.*

**D-131 · 2026-08-07 — THE JUNK & FLAVOUR CATALOGUE (Ch.3), FIRST REPRESENTATIVES. Six objects that do nothing, on purpose.**

**FIRST REPRESENTATIVES, NOT THE FULL CATALOGUE, and that is the scope rather than an apology**: six authored objects, real and complete, spread across the three zones that are actually built. The rest is future work and is named as such **in the tests**, so `expect(JUNK_SITES.length).toBe(6)` reads as a statement about this pass rather than a ceiling on the idea.

**NO SPEC DOCUMENT EXISTS IN THE REPO for Ch.3**, so this is scoped from established precedent — the far island's own traces ([[D-126]]), the world-truth visual law, and the affordance layer — and **is named as that rather than presented as a transcription**. On SPAWN ISLAND: a rusted iron head half in the sand with no handle left; and notches cut into a trunk in rows of five, the last row unfinished. At THE WRECK: a figure whittled from a broom handle, jammed in a split seam; and a mess tin with a name scratched inside and a second hand underneath it. On THE FAR ISLAND: a plank end with square holes at even spacing; and a pair of boots set side by side above the tideline, laces tucked in.

**HALF HOLD A NOTE AND HALF DO NOT, and the difference is mechanical rather than cosmetic.** A **noted** object is READ, once, through exactly the found-content channel ([[D-068]]) the Journal and the far island's traces already share — no second reading system, which the brief forbade and which this project has paid for twice before. An **unnoted** object is INSPECTED, always: nothing to consume, so it records nothing, changes nothing, and answers the hundredth tap as it answered the first. What it gives back is the affordance layer's own vocabulary — **observable properties and open questions, never a named answer**.

**SAME TYPE, SEPARATE CATALOGUE.** `TRACE_SITES` stays exactly the far island's three, because that island's own tests and harness section are ABOUT those three and counting them is a real assertion. Junk gets its own array and shares every mechanism through `traces.ts` — **one lookup over both**, because two code paths is how a tap on junk comes to resolve differently from a tap on a trace.

**INERT BY CONSTRUCTION, and the brief's third item answered rather than sidestepped.** A rusted iron head is, in fiction, **METAL** — and `metal` is wreck-era, the material whose entire point is that a fresh castaway cannot obtain one without crossing ([[D-124]]). Junk that handed one over on the spawn beach would **silently undo the Wreck slice's central claim through a channel nobody was watching**. So the fiction and the mechanics are made to agree instead: the rust goes all the way through, everything yields `{}`, and a test walks every recipe slot against everything junk could produce. **That walk is proven non-vacuous** by running the same code over the traces, which DO leave goods, and finding a real match there — [[D-066]] (a) applied to an emptiness proof.

**NOTHING IS SILENT.** Every unnoted object carries observations a survivor could actually make, and a check sweeps the whole catalogue **with a real finger** to prove each one answers. A decorative object that looks interactable and says nothing is exactly what the world-truth law forbids, and **it is the defect a flavour pass ships by accident**.

**ONE REAL FIX CARRIED IN:** the trace builder drew at `groundHeight`, which would have put the two wreck objects on a seabed eight metres down — invisible and unpickable. `surfaceHeightAt` is **[[D-124]]'s own fix applied where it was always going to be needed next**, and it changes nothing on land.

**NO SAVE MIGRATION, and that is the reuse paying off rather than an omission**: junk adds no state. `traces.read` is a list of ids that already exists and already migrates; an old save has simply not read these yet.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `JUNK_SITES` in `src/data/world.ts` with `junkAffordanceOf` and the unified `allSites()` lookup in `src/brain/traces.ts`; shipped in this batch).

**DEFERRED, named rather than implied:** the rest of the catalogue; no junk that can be picked up or carried, none that decays, none in the cave or underwater, and no second note-bearing object per site.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity, docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1121 checks, 23 new**, with **six planted defects each proven red and restored** — a consumable tool head, metal on the spawn beach, a silent prop, prose that instructs, junk moving the knowledge ladder, and the two catalogues merging. **DEVICE: ran. 413/418 with all 21 JUNK checks green**, including every one of the six **projecting onto a real screen and answering a real tap**. The five reds are pre-existing or measured load artifacts, each settled by running it rather than arguing it: `FIX 5` and `WRECK 6` were baseline-confirmed in earlier sessions; the absence-clock check passes standalone on both builds; `F3 — out of range` and `MARITIME 3d` were new to this run and **both passed standalone on this build** (9/9 and 31/31), with the sweep running at 1.10 GB free against [[D-116]]'s recorded ~2 GB condition. **That load attribution was later proven wrong for `MARITIME 3d`** — see [[D-134]]. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `2cb009e`.*

**D-130 · 2026-08-07 — FISHING. Three methods, one fish, and four defects only a device could find.**

**THREE METHODS, AND EACH IS THE WORST OPTION IN SOMEBODY'S SITUATION.** That is the whole design rail — *a method that is strictly better than another is a method the other two are never chosen over* — and each spends something different. **HANDLINE** spends your ATTENTION: two fibre and a barb, and the line is HELD, so walking out of reach loses the cast. One fish, and gentlest on the water. It is the `fish` verb that has sat on the pond's circle since Slice 2 saying *"nothing yet"*, and the `tools.fishingLine` boolean that has been in the save since v11 **with no recipe anywhere able to set it**. **NET** spends a PLACE: it fishes while your hands are busy and only while you stay within `netTendRadiusM`, holds nothing until it has soaked (~53 real seconds of dead window), and hauling LIFTS it so setting it again is a fresh decision. Best yield in the game, worst answer to *"I am hungry now"*. **SPEAR** spends the SITE: no new tool — this is Drop 1's spear and Drop 1's thrust ([[D-109]]) given water to aim at, which is exactly why it cost a fraction of the other two. Instant, best per hit, and **a MISS SCARES THE SHOAL ANYWAY**, so the worst thrower is hardest on the water, and that is the whole of its skill expression. The roll is a parameter, never a `Math.random()` in the brain.

**POPULATION IS TWO STATES AND NOT ONE LINE OF NEW MACHINERY.** A spot is a `WoodNode`; `available` **is** present/locally-depleted; `pool` is the quarry's own field ([[D-070]]); it comes back through `regrowGameHoursFor` like every bush ([[D-051]]). What the METHOD chooses is **how fast the water empties** — six catches by line, three attempts by spear, two hauls by net.

**[[D-011]]:** `Session.advanceFishing` runs on the online tick and nowhere else. The argument is stronger here than in any stage before it — no method has a harm term at all, so an absence cannot reach one. What the structure buys is that **a net cannot fill while the game is closed**.

**FOUR DEFECTS THE DEVICE FOUND, each after fixing the last.** A fishery is the first node kind that **OUTLIVES ITS OWN DEPLETION**, and three separate layers refused to handle that. (1) The shore sites were authored **ON DRY SAND** — the harness read `fp-north 0.00 m`; they are now 0.61 m and 1.54 m of real wading water, with the reef at 5.87 m. (2) `pendingTarget` returned null for a spent node, so a tap on fished-out water was dropped **before the fail-loud branch could speak** — a branch whose own comment claimed it had been placed to prevent exactly that. (3) `pickNode` refused it one layer higher still. (4) `fishRingSpent` was chosen at **BUILD time** and never again, so a site fished out during play kept its pale ring; the palette entry had zero callers after creation — [[D-114]]'s shape again.

**AND FOUR IN SHIPPED CODE, found while wiring fish into "the existing food chain":** meat could never be eaten (every constant shipped, `Food` never included it); food rotted offline against `gameHoursElapsed`; the line was unreachable as a one-slot recipe because Try-Combining needs two to four materials; and the pond's Fish verb and its fishing spot **disagreed about what "at the pond" means**.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `src/brain/fishing.ts` with `advanceFishing` on `Session`'s online tick, and `fishingspot` nodes in `src/data/world.ts`; shipped in this batch).

**DEFERRED, named rather than implied:** boat-fishing (the raft is not a platform), multiple fish species, cooking (still the NEXT discovery, exactly as Drop 1 left it), bait, per-unit spoilage, and any population model richer than two states — no stock counts, no migration, no carrying capacity.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity, docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1098 checks, 45 new**, with **seven planted defects each proven red and restored**. **DEVICE: ran. 401/406 with all 18 FISH checks green** — including **the three reachability proofs the brief made mandatory, each on its own route**: the line crafted by a real Build-panel tap and cast by a real tap on the water; the net set and hauled through real circle segments after paying its real soak; and the spear striking for a survivor who crafted NOTHING. The five reds are pre-existing and each is evidenced rather than assumed: `FIX 5`, `WRECK 6` and the absence-clock check were confirmed failing on a stashed baseline; `REGRESSION — fell → gather → fell` is demonstrably intermittent at baseline (failed one pre-fishing sweep, passed the next on the same commit); and `F1` compares a counter captured inside its own section against a value read **fourteen sections later**. **NOTE ON THE PRECEDING COMMIT:** `ad444fb` is an explicit **WIP — built and unit-green, NOT device-verified and NOT shipped**, kept in history rather than squashed so the gap between "green" and "witnessed" stays legible. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `179b1f2`.*

**D-129 · 2026-08-07 — THE UNDERWATER SLICE. One site, seven metres down, and two [[D-011]] breaches only a device could find.**

**THE SEA WAS A PLACE YOU COULD BE ON. IT IS NOW A PLACE YOU CAN BE IN.** Depth is read from `waterDepthAt` — the same function the shore, the wading band and the swim rule already derive from ([[D-121]]). Cold comes through `netHeatFlowPerGameHour`'s existing evaporative term, scaled by depth: **one new INPUT to the shipped heat balance, not a second opinion about heat**. Salvage is ordinary `gatherNode` on ordinary nodes. Development goes through `trainingStimulus`, exactly as swimming does.

**WHAT IT ADDS IS THE AIR BUDGET, and that is the whole of the new mechanic.** A breath at the site's real 7.0 m depth is **~15 real seconds**: long enough to descend, work one thing and come up; short enough that a second target is a decision. **Surfacing in time is the skill.** `breathWaterConfidence` gains its second producer and grows a diver's breath by a bounded 45% — §12's *"does not extend human physiology without limit"*.

**THE FIVE STAGES, in this project's own grammar:** surfaced / holding / burning / failing / blacking-out. **`holding` is SILENT**, by the rule that keeps `swimming` and a sound hull quiet — two warnings only work because the stage before them says nothing. Disorientation is **mechanical, not a camera trick**: a failing diver loses coordination, expressed through the multiplier the water already owns. The honest-systems rail forbids misleading a player about real state, and **a spun camera is exactly that**.

**[[D-011]], STRUCTURALLY.** `Session.advanceDive` is the only function that lowers air and it runs on the online tick alone; `reconcile` has **no elapsed-time term on the breath at all**. An absence does not merely decline to spend one — it brings the diver **UP with a full one**, because absence making things better is legal where absence making them worse never is.

**TWO DEFECTS THE DEVICE FOUND, AND ONLY THE DEVICE COULD.** (1) `surfaceOnAbsence` was in `reconcile` — which `Session.tick` calls **every frame** with a 16 ms span. It surfaced divers sixty times a second, **so no dive could begin**. Every brain test passed throughout: every one called `reconcile` with a LONG span and **never once with a frame**. The harness read `probe node:dv1, outcome node:dv1, submerged false`. (2) **The fix reached one of the two absence paths.** `Session.start` folds in the time a RELOAD was away, `Session.resume` the time a BACKGROUNDED TAB was away — **the same event to a player, two call sites to me**. The harness reloads, and came back with a survivor at health 0.000, still submerged, **drowned across four hours of not playing**. D-011 breached by a missing line. Both now go through one `afterAbsence`, which runs **BEFORE** the span so the depth chill can never reach an offline hour.

**AND ONE ZERO-CALLER, CAUGHT BEFORE IT SHIPPED:** `submergedDepthM` existed on the context, the multiplier existed in the balance, and nothing in the game ever passed it — *"deeper is colder" was a field, a formula and no wire*. **Fifth instance of [[D-114]]'s shape.** It now has a caller and a test that fails when the wire is cut.

**ALSO FIXED: `--list` CRASHED ON EVERY INVOCATION** since the `--section` filter shipped ([[D-126]]) — it assigned to `names`, one of the 62 hoisted cross-section bindings, and hit the temporal dead zone **in the one path that exits before the declaration**, which the full sweep never takes.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `src/brain/dive.ts` with `advanceDive`/`afterAbsence` on `Session`, and `DIVE_SITE`/`DIVE_PARTS` in `src/data/world.ts`; shipped in this batch).

**DEFERRED, named rather than implied:** one site, not a zone system. No second dive site, no depth tiers below 7 m, no underwater flora or fauna, **no visual treatment of being submerged** (no colour grade, no caustics, no surface-from-below), no held-breath UI beyond the goal line's words, no equipment that extends a breath, and no buoyancy — a diver moves as a swimmer does, more slowly.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity, docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1053 checks, 45 new**, fail-then-pass proven on **five planted defects** (the surfacing regression, an absence air drain, a narrating `holding`, harm one stage early, and the cut depth-chill wire). **DEVICE: ran. 384/388 with all 12 UNDER checks green** — a real tap on a mesh 7 m down, both warnings read off the page in words, harm only after both, a real press of the real Surface button, real salvage, and **a four-hour absence begun mid-drowning that comes back alive, up and breathing**. The four reds are pre-existing: `FIX 5` and `WRECK 6` confirmed failing on a stashed baseline, the absence-clock check confirmed passing standalone on both builds, and SLICE 2C's tab leak is a registered measured-intermittent. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `be40b9f`.*

**D-128 · 2026-08-06 — MARITIME 3d FIXED ON TIMING AND SETUP, and the aim path stops guessing at heights.**

**MY FIRST FIX WAS WRONG AND THE SECTION SAID SO.** I assumed the ~2 s swim before the read was draining the reserve, and made it read at the first true moment. Still red — and **going-under on the VERY FIRST poll**, which killed that theory outright. The real cause: `editSave` stamps both clocks and **then** reloads, so the whole page-load window (5–15 s here) arrives as elapsed time on the first online tick. At 70 energy per game hour, multiplied by whatever load earlier sections left in the pack, a survivor started at 10 energy **was already at zero before anything could read them**. The check was sampling a corpse and reporting the wrong sentence.

**FIXED ON SETUP, NEVER ON THE THRESHOLD.** The stage boundaries are correct and MARITIME 3b/3c prove the one below it, so **nothing about the assertion moved**. What changed: start ABOVE the boundary with headroom the boot window cannot eat, empty the pack to remove the load multiplier as a variable, and poll to the first genuinely-SPENT reading, capturing goal line and health **in one `evaluate`** so the two cannot describe different instants.

**THE AUDIT THE BRIEF ASKED FOR — and the answer is that the aim path was NOT general.** `projectToScreen` takes X and Z and **derives** height as `surfaceHeightAt + 0.4`. That is a guess. It is right for exactly two cases — standing on the ground, and floating at the surface — and **it had already been wrong twice**: [[D-124]], where the wreck parts float and it aimed at the SEABED 7 m under them; and [[D-127]], where the fire ring is 0.29 m tall and it aimed 0.4 m OVER it. Each time the answer was a local patch — move the derived height, then bolt on proximity forgiveness — and **neither made it general**. Anything below the surface breaks it by construction, which is every target underwater would add. So there is now **one path that does not guess**: `screenOfMesh` projects the mesh's own drawn world centre, so underwater, mid-air or flat on the sand **it aims at the object because it asks the object**. `tapMesh` uses it. The derived-height path stays for callers that only have a ground position, and is now **the special case rather than the rule**.

**THE UNDERWATER ITEM WAS DELIBERATELY NOT STARTED.** It needs a depth/air model, a five-stage telegraph, an authored site with salvage, its own D-011 property tests, a migration, body rendering and a harness section. There was room to WRITE a brain layer and not to give it a player path and verify it — and **a brain-only underwater with no way in is exactly the failure [[D-075]] and standing hazard 4 are named after**. Half-building it is worse than not starting. Its prerequisite is shipped: underwater targets are testable at all now, and were not before. It landed as [[D-129]].

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `runtime.screenOfMesh` in `src/body/game.ts` with `tapMesh` in `tools/smoke.mjs`, plus the MARITIME 3d fixture; shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity, docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1008/1008 across 56 files.** **DEVICE: ran. 369/376, MARITIME 3d PASS, FAR 10/10.** Seven graded failures, none from this pass: `FIX 5` and `WRECK 6` are carried and named; the absence-clock, `F1` counter and a `net::ERR` DNS blip have all appeared before. **Two were NEW and were ATTRIBUTED RATHER THAN ASSUMED** — the quarry setup and [[D-059]]'s carry-weight drain, both in sections this pass never touched and both running EARLIER in the sweep than anything changed; D-059 re-ran with `--only` and passed clean at exit 0 including the exact failing check, so it is timing inside a long continuous run, **measured (1.62 GB free, 37% CPU) rather than blamed on load**. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `e7250c4`. **THIS FIX WAS LATER SHOWN INSUFFICIENT FOR ITS OWN STATED CASE** — the headroom of 6 does not cover the 15-second boot this entry cites as worst case, and the raised headroom pushed the drain past what the poll could wait for. Root-caused and closed at [[D-134]].*

**D-127 · 2026-08-06 — THE FAR ISLAND IS WITNESSED. FAR 10/10, and two defects only a device could find.**

**THE DEVICE LEG [[D-126]] OWED.** The far island's body was typechecked and never seen — the same gap the Wreck carried at [[D-124]]. **FAR ISLAND is now 10/10 both in isolation and in the full sweep**: real land at 7.50 m, the castaway standing ON it, a real shore (centre 7.5 m, open water −8.0 m past the rim), all three traces present, **a REAL tap reading one**, the rung moving `physically-possible → conceptually-suspected` **and no further**, a cache handing over metal and medicine **exactly once**, and [[D-011]] holding across a four-hour absence.

**THE `--section` FILTER EARNED ITSELF ON ITS FIRST REAL USE:** 0.8–4.9 min per iteration against ~115. **Seven iterations happened inside the time a single full sweep takes.**

**TWO REAL DEFECTS THE UNIT SUITE STRUCTURALLY COULD NOT SEE.** (1) **THE TRACE TAP HAD ZERO CALLERS.** The pending kind existed, `pendingTarget` could walk to a trace, `actOnArrival` could read one — **and nothing created the pending.** `worldCandidateAt` resolves by proximity and returns a BARE KIND STRING, so it had nowhere to put an id and never knew traces existed. Every tap on a cairn fell through to *"empty ground"*. Exactly `craftSpear`'s shape ([[D-114]]): type, handler and verb all present, **the one line reaching them missing** — and invisible to unit tests because every one calls `readTrace` directly, which is the reachability gap [[D-090]] names. Fixed with `pickTrace`, a mesh ray like the boar's, wired into **BOTH** the real tap and the fidelity probe so they cannot diverge. (2) **FLAT OBJECTS WERE UNTAPPABLE.** Taps aim at ground +0.4 m; the fire ring is a torus 0.29 m tall, so the ray **flew over it** into the terrain. The cache (0.62 m) was hit; the ring never was. Fixed with `traceTapRadiusM` proximity forgiveness — the raft's own rule applied to ground-level objects — **rather than making the ring stand up so the input model could find it.**

**TWO WERE MINE, NOT THE PRODUCT'S.** `approach()` walks **ONTO** a small object, putting its projected point under the survivor's own body where `worldPick` correctly refuses it; the probe now stands back, as a player does. And **`FAR 5b` passed alone and failed in the full sweep** — *"raft rung demonstrated → demonstrated"* — because the MARITIME section builds a raft earlier, so the survivor already stood at `demonstrated` and the ladder **rightly refused to LOWER them** for reading a note. The product was right; **the fixture measured what rung they were AT when the claim is what the TRACE GRANTS.** Now forced to the floor, per [[D-055]]'s *"journal forced empty first rather than assumed"*. **That one appeared ONLY in the full sweep: the filter accelerates iteration; it does not replace the confirming pass.**

**`MARITIME 3d` IS NEWLY RED AND ATTRIBUTED RATHER THAN WAVED THROUGH.** It reproduces with `--only=MARITIME`, so it is not caused by this pass. The detail names the mechanism — goal *"You are going under."*, health 97.71 — the swimmer drains at 70/gh and crosses PAST spent into going-under before the check samples the line. **Same defect class as the arrival-profile boot-timing bug fixed the same day** ([[D-126]]): *a threshold read against a value that is still moving.* Recorded, not patched blind; carried to [[D-128]] and finally root-caused at [[D-134]].

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `pickTrace` in `src/body/game.ts` and `traceTapRadiusM` in `src/data/tune.ts`; shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity, docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build, all green. **UNIT: ran. 1008/1008 across 56 files.** **DEVICE: ran. Full sweep 370/376, FAR 10/10.** Six graded failures, none from this pass: `FIX 5` and `WRECK 6` are carried and named; the absence-clock and `F1` counter have both appeared before; a `net::ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` is a DNS blip. **The underwater item was NOT STARTED** — no half-built state, no partial migration, no orphaned types. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `58fda7e`.*

**D-126 · 2026-08-06 — THE FAR ISLAND, TRACES-FIRST. The arrival-profile boot-timing defect closed, and the harness gets a `--section` filter.**

**THE ARRIVAL-PROFILE BOOT-TIMING DEFECT ([[D-116]]/[[D-120]]) — one fix, both checks, device-confirmed.** Both read health against a fixed ±1.5 band **while health CLIMBS from boot**, so a slow boot drifted them out of a band that was never wrong about the profile, only about the clock. The fix **stops pretending the target is still**: the upper bound is the arrival value plus the regen ACTUALLY EARNED, from `gameHoursElapsed − survivorStartedAtGameHours`, which the state knows exactly. **Asymmetric on purpose** — the lower bound stays tight, because a body BELOW its arrival profile is a real defect and must still fail; widening to ±5 would have hidden the thing the check exists to catch and drifted again on a slower machine. Both now pass **and print their own arithmetic**: *"arrival 65, +0.08 regen earned in 0.021 gh"*.

**THE FAR ISLAND, and the whole of it falls out of what already shipped.** It exists as real land at ~424 m, beyond the wreck on the same bearing, so the three things visible from the spawn beach line up into one story: **the surf you can reach, the wreck you can paddle to, and the shape past it that is clearly not weather.** Its shore cost nothing to build — `waterDepthAt`, `isDryLand`, `waterZoneAt` and `surfaceHeightAt` all derive from `groundHeight` ([[D-121]]), so raising land there gave the island a real waterline, wading band and walkable ground **with no new rules**; a test walks a radial out from its centre and finds dry → wading → swimming in order. *That is the Maritime Slice's design paying for itself.* It is **RAFT-ONLY, the first place in the game that is**: ~296 m of open water against a full reserve that buys ~218 m of swimming, **asserted as arithmetic so a later tune cannot quietly make it swimmable**.

**THREE TRACES, through the SHIPPED found-content channel at exactly the Survivor's Journal's rung** ([[D-068]]): `conceptually-suspected`, **never `demonstrated`**. A stranger's note is a stranger's hands — it says the thing is possible and that there is a wrong way to do it, and stops. Granting `demonstrated` from a note would make the writer's work transferable, which is the inheritance [[D-105]] forbids, **and a stranger is no more entitled to hand it over than a predecessor is.** Having READ one is personal and dies with the survivor; the notes stay out there. Left goods are handed over **ONCE** — a thing a person left, not a node.

**[[D-011]] needs almost no argument here and gets a property test anyway:** a trace has no rate, no timer, no decay and no threat. It does not act; it only ever answers being read. `reconcile` never touches trace state and **there is nothing for it to touch** — 1200 samples across bodies on and around the island.

**THE ISLAND GETS ITS OWN MESH**, and that is a decision rather than a convenience: home's terrain spans about ±152 m, and widening it to reach 420 m would have meant sampling home's beach every 11.7 m instead of 3.6. A second static frozen mesh keeps home exactly as it is and **samples the SAME `groundHeight`, so picture and rule cannot drift.**

**THE HARNESS GETS A `--section` FILTER** in the same batch: iterate on one section without re-witnessing 350. It carries its own guard — a filtered run **refuses to print a comparable pass count**, naming itself FILTERED and listing the skipped sections, because the failure that would matter is somebody pasting a full-run number from a run that executed one section. It earned itself immediately at [[D-127]], and its `--list` path crashed on every invocation until [[D-129]].

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `FAR_ISLAND` and `TRACE_SITES` in `src/data/world.ts`, `src/brain/traces.ts`, `buildFarIsland` in `src/body/island.ts`, and the `--section` filter in `tools/smoke.mjs`; shipped in this batch).

**DEFERRED, PLAINLY — real work, not omissions:** the island's own geology, forage and fresh water; harvestable nodes of any kind; structures to use or repair; weather or hazards of its own; more than three sites, or anything resembling a settlement; and any live-player presence. **It is a place you visit and read. It cannot sustain you and it is not a second home.**

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran** — purity, docs-integrity, tune-mirror, typecheck, build. **UNIT: ran. 1008/1008 across 56 files**, with **three planted mutations** — traces granting `demonstrated`, traces surviving death, the island drifting into swimming range — **all three caught by six distinct assertions**. **DEVICE: ran. 363/366** on a verified-served bundle, no crash, **the same three graded failures as the pre-existing baseline** (SLICE 2C tab leak, `FIX 5`, `WRECK 6`) and no new ones. **THE FAR ISLAND HAS NO DEVICE SECTION OF ITS OWN, and that is named as owed rather than implied** — every claim above about the island's body is typechecked and unwitnessed. Closed one day later at [[D-127]]. Save schema **v24 → v25**; the island and its sites are terrain and content, stored in no save, so they simply appear, and `traces` migrates in **EMPTY**, because crossing 296 m to read a stranger's note is an achievement, not a fact about the world. **LIVE: witnessed** — pushed and served as `bb52c52`.*

**D-125 · 2026-08-05 — THE WRECK IS WITNESSED. 363/366, the projection fix confirmed, and a check left RED on purpose.**

**ONE DIAGNOSED CHANGE CLOSED ALL FIVE.** [[D-124]]'s device leg reported the wreck unworkable; the wreck was fine and the INSTRUMENT was wrong. `runtime.projectToScreen` aimed at `groundHeight(x, z) + 0.4` — the terrain — so at the wreck it returned a point ~7 m below hull parts that float at the waterline, and every aimed tap landed in open water. `surfaceHeightAt(x, z) = max(terrain, seaLevel)` replaces it. **All five previously-vacuous checks now pass on device**: `WRECK 2` works a real hull part and gains metal, `2b` charges 12.2 energy through the shipped resolver, `2c` trains seamanship 8.80 → 10.03, `2d` shifts the hull 0 → 17.98.

**IT LIVES IN THE PURE DATA LAYER, and that is the durable half.** The body has no unit coverage by construction, which is exactly how this rule survived two full device passes unnoticed. As a pure function of the terrain it is provable in milliseconds, and the fail-then-pass is exact without a branch checkout: the old behaviour IS `groundHeight`, so reverting `surfaceHeightAt` to it turns two of the four new tests red with *"expected -8 to be -1"*. A third asserts the fix changes **nothing on land**, so no existing aimed check can move — and none did.

**DELIBERATELY NOT SHARED WITH `drawHeightFor`.** A wading survivor stands on the seabed with their feet down; an object in the same water floats. The two questions agree everywhere except the shallows, which is precisely where merging them would be wrong. A test asserts they differ there.

**THE FAIR-CHALLENGE CONTRACT IS NOW WITNESSED IN WORDS, ON A DEVICE.** `WRECK 3` reads *"The hull groans and settles under you"* off the page with health at 100.0 and bleeding at 0; `3c` reads a genuinely different sentence — *"Metal is tearing somewhere below the waterline"* — still at 100.0; and only `WRECK 4`, working on after both warnings, takes 100.0 → 90.0 with a real bleed of 0.499 **and still hands over the salvage**. Three quiet stages, two spoken warnings, then a price, proven end to end. `WRECK 5` confirms [[D-011]] on device: 99.97 → 0 across four hours away.

**THE TWO FIXTURE FIXES WERE MINE AND BOTH ARE CONFIRMED.** The thresholds had been set EXACTLY on their own boundaries, and boot-time settling sheds a fraction — so 66 read `shifting` and 88 read `groaning`, one stage low each. Same class as the arrival-profile boot-timing defect carried since [[D-116]]. With a game-hour of boot budget as margin, both now read their intended stage.

**`WRECK 6` IS LEFT RED ON PURPOSE, and this is the entry's most important line.** It asserts the medical store is visible WITHOUT SCROLLING; it reads `off-screen`. Its sibling `WRECK 6b` — a real finger through the real Backpack → Vitals route — **passes**: medicine 2 → 1, severity 2.00 → 1.25. So the player path is genuinely witnessed and only the no-scroll claim fails. **I did not edit the check to agree with me.** A red check naming a real design question is worth more than a green one bent to fit, and the question it names is real: [[D-052]] made these panels scrollable by design, while [[D-053]] moved Rest to the top of the Build panel because *"a primary action a tired player is hunting for should not need a scroll to be seen"*. Whether a medical store, held by an ill survivor, is that kind of primary action is a design call and not the builder's to settle silently.

**THE THIRD FAILURE IS EXONERATED BY EVIDENCE, not by assertion.** `SLICE 2C — switching tabs never leaks a world tap` went red at `failedTaps 4 -> 5`, and this pass edited the Backpack's Vitals tab, so it was a genuine regression candidate. It is not: the check **passed on the identical HUD code one run earlier** (`run-wreck.log`, `failedTaps 1 -> 1`) and again at [[D-123]] (`16 -> 16`). Newly-observed intermittent, recorded rather than waved through. `FIX 5 — pack tappability` remains carried and pre-existing.

**THE ENVIRONMENT COST FOUR ATTEMPTS, and the cause was measured rather than assumed.** Attempt 2 died at line 83 on a Puppeteer CDP timeout with **0.65 GB free**; attempts 3 and 4 were healthy (362 and 302 lines, one failure between them) and were killed by the session ending, not by the machine. The run that completed did so at **1.78 GB free** — which is [[D-116]]'s own recorded closing condition for this failure class, *"one harness pass on a machine with ~2 GB free"*, met for the first time. The memory was held by an unrelated project and was freed by the director on request. **No harness-owned process was ever orphaned**; that was checked at each attempt rather than assumed, per [[D-072]]'s kill-only-what-you-own rule.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `surfaceHeightAt` in `src/data/world.ts`, read by `runtime.projectToScreen`; shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran.** purity (45 brain files), docs-integrity, tune-mirror (46 refs / 56 values), typecheck, production build. **UNIT: ran. 986/986 across 55 files**, including four new projection tests proven to fail against the pre-fix rule. **DEVICE: ran. 363/366**, zero console errors, on a verified-served bundle (`index-WzX-IDoe.js`), full output captured to `.smoke/run-wreck5.log`. **WRECK 14/15** — every one of the slice's four items witnessed, with the single failure being a stricter-than-design visibility claim whose sibling proves the path. **LIVE: see the push SHA and the deploy's served-SHA gate in this batch's report.***

**D-124 · 2026-08-05 — THE WRECK. Built, unit-green, and NOT SHIPPED: the device leg found the harness cannot aim at anything that floats.**

**WHAT WAS BUILT.** The Maritime Slice made the wreck reachable and deliberately left it a destination marker. This gives it a body of work. (1) **Explorable through the shipped verb** — six authored `wreckpart` nodes around the hull, spread wider than one interact radius so exploring means moving around a structure, worked with the same `gatherNode`, the same One Body Resolver effort cost, and the same depletion/regrow machinery as a tree. There is no exploration mechanic; v0_10's Zone U0 ruling — *"a worksite, not a treasure room"* — is the design. (2) **Real salvage**: the wreck-era family the codex has named since Cycle 01 (metal / wiring / glass / medicine), authored PER PART so the medical store is a place a survivor learns rather than a roll. `metal` is the heaviest material in the game and `wiring` the bulkiest, so what you carry home is a decision made 115 m out. **Medicine** is the member that answers a shipped problem now — it relieves illness anywhere, with no fire and no forage, stronger than the brewed remedy, and still relief rather than a cure. `conductive`, in the property vocabulary since Ch.1 describing nothing, finally describes cable. (3) **Risk matching the crossing**: the hull is not inert — every part taken shifts what is left, across five stages, three quiet and two spoken, and the price is a WOUND through the shipped injury model 115 m from shore. (4) **[[D-011]] by a STRONGER mechanism than the water's**: instability has no elapsed-time term that can raise it — it rises in `disturb()`, which takes no duration and is called from a player action, and falls in `settleOverGameHours()`. An absence can only ever make the wreck safer. Save schema **v23 → v24**; the nodes MERGE (the wreck has been in that water since before anyone washed ashore), the salvage does NOT.

**TWO SELF-CORRECTIONS, both caught by the project's own machinery.** I first tagged the salvage structurally (`metal` → `blade`, `wiring` → `textile`, `glass` → `blade`), spending the [[D-055]] schema. The cost gates are still exact-kind, so wood + metal + fibre would have resolved to AXE and the Build panel would then have demanded a knapped stone blade — **[[D-114]]'s exact defect shape, newly invented by me.** Stripped to inert, with a test asserting no wreck-era material satisfies any existing slot. Then my own test caught a tuning defect: at 22 per part, three parts hit *exactly* the 66 warning threshold, so the THIRD part warned while the tune comment claimed "half the wreck unwarned". Retuned to 18. The check asserts the BEHAVIOUR rather than the number, which is why it could catch a number that disagreed with its own stated intent.

**AND THEN THE DEVICE LEG REFUSED IT, which is the entire point of having one.** **351/366, nine WRECK failures**, and the core claim — *working a wreck part through the real player path* — came back **UNPROVEN rather than red**. Root cause, read from the code rather than inferred: `runtime.projectToScreen` projects `island.heightAt(x, z) + 0.4`, the **terrain** height. That was correct while every pickable object stood on the ground. The Maritime Slice introduced objects that FLOAT at `WORLD.seaLevel`, and the wreck parts are the first floating things that must be picked **as nodes**. At the wreck the seabed is −8 m, so `screenOf` hands back a point ~7 m below the visible hull and every harness tap lands in open water. **A check that cannot strike its target proves nothing either way** ([[D-066]] a). Same family as [[D-119]]'s screen-pixels-into-`holdWorld`: an instrument error that makes a working product look broken. It did not surface at [[D-122]]/[[D-123]] because `MARITIME 5` resolves the raft by PROXIMITY to the struck ground point, so a ray landing near the deck still answers "raft"; `pickNode` is a direct mesh ray and has no such tolerance.

**THE OTHER WRECK FAILURES ARE MINE TOO, and neither is the product.** `WRECK 3`/`3c` set instability to precisely `wreckGroaningAt` (66) and `wreckGivingWayAt` (88); `editSave` reloads, boot `reconcile` runs, and settling sheds a fraction — so 66 lands at 65.99 and reads `shifting` (silent) while 88 lands at 87.9 and reads `groaning`. Arithmetic, not a hypothesis, and the **same class as the arrival-profile boot-timing defect carried since [[D-116]]**. `WRECK 6` used `.tab-vitals`, a selector that does not exist, so the medicine's player path is simply unwitnessed.

**THREE NON-WRECK CHECKS WENT RED THAT WERE GREEN AT [[D-123]]** — `fell -> gather -> fell`, the absence clock rate, and `F1`'s tap-to-movement latency. All three are timing-shaped and the run took ~2 h on a machine with ~0.5 GB free, but **none has been attributed**, and blaming load without measuring it is precisely what [[D-072]] forbids. Recorded as unattributed.

**NOT PUSHED.** `fd0cd1f` is local only; `origin/main` stands at [[D-123]]'s `4511ee1`. Shipping a slice whose central verb is unwitnessed would be [[D-090]]'s failure mode with a green unit suite in front of it, and the last three passes have all turned on refusing exactly that.

**Class: DESIGN-BINDING** — the build exists and is unit-proven; nothing in it is live, and its trigger is the projection fix plus one confirming pass.

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran.** purity (45 brain files), docs-integrity, tune-mirror (46 refs / 56 values — it caught three constants missing from the harness mirror BEFORE the run rather than twenty minutes into it), typecheck, production build. **UNIT: ran. 982/982 across 55 files**, with three planted mutations — `reconcile` raising instability, harm read after the disturbance, metal regaining a structural tag — each caught by the intended assertion. **DEVICE: ran, and the result is a REFUSAL: 351/366**, with the slice's central claim vacuous rather than proven. **LIVE: nothing.** This entry describes a build on a local commit.*

**D-123 · 2026-08-05 — THE CONFIRMING PASS (348/351, Maritime 22/22), the `reveal.ts` class sweep, and the failure nobody had ever seen.**

**THE MARITIME SLICE IS NOW FULLY DEVICE-WITNESSED.** [[D-122]] closed the leg but tested a bundle that predated four things: the two `reveal.ts` derivations, the `cameraPosition` readout, and the corrected `MARITIME 4`. This pass ran on a rebuilt bundle (`index-CuEri_Pl.js`, verified served before the run) and **all 22 Maritime checks passed**, including the two that were fixed blind last session: `MARITIME 2c` reads **camera y 2.09** — the view genuinely rides the water rather than following an eight-metre seabed down — and `MARITIME 4` now reads *"raft reachable, shipped sibling reachable"*. **348/351**, up from 341/350.

**THE FAILURE NOBODY HAD EVER SEEN IS IDENTIFIED, and it is a documented one.** [[D-122]] recorded, as a defect of its own, that piping the harness through `tail -120` destroyed all but the last 120 lines, leaving one non-passing check unaccounted for. This pass wrote full output to `.smoke/run-confirming.log` before anything read it, and the missing check is `loads a playable 3D scene — WASHED ASHORE`, reading **health 66.7** against a ±1.5 band around `arrivalHealthFraction` (65). That is **exactly the arrival-profile boot-timing defect this state doc has carried since [[D-116]] and re-stated at [[D-120]]**: health regenerates at `healthRegenPerGameHour` from the moment of arrival, so a slow boot drifts the read out of its band. Arithmetic, not a hypothesis, and not a maritime regression. **The instrumentation fix paid for itself in one run.**

**THE THIRD FAILURE IS THE HARNESS COMPARING TWO DIFFERENT LIVES, and this is arithmetic too.** `F1 — no input went missing during the feel section` asserts `failedInteractionTaps` has not grown since a baseline captured ~2000 lines earlier. It reported **2 at the baseline and 1 now**. A monotonically-incrementing counter cannot go DOWN, so a save reset (`startFresh`/`editSave`) intervened between the two reads and the check is comparing one life's counter against another's. **It is not a product defect and it is not evidence of a swallowed tap.** Deliberately NOT "fixed" in this pass: re-baselining it is a real improvement to a check this pass could not then re-run, and loosening a check you cannot re-verify is how vacuity enters. Named here with its mechanism so the next author does not re-derive it. *`FIX 5 — pack tappability` remains the third, carried and pre-existing since before any of this work.*

**THE `reveal.ts` SWEEP — the CLASS, not the fourth occurrence.** That file has had a hardcoded list go stale in three separate slices — [[D-053]] (the Build-button gate), [[D-114]] (the spear and backpack stranded), [[D-122]] (`satisfied()` missing the raft, `panelHints()` walking a hardcoded five) — each found by a human reading it, never by a test, while the file's own header states the rule: *"a gate you have to remember to extend is a defect with a delay on it."* Three enumerations, all now guarded:

  1. **`SURVIVAL_BASIC`** — a `Set<string>` with no type relationship to the recipes. `new Set(['torhc'])` would silently switch Law 113's fire scaffold off: the first night stops being scaffolded, blind experimentation becomes the only route to fire, and **nothing fails**. The most expensive silent failure available in the file. Now asserted well-formed, asserted to still DO its job, and asserted to be a floor rather than a ceiling.
  2. **`satisfied()`** — the switch that cost the raft its bug. Now total **by construction**: every recipe is driven through the real function in a state where every product is genuinely made, so a future craftable with no case appears **by name**. The one exemption (`knap` — repeatable, no done state, D-055) is named in the test and its reasoning asserted present in the file, because an exemption nobody wrote down is an oversight.
  3. **`panelHints()`** — locked at the level of the property, plus the other side of the rule (a revealed recipe stops hinting) so "derive from the routes" cannot be over-read into "hint about everything forever".

Plus the durable class guard: **no function in `reveal.ts` may carry a literal array of recipe ids**, with comments stripped first — the file discusses recipe names in prose at length, and a check that cannot tell code from commentary would either false-positive forever or be quietly loosened until it caught nothing. Every assertion is BEHAVIOURAL rather than source-grepping: a test that greps for `case 'raft'` proves a string is present, not that the answer is right.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `tests/reveal-sweep.test.ts`, shipped in this batch).
