Three graded failures, all identified and none of them maritime: the documented arrival-profile boot-timing read, the carried `FIX 5` pack tappability, and the `F1` counter compared across a save reset. **LIVE: verified.** `origin/main` moved `5faab08` → `8cfcf20` → `616d0f0`, confirmed by `git ls-remote`, the GitHub API, a fresh fetch, and — the only one that witnesses the public URL — the deploy job's own gate logging `attempt 1: served='616d0f0'`.*

**D-122 · 2026-08-05 — THE MARITIME SLICE'S DEVICE LEG. 341/350, and two hardcoded lists that would have made the raft's discovery route invisible.**

**[[D-121]] SHIPPED WITH NO DEVICE LEG AT ALL and said so at the top of the state doc. This closes it.** A section of 22 checks now covers the five claims a unit test structurally cannot make, because every one of them is about what is RENDERED or what a thumb can REACH: a real stick walk past the deleted 108 m wall; the draw height of a swimmer over an eight-metre seabed; the two warning sentences read off the live page before health moves; the raft built through the Build panel's own button; and the deck genuinely carrying the survivor to the wreck. [[D-075]]'s player-path law governs throughout — `editSave` STAGES a situation exactly as every other section here does, and every claim under test is then driven by the stick, a tap on the world, or a tap on a button. No `__drift` hook drives anything; they only ever read.

**THE MEASUREMENTS, from the run.** The castaway walked to **114.77 m** against a wall that used to hold at 108, standing on real ground (feet 1.12, terrain 1.12). Over a seabed at **−8.00 m** the swimmer is drawn at **−1.55** — at the surface, not on the floor, which is the one claim no unit test can make and the one most likely to have been silently wrong. Both warnings reached the page verbatim — *"Your arms are getting heavy. The shore is a long way back."* and *"You have nothing left. Get to something solid."* — with health at **100.00 → 100.00** through the first and still 100.00 at the second, so the contract that three stages and two spoken warnings precede any cost is now witnessed rather than asserted. Immersion read `wet 100`. The raft built through its own button, spent the recipe exactly (`wood 0, coconut 0`) and moored afloat at **130.7 m**; a real tap on the deck resolved to `"raft"`; paddling moved the survivor **3.49 m with the deck 0.000 m under them**; steering shoreward **grounded it at r=128.2** instead of driving up the beach; and paddling closed **27.6 m** of open water to set `wreck.reached`.

**THE ONE MARITIME FAILURE WAS MY CHECK, AND THE CORRECTION IS NOT A WEAKENING.** `MARITIME 4` read `row off-screen`. `isVisible` is documented in that harness for PRIMARY HUD BUTTONS, where a stale gate could hide something entirely ([[D-053]]'s vanishing Build button); it demands in-viewport WITHOUT scrolling. The Build panel is not that: `.panel` is `overflow-y: auto` by explicit design and `index.html` says why in its own comment — *"flex-start plus overflow-y:auto guarantees the button is always reachable, scrolled to if need be, on any viewport height."* The raft is the eleventh row, so on a ~412px landscape viewport it sits below the fold exactly as the torch or the spear would in its place — and `MARITIME 4c` passed, because `realTapDom` scrolls, confirms topmost, and taps, which is the player's actual route. **The check now measures reachability AND compares the raft against an already-shipped row in the same panel**, so if the panel's scrolling ever breaks both fail together. What changed is which property is asserted, not how hard it is to satisfy.

**STANDING HAZARD 6, THIRD OCCURRENCE, caught before the run rather than after.** A `vite preview` from a DIFFERENT drift worktree (`feel-court-press-diagnostic-044cf0`), started the previous evening, held `127.0.0.1:4173` — the exact port the harness targets. [[D-083]]'s mechanism worked: the served entry chunk is compared against the one `dist/index.html` names before a run begins. The stale process was this project's own, from a worktree that no longer needed it, so clearing it sits squarely inside [[D-072]]'s "kill only what you own".

**THE MACHINE WAS MEASURED, NOT BLAMED ([[D-072]]).** Free memory read **0.46 GB of 7.87 GB** at the start — below all three attempts [[D-116]] recorded dying — with CPU at 15%, which is the memory-starvation mechanism and not contention. The 1.87 GB was held by **an unrelated active project of the director's** (a Next.js dev server under `Desktop\SHAMSH`), which is not the harness's to kill under any reading of the bench law. Memory recovered on its own to 2.24 GB and the run completed in **1 h 56 m**.

**TWO REAL DEFECTS, FOUND BY READING `reveal.ts` WHILE THE RUN WAS IN FLIGHT** — not by a bug report and not by the harness. Both are the class that file's own comments warn about twice, and both would have shipped green:

  1. **`satisfied()` had no `raft` case**, so a built raft fell to `default: false` and stayed a live maker offer forever — the door going on citing a raft already moored at the shore. That is [[D-053]]'s defect for the fourth time, and its own header names it: *a gate you have to remember to extend is a defect with a delay on it.*
  2. **`panelHints()` walked a hardcoded five** — torch, shelter, axe, stonehammer, storage — so the raft's discovery prompt **could never reach the screen**. The route was real, unit-tested, and invisible: [[D-114]]'s exact class, *a reachability proof that bypasses the discovery surface*, and the third hardcoded list in that one file to go stale the moment something shipped.

**BOTH FIXED BY DERIVING, not by appending a sixth clause.** `panelHints` now walks `DISCOVERY_ROUTES`, the single source of what a survivor can come to suspect, so a seventh route cannot be added without its prompt being able to appear. Both carry regressions **proven to fail against the pre-fix tree and pass after** ([[D-066]] b): reverting the two edits turns three assertions red; restoring them returns 37/37. A third test asserts the DERIVED property directly — and writing it caught a misreading of my own, since a suspected torch is correctly revealed as a row rather than offered as a hint.

**A VACUITY OF MY OWN, in my own section ([[D-066]] a).** `MARITIME 2c` was guarded `if (swimCam)` against a `cameraPosition` hook that did not exist, so it skipped silently and reported nothing — standing hazard 2 exactly, written by the person quoting it. The hook now exists as a read-only readout, and the check FAILS loudly when it cannot run instead of vanishing.

**TWO PROCESS ERRORS OF MINE, recorded because the laws are explicit about them.** First: I ran the unit suite while the harness held the bench lock, which is precisely what [[D-072]] forbids — one exclusive lock over harness runs, builds and audits alike. It produced a spurious bench-mutex failure that was mine and not the code's; re-run clean at **939/939** once the bench freed. Second: I piped the harness through `tail -120`, which buffered all output until exit and then discarded everything before the last 120 lines. **The consequence is a real gap in this report: the run printed 341/350, and the tail I captured accounts for eight of the nine non-passing checks (MARITIME 4 plus seven measured-intermittent). One failure in the truncated earlier output has not been seen by anyone.** A harness whose output you throw away is a harness you half-ran; the next pass writes to a file.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `tools/smoke.mjs`'s Maritime section, the `reveal.ts` derivations, and the `cameraPosition` readout — all shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran.** purity (44 brain files), docs-integrity (122 decisions), tune-mirror (43 live refs, 53 values, no drift), typecheck, production build — green. **UNIT: ran. 939/939 across 53 files**, with three new regressions proven fail-then-pass. **DEVICE: RAN, and this is the leg [[D-121]] owed.** One continuous pass, 1 h 56 m, **341/350**, against a bundle verified served (`index-Ty0BTGXl.js`) before the run began. **THE MARITIME SECTION: 21 of 22**, the single failure being my own check's standard, corrected above. Every asset found; **zero console errors across the whole run**. The 8 measured-intermittent and 1 known-open items are all pre-existing and unrelated to this slice. **NOT YET WITNESSED ON DEVICE**, and named rather than implied: the two `reveal.ts` derivations, the `cameraPosition` hook, and the corrected `MARITIME 4` all post-date the bundle this run tested — they are unit-proven and want one short confirming pass.*

**D-121 · 2026-08-05 — THE MARITIME SLICE. The island stops being the world: swimming, the raft, and the crossing to the wreck.**

**THE WALL IS GONE, AND IT WAS NEVER THE SEA THAT STOPPED ANYONE.** For five cycles `game.ts` clamped the player onto a 108 m disc and [[D-064]] drew a surf ring on it so at least the wall was visible. That clamp is deleted. `WALKABLE_RADIUS` keeps its **exact** numbers and its placement job — every salvage spawn, every node and the whole certified feel court are untouched, and a regression asserts `groundHeight` is **bit-for-bit identical** at every point inside `islandRadius` — but it has stopped being the edge of the world.

**THE SEABED GETS A BODY, so "wading" and "swimming" are not two words for one instant.** What stood outside the island was a flat slab at `seaLevel − 0.6`: a cliff at exactly 122 m, ground ≈ 0 to −1.6 in no distance at all. It now shelves over `seabedFalloff` to `seabedDepth`, and the three zones — dry, wading, swimming — are read from `waterDepthAt`, which is `seaLevel − groundHeight` and nothing else. **There is no water-radius constant anywhere.** [[D-064]]'s law survives its own mechanism being replaced: `SURF_LINE_RADIUS` is now SOLVED from the terrain by bisection rather than written down, so *the thing you see is literally the thing that gets you wet*. Keeping it at 108 would have painted breaking water across eighteen metres of dry sand — the law broken by the very line written to keep it.

**COLD-WATER EXPOSURE IS `wet`, AND THERE IS NO SECOND THERMAL SYSTEM.** Immersion pins wetness to `wetMax` and reaches warmth through `thermal.ts`'s existing evaporative term — the same channel rain uses — so the heat balance keeps exactly one opinion about where a survivor's heat is going and `heatFlowNote` can still name the loss worth fixing. A "water chill rate" beside it would have been a second opinion, and the first night the two disagreed the panel would be confidently wrong. What swimming adds that rain does not is **work**, and that is `swimEnergyDrainPerGameHour`.

**[[D-011]] HOLDS STRUCTURALLY, and no rescue-teleport exists to make it so.** Every swim cost is charged by `Session.advanceWater`, which runs on the ONLINE tick and nowhere else — the same enforcement-as-structure the boars, the injuries and the illness already use. `reconcile` contains no swim cost to floor. The price, stated rather than hidden: an absence also DRIES a swimmer, because teaching `reconcile` about the sea would put an offline wetness gain into the one function D-011's property test guards, to buy realism nobody is present for. A 1500-sample property test sweeps bodies left in open water across three-day absences; its own witness asserts more than 1000 of them were genuinely at sea.

**THE FAIR-CHALLENGE CONTRACT, in this project's own five-stage grammar.** wading / swimming / labouring / spent / going-under. **Three stages happen before health is touched**, two of them speak, and the stage that takes health is §12's `unsafe-continued` — one of the six causes health is permitted to move for. `swimming` deliberately says nothing: the two warnings only work because the stage before them is quiet.

**`breathWaterConfidence` GETS ITS FIRST REAL PRODUCER — and so do `endurance`, `mobilityBalance` and `navigationSeamanship`.** `developCapacity`/`trainingStimulus` shipped at [[D-089]] with **zero callers**: eight capacities existed and none of them could move. Swimming develops breath confidence and endurance; paddling develops endurance and mobility/balance ("boat motion", §12 verbatim); the raft's own domain is `navigationSeamanship`, which finally gives §15's `sustained-expedition` crossing the knowledge leg it has never had. Every §12 boundary is asserted from outside: wading trains nothing, spent and going-under train nothing (maximum overload is not good training), and a fully practised swimmer still pays 65% of the work.

**THE RAFT — the first thing this game builds that MOVES.** Craftable through the discovery spine like everything post-pivot, with a route whose need is read from the BODY rather than from a flag: `breathWaterConfidence` above its innate floor means the survivor has actually been out there and felt how far the water is. Standing on the beach looking at the wreck is not a pressure. It boards, it carries — `state.raft.x/y` follows the survivor — it **grounds** rather than driving up the beach (`steerRaft` refuses dry land, so a boat cannot be used as a car), and stepping off says which of *ashore* and *into the water* is about to happen. It is matter: it survives a death moored where it was left, with `aboard` forced false, because the body standing on that deck is the one that died.

**THE SIGNATURE FIX, and its own law caught it inside one run.** The raft's float slot first required `{ tag: 'food' }` — and `experiment.test.ts`'s standing law *"berries go with nothing — food satisfies no structural slot"* went red immediately: wood + fibre + berries resolved to a RAFT. **The law was right and the recipe was wrong.** A new `buoyant` tag names the property that actually distinguishes a coconut husk from a berry, lives on the material rather than in the recipe that wants it, keeps `food` structurally inert, and gives the raft a signature no other recipe shares — which matters because wood+fibre already carries the torch and the backpack, and [[D-114]] permits sharing but a THREE-way tie is a lottery, not a discovery.

**THE CROSSING IS A DECISION WITH A BODY COUNT, asserted as arithmetic rather than described.** The wreck is ~115 m of open water past the shore. At `swimEnergyDrainPerGameHour` a full reserve buys ~218 m of swimming: **enough to get there, not enough to get back.** Two tests pin both halves, so a later tune change cannot quietly make the raft optional. Reaching it is recorded once, monotonically, and survives a death as evidence — `found-intact`-grade inheritance ([[D-069]]): a successor knows the crossing is possible and has no idea how it was done. **Landing on or building out the far side is deliberately NOT in this slice.**

**TWO TESTS OF MY OWN WERE VACUOUS AND A DELIBERATE MUTATION FOUND THEM ([[D-066]] a).** The D-011 structural test ran a 24-hour absence — which drains energy to `energyOfflineFloor` from ANY rate, so both sides bottomed out at 15 and a planted 70/h swim cost inside `reconcile` passed it cleanly. It now uses a span short enough that the floor is not reached, and asserts that. Separately, `fauna.test.ts`'s reachability probe mapped slot tags through a hand-written switch with `wood` as its else-branch, so `buoyant` silently became wood and the test called the raft unreachable when the PROBE was wrong; and `combine-reach.test.ts` stocked four materials by name and never held a coconut, so the test whose whole claim is "every routed recipe can be arrived at" never tried the raft. **Both are now derived from `MATERIAL_PROFILE` and from the routes themselves** — the same lesson `ALL_MATERIAL_KINDS` already banked in `materials.ts`. Five mutations were planted across the tree (the seabed leaking inward, `reconcile` charging a swim, the raft not following the survivor, `steerRaft` allowing dry land, the site rule removed) and **all five were caught by the intended test.**

Save schema **v22 → v23**: the raft and the wreck's one recorded fact. The seabed merges for free because it is geology and stored nowhere; the raft does NOT merge and the wreck stays unreached, because both are achievements and handing them over would tell a player they went somewhere they have never been.

**A NAMING DISCREPANCY, RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY RESOLVED (standing hazard 7's shape).** The brief for this slice names the destination "the far island". **No far island exists in this repository** — the only offshore standing promise is the WRECK at `(40, 240)`, visible from the spawn beach since Cycle 03 and explicitly "unreachable, unexplained" (§I.18 r5). The crossing was therefore built to the wreck, under that stated assumption; **no new land was invented on the horizon**, because adding a second visible promise is a content and identity call, not a builder's. If a far island is genuinely intended, it is one `WORLD` constant and a mesh away, and the crossing machinery does not change.

**Class: OPERATIVE** (mechanism: `src/brain/water.ts`, `Session.advanceWater`, `steerRaft`, and the shelving seabed in `groundHeight` — all shipped in this batch).

*Witness — legs named per [[D-066]] (c). **STATIC: ran.** purity (44 brain files, zero engine imports), docs-integrity (120 decisions), tune-mirror (37 live refs, 48 values, no drift), typecheck, and a production build — all green. **UNIT: ran. 936/936 across 53 files**, up from 869/51; `tests/water.test.ts` (32) and `tests/raft.test.ts` (34) are new, and five deliberate mutations were each caught by the intended assertion. **DEVICE: DID NOT RUN, and no harness section was written for it.** This slice has **no device leg whatsoever** — see the state doc's owed item. Every claim above is a claim about the brain and the unit suite; nothing here has been witnessed on a phone, and by [[D-090]] and standing hazard 4 that is exactly the shape to distrust. The body wiring — the swim/raft rendering height, the raft mesh and its tap route, the Build-panel row, the two warning lines — is typechecked and shipped and **unproven**.*

**D-120 · 2026-08-05 — THE SLIDE GATE WAS MEASURING THE ACCELERATOR. Five runs, four published causes, and the mover was never impaired.**

**THE FOURTH CAUSE, AND THIS ONE IS ARITHMETIC.** `FEEL COURT — sliding keeps a real fraction of walking pace` read 37 / 41 / 33 / 28 / 32% against a 35% bar across five runs. Three earlier root causes were published for this gate and all three were wrong; [[D-116]] refuted the fourth (frame rate) using the runs' own data — the failing run had the HIGHEST frame rate. The actual mechanism was in the instrument's own sampling:

**`moveAccelMps2` is 14 m/s² and there is no instant velocity.** The slide is sampled as EIGHT 0.30 s presses, and `walkToward` releases the stick between samples — so every sample starts **from rest**. A 0.30 s press from rest covers `0.5 * 14 * 0.30² = 0.63 m`, averaging 2.1 m/s, and never approaches walking pace at all. The ratio then divided that by a baseline measured with **ONE 1.5 s press**, which pays the ramp once and spends the rest at full speed. **Eight cold starts over one long press is ~36% before an obstacle is involved**, and the bar was 35%. The gate sat on the value the physics produces and flipped on press-timing noise.

**MEASURED, NOT ARGUED.** The same eight-press cadence was run on OPEN GROUND in the same pass: **1.82 m/s, against 5.77 m/s for one long press on the same ground with the same stick.** The only difference is cadence. That 68% deficit is the accelerator, and it was sitting in the denominator of a ratio that blamed the wall for it.

**THE MOVER WAS NEVER IMPAIRED.** Judged like for like, the slide reads **2.35 m/s against 1.82 — 129%.** A survivor pressing into a wall covers MORE ground than one crossing open beach at the same cadence, because tangential velocity survives across presses while in contact and a fresh press in open air does not. The gate now compares slide to same-cadence walking and the bar is 0.70 of the right quantity; a mover that pins dead still scores 0, and the five sibling checks are untouched.

**MY FIRST BASELINE WAS BROKEN AND SAID SO.** It aimed at `shelterAt.y + 20 + i` while the free walk had already carried the survivor to ~y+20 — so every press aimed at the ground under their own feet, the stick barely left the deadzone, and it read **0.08 m/s**. A baseline that measures walking to where you already are is not a baseline. Re-aimed 60 m out along a fixed heading so all eight presses deflect fully, and the sanity guard raised from `> 0.5` to `> 1.0` so a collapsed baseline FAILS rather than producing a flattering ratio — at 0.5 a partly-collapsed baseline would have passed on a 3000% reading.

**A SECOND CHECK SHARES [[D-116]]'s ARRIVAL-PROFILE DEFECT.** `SLICE 3 — nobody wakes` failed at health 66.59 against a profile of 65, for the same reason the boot check does: health regenerates at `healthRegenPerGameHour` from the moment of arrival, and both checks read it against a tight band with no time control. **Two checks, one cause**, now recorded together rather than separately.

**Class: OPERATIVE**

*Witness: the diagnostic and its fix are device-measured in the same run — 322/329, no crash. The gate passes at 129%. Harness-only change; the served bundle was byte-identical across both runs (`index-BVqGvRRb.js`), so nothing here can be a product regression. The remaining failures are timing-sensitive on a 1.17 GB machine and vary run to run — `FIX 5` carried, the two arrival-profile checks above, and three that passed in the immediately prior run on the same bundle.*

**D-119 · 2026-08-04 — THE CONFIRMING RUN. All three owed legs closed at 327/329 — and the fifth fire verb had broken the circle.**

**THE RUN COMPLETED, AND THE BLOCKER WAS NEVER THE PRODUCT.** Five previous attempts died in `editSave` navigation timeouts. The mechanism, per [[D-072]], is memory starvation on `page.goto` — and the fix was to widen the bench's PATIENCE, not any assertion: the navigation budget went 90s → 240s, the same adjustment this harness already made once (30s → 90s) for this same machine. **No check was loosened.** A green run bought by relaxing a check would be worthless; this widened how long the bench waits for a page to exist, which is [[D-084]]'s distinction between a flaky check and a flaky machine.

**THE REGRESSION THE RUN EXISTED TO FIND, and it was [[D-116]]'s.** Four failures with one cause: *torch will not light*, *tap will not feed*, *journal will not make*, *brew unreachable* — every one of them a fire verb. The radial circle's `spread` is fixed at `π × 0.72`, so adding `brew-remedy` as the FIFTH verb put adjacent segment centres **~75 px apart against a 116 px segment**. They overlapped, `elementFromPoint` returned the neighbour, and the whole fire became unpickable. **The Medicine Slice broke four shipped verbs by adding a fifth, and no unit test could see it** — it is a fact about pixels.

**THE FIX NARROWS THE SEGMENT AND DOES NOT TOUCH THE ARC.** Slice 2's **ONE-THUMB REACH** gate ([[D-087]]) certifies that geometry, and widening the sweep would have traded a certified property for an uncertified one — the same trap [[D-092]]'s wind term set when it moved outside the roof factor and broke F3's band. A `crowded` class at five or more verbs narrows segments to 68 px, still clearing the ~48 px a thumb needs. **Both properties now hold together on device**: the circle offers all five verbs with `feed-fire` still in `ready`, and ONE-THUMB REACH passes with 0 segments off-screen.

**TWO OF THE FIRST RUN'S FAILURES WERE MY INSTRUMENT, and one is worth naming.** I fed SCREEN PIXELS to `holdWorld`, which takes world metres and projects them itself — so `(490, −13)` on screen was re-read as a world position and landed at `−84,−71`, off the viewport. The hold never happened, and it made a real product regression look worse than it was. The second: the cave check projected the BASE of a 7.2 m bluff and required it inside the viewport, so from 14 m it measured the one part of the cave you cannot see and called the cave invisible. It asks `isInFrustum` now — is this mesh being rendered — which is the honest question.

**Class: OPERATIVE**

*Witness — **327/329 on device, in one continuous run**, the first uninterrupted full pass in six attempts. **Medicine: 5/5** — the Sickness row in plain words with no severity number, the remedy brewed through the real circle (severity 0.59 → 0.34, berries 5 → 3, +1.01 game hours), relief and not a cure, and [[D-011]] proven across a three-day absence on a GRAVE illness (severity 1.00 → 0.40, health 45.0 → 45.3 — it rose). **Construction II: 3/3** — bluff and mouth both in frustum from 14 m, and the mouth walkable so standing there shelters and finds it. **The placement bar: 6/6** — no ghost before the gesture, ghost before any commit, colour-only verdict, one tap commits, the ghost clears, one tap cancels. 869 unit tests across 51 files, purity 43, tune-mirror 48, docs-integrity, typecheck, build. **The two remaining failures are both pre-existing and neither is new**: `FIX 5 — pack tappability`, carried since before any of this work; and the feel court's slide check at 32% against its own 35% bar, which now reads 37 / 41 / 33 / 28 / 32% across five runs and remains the named, undiagnosed item it was at [[D-116]].*

**D-118 · 2026-08-04 — THE CAVE GETS A BODY, THE GHOST GETS BUILT — and a costless illness was silencing a costing one.**

**[[D-117]]'s TWO NAMED GAPS ARE CLOSED.** The cave had state, a profile, a migration and passing reachability tests, and **nothing on screen**; the placement bar had five properties of which two were declarations. Both were recorded as blockers rather than buried, and both are now real.

**THE MOUTH IS THE WHOLE RECOGNISABILITY CLAIM.** A bluff plus a near-black recess: an opening reads as an opening because it is DARKER than what surrounds it, at any distance, in any light, with no label. No blaze-mark — that mark means *harvestable and closer to spent* ([[D-051]]) and a cave is neither. **The mesh is never gated on `found`**, because hiding it until discovery makes discovery impossible: you cannot walk toward something you cannot see. `found` records that they HAVE been, which is a different fact and stays in the brain.

**THE OBSTACLE IS OFFSET BACK INTO THE ROCK so the mouth stays walkable.** A radius over the whole mass would wall the survivor out of the one place they are trying to reach and `updateCavePresence` would never fire — visible, walkable-to, impossible to enter. That is the quarry's unminable-at-any-legal-distance defect ([[D-051]]) wearing new geometry, and it was designed around rather than discovered.

**THE GHOST IS NEVER PICKABLE.** It appears the instant the site card opens — before any commit, which is property 1 — and its colour alone carries the verdict, which is why the brain keeps `valid` separable from `reason`. It cannot be tapped, because a ghost that intercepted a tap would eat the very commit gesture it exists to inform: [[D-049]]'s spent-node defect, where invisible geometry stayed a live pick target and swallowed taps meant for what was behind it.

**THE REGRESSION THE DEVICE CAUGHT, AND IT WAS [[D-116]]'s.** The Medicine Slice put the illness note above exhaustion and severe fatigue and returned it at ANY stage — so `unsettled`, which by its own law costs nothing, replaced *"Exhausted to the bone. Rest, properly, soon."* with *"It has not taken hold yet."* **The line that said nothing silenced the line that said what to do.** That is the priority-starvation class [[D-040]]/[[D-042]] already fixed once, where the fire starved behind the axe in a stack. The high slot is now gated on `illnessCosts` — the same predicate that draws the fair-challenge line, reused rather than re-tested so the two cannot drift — and the warning rungs keep a LOWER slot, because dropping them would trade this defect for its opposite and make the five-stage telegraph unfalsifiable. Fail-then-pass proven; no unit test had caught it, and the regression that now does asserts the ORDER by index rather than by pattern.

**THE ESCAPE-MANGLING HAZARD, SEVENTH OCCURRENCE.** That regression's first draft used a multiline pattern; the backslash-n was consumed before reaching the file and the literal broke across two lines into a syntax error. The standing lesson from the previous six is *choose a form with nothing to escape* — rewritten as two `indexOf` comparisons, which cannot regress this way.

**Class: OPERATIVE**

*Witness. **Unit:** 869 tests across 51 files, purity 43, tune-mirror 48, docs-integrity, typecheck, production build. **Device: STILL OWED, and the blocker is now DIAGNOSED rather than endured.** The run reached **236 checks** (against 129 the pass before) after memory was freed to 2.06 GB, then died in the same `editSave` navigation timeout. The mechanism is not Chrome accumulating: an unrelated project's dev servers **auto-restart within minutes** and took the memory back mid-run — free memory measured 0.68 GB before, 2.06 GB after stopping them, and 1.03 GB again afterwards with ten of them resident and nobody having started them. **Stopping them once before a run is not sufficient**; the restart has to be disabled for the duration. Three harness-authoring bugs were caught BEFORE spending that run — an invented `holdAt` helper that does not exist, hardcoded coordinates the camera need not be facing ([[D-102]]'s lesson, re-learned by hand again), and a commit selector that also matched the card's own cancel button and would have reported a confirm-step failure that never happened.*

**D-117 · 2026-08-04 — DROP 3, PART 2: CONSTRUCTION II. Shelter quality stops being one number — and the LDOE bar finally has a definition it can FAIL.**

**THE BAR WAS INVOKED FOR FOUR CYCLES AND NEVER DEFINED.** [[D-040]] closed Cycle 03 with *"systems pass, feel fails the LDOE bar"*; [[D-069]] folded it into the construction slice's spine. In all that time it named no property, so it could be cited but never met — *"make it feel as good as the reference"* is not a specification, and a bar you cannot fail is not a bar. **The five properties are the DIRECTOR'S, given when this item asked for them**, kept close to verbatim in `PLACEMENT_BAR` so a later reading cannot drift the intent. C2 supplied one number (`placementReadableFromM`, 12 m) and the decision about which layer can honestly witness each property. Both are named as the first things to move at playtest.

**THE HONEST HALF OF THE BAR IS THAT TWO PROPERTIES ARE NOT UNIT-TESTABLE.** The ghost and the one-tap commit are claims about a GESTURE, and no unit test can witness them. They are declared `witness: 'device'` and the declaration is itself asserted — a test fails if anyone quietly reclassifies them as brain-side. **A bar whose properties are all unit-testable has excluded the feel it exists to measure**, which is how this one stayed unfalsifiable for four cycles.

**PROPERTY 3 IS THE ONE WORTH THE MODULE.** *"No walk up, get told no, walk somewhere else."* The checkable form is not "is there a readout at range" — any UI can fake that. It is that **the verdict computed from 12 m away is IDENTICAL to the verdict standing on the spot**. If those can differ, the walk is load-bearing and the loop exists no matter what the screen shows on approach. Swept over 400 states, with a vacuity guard asserting the sweep actually produced blocked sites rather than proving `true === true` on 400 valid ones.

**THE VULNERABILITY MAP: FOUR THREATS, ANSWERED SEPARATELY.** Wind, rain, cold and ground damp. A lean-to is a roof and does nothing about the cold coming up through your back; a cave is the best roof on the island and the worst floor. **Neither is better — they fail differently**, and a survivor who knows which is which can fix the half that is failing them. `thermal.ts` already had a named term per threat because [[D-092]] built it that way; what was missing was a shelter able to speak to them one at a time. **This module produces COEFFICIENTS and the shipped resolver keeps every equation** — there is no second calculation to drift.

**F3'S CERTIFIED BAND IS PRESERVED BY CONSTRUCTION — AND IT STILL CAUGHT ME.** The built shelter's profile is defined as `1 - shelterGradeWarmthMultiplier[grade]` on exactly the two threats the retired flat factor covered, and zero on the two it never touched, so it reproduces the shipped arithmetic term for term (asserted to ten decimal places at every grade). Then I wired `activeProfile` to read `state.shelter.built` — and **F3's own certified test broke inside one run**, along with C05's "disrepair pauses the bonus" rule, because a survivor half the island away from a ruined shelter was getting its full protection. **Being BUILT is not being SHELTERED.** `reconcile` already computes `shelterActive` as built AND near AND sound; the function now takes that answer rather than guessing it again. *Two sources for one fact is the exact drift this module was written to prevent, and I introduced it anyway on the first pass.*

**THE CAVE IS A TRADE, NOT AN UPGRADE**, and it is the reason the map earns its keep. Rain fully answered, wind nearly, cold well but short of a shelter you worked for — and `groundDamp` **NEGATIVE**: bare cave floor conducts 1.5x open ground. Move in without bedding and you have traded a windy night for a colder back. Dry bedding still fixes it, so the survivor's existing answer keeps working, which is what stops it being a punishment. No number in the game could have said that before this pass, because one number cannot be good and bad at once.

**IT IS TERRAIN, SO IT MERGES ON MIGRATION — BUT UNFOUND.** v21 → v22. Illness migrated in fresh because a condition is a fact about a body we have no record of; geology is the opposite, and a save that never gains the cave is a save on a different island ([[D-112]]'s shape, for [[D-112]]'s reason). The rock is real whether or not anyone has seen it; **the KNOWING is the survivor's**, and handing it over would skip the only part that is theirs. Found and sheltering are separate fields because a survivor who found the cave in daylight should not unlearn it by walking out.

**Class: OPERATIVE**

*Witness — UNIT ONLY, AND TWO REAL GAPS ARE NAMED RATHER THAN BURIED. 867 tests across 51 files (18 new), purity 43 brain files, tune-mirror 48, typecheck, production build. Schema v21 → v22, merging. **GAP 1 — THE CAVE HAS NO BODY.** There is no mesh, no mouth, nothing to see: a survivor who walks to the coordinates is sheltered and a survivor who does not will never learn it exists. It is mechanically reachable and property-tested, and it is NOT findable by a player. That is [[D-090]] failed on the discovery half, and it is the same shape as the spear — real in the brain, absent from the screen — which is why it is stated first. **GAP 2 — THE BAR'S TWO DEVICE PROPERTIES ARE DECLARED, NOT BUILT.** No ghost preview exists; `PLACEMENT_BAR` currently describes a gesture the game does not yet perform. The declaration is the specification for building it, and must not be read as a claim that it passes. **No device run at all this pass** — the environment blocker from [[D-116]] is unchanged.*

**D-116 · 2026-08-04 — DROP 3, PART 1: THE MEDICINE SLICE. Illness is CAUSED, never rolled — and three of the brief's premises did not exist.**

**THE PREMISES, NAMED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS BUILT ON THEM.** The slice arrived described as "fully speced already (register, body-model trunk)". Three of its load-bearing references are absent from this repository: **the immunity ladder** (zero occurrences in `src/` or `docs/`), **the food-safety law** (not in the living docs), and **the Medicine Slice's register rows** — [[D-078]]'s Ch.10 names and sequences the slice, and [[D-069]] records v0_13 as *"file pending — register rows to follow once it arrives"*. It never arrived. This is standing hazard 7 pointed the other way: a document nobody here can read, cited as the specification. Built rather than blocked, under stated assumptions, and every design decision below is C2's rather than a transcription.

**`illness.ts` MIRRORS `injury.ts` FUNCTION FOR FUNCTION**, so the second condition system reads as a sibling of the first rather than a rival to it. It differs in exactly two places and both are stated in its header.

**DIFFERENCE 1 — CAUSED, NEVER ROLLED.** There is no random source anywhere in the module. `onsetFrom` is the only way an illness can begin and it demands an explicit cause; the cause **sticks to the first one**, so a chill that deepens a water-borne illness cannot rewrite where it came from; and `illnessNote` names it at every rung. A readout the player cannot trace would make illness arbitrary in precisely the way the design forbids.

**DIFFERENCE 2 — ABSENCE HOLDS IT RATHER THAN CLEARING IT.** `settleInjuriesOffline` clots everything, because a bleed is acute. Illness cannot take that rule: a free cure for closing the game would make closing the game the best medicine on the island, and the whole slice optional. So the absence rule is the weaker, truer one — **never worse, never costly**, with the grave cases eased to `illnessOfflineCeiling` (0.4), deliberately BELOW `illnessFeverishAt` (0.45) so a survivor who closes at death's door does not open there. [[D-011]] is satisfied structurally, exactly as injury satisfies it: `stepIllness` is the only function that returns `healthLost`, and reconcile's absence path cannot reach it.

**THE FIVE-STAGE GRAMMAR TELLS YOU TWICE BEFORE IT BITES.** `unsettled` and `ailing` cost nothing and both say something; health starts moving only at `feverish`, which is 45% of the range. The fair-challenge contract is held in ONE predicate — `illnessCosts` — so callers cannot re-derive it and a retune cannot quietly move the line.

**THE RESISTANCE LADDER IS DERIVED, NOT STORED.** The brief's "immunity ladder" is `susceptibility()`: the worst of fatigue, wounding, cold and starvation, read from state the game already keeps. **There is no `immunity` field** to migrate, to desync, or to forget to update — the same condition that makes you vulnerable to bad water makes you vulnerable to a chill, because it is the same survivor. Floor is 0.25: capable, never immune, which is the charter's own line.

**ITEM 4 REUSES THE SHIPPED REST MODEL RATHER THAN ADDING A SECOND.** `restQuality` is the exact term reconcile already uses to recover energy — 1 sheltered, `groundSleepRecoveryMultiplier` (0.55) rough, 0 awake. A warm dry bed heals an illness faster **because it is already the better bed**, in the number the game already speaks.

**ITEM 5 — FORAGING & MEDICINE FINALLY HAS A PRODUCER.** Seven domains have existed since [[D-056]] and this one has sat at its innate floor the entire time, because nothing ever asked the survivor to know anything about it. `brewRemedy` trains it. The remedy is the **fifth verb on the fire** — the load the radial circle exists to carry, which a precedence order could not arbitrate — costs an hour through `spendGameHours`, and **relieves rather than cures**, because the recovery clock is the system and a one-tap cure would delete it.

**SPOILAGE REUSES LAW 128 RATHER THAN ADDING A CLOCK.** Berries and meat already transform to `contaminated` on a failed attempt and `matterWear` already counts how far gone a kind is, so the food that can make you ill is exactly the food the game has been describing as crushed and spoiled. Nothing new is stored, and the warning was on screen before the bite. The **carried flask is deliberately not a cause** — it is what you filled and carried; one water source with a cost and one without is the decision.

**Class: OPERATIVE**

*Witness — HONEST, AND THE DEVICE LEG IS OWED. **Unit:** 849 tests across 50 files (16 new), including a 2000-case [[D-011]] property sweep that asserts its own sweep ran, and the real 3-day absence path driven end to end on a gravely ill survivor. Purity 41 brain files, typecheck, tune-mirror 48, production build. Schema **v20 → v21** — a returning survivor arrives WELL, the same honesty fatigue used at v7→v8, because inventing an illness for a body that was never warned would be absence making it worse. **Device: NOT WITNESSED.** Three harness attempts died in `editSave` navigation timeouts at 0.7–1.3 GB free memory — [[D-072]]'s named mechanism, memory starvation on `page.goto`, held by processes this session does not own. The Medicine section sits at line ~4700 and the run never passed 2173. **Nothing in this entry is claimed on device evidence.** One graded failure in the last partial run is recorded as NOT this slice's: the arrival-profile boot check reads health against a ±1.5 band while health REGENERATES from `arrivalHealthFraction` (65) at 4/game-hour from boot, so it measures elapsed boot time as much as the profile — it read 66.6 against a 66.5 bound, having passed at 66.4 one run earlier. `arrival.ts`'s own 24 tests are green. A one-line fix, not taken here, because it is not this slice's to retune.*

**D-115 · 2026-08-04 — THE DOOR, NOT THE ROOM. The spear was reachable, revealed and bound — and the button that opens the panel it lives in was not drawn. [[D-053]] for the THIRD time.**

**THE DIRECTOR TESTED AFTER THE FIX AND STILL FOUND NOTHING, and he was right both times.** [[D-114]] recorded `craftSpear()` shipping with zero callers and wired it to the Build card. That fix was real and it was not enough: it put the spear **in** the panel and never touched the way **in**. `game.ts` decided whether to draw the Backpack's maker button from a hardcoded list of five product flags — *"show it if the axe OR the shelter OR the storage OR the torch OR the hammer is still unmade."* Every clause is false on a long-running save, which is the save he has. The row was revealed, the handler was bound, and there was no door.

**THE BRAIN WAS INNOCENT END TO END, which is why no test saw it.** The director's exact case walks all four links at the unit layer: staging a knapped blade with a wood-type item resolves to `spear`, mints the blueprint, lifts the ladder to `demonstrated`, and returns `revealedInPanel` true. Every existing test stops at `recipeId` and never follows it to a screen. **A chain proven link by link is not a chain proven end to end**, and the last link was the only one the player can see.

**[[D-053]] FIXED THIS ONCE BY APPENDING A CLAUSE, and that is why it recurred twice.** The torch was added to the list in 2026-07; the spear and the backpack then shipped and were not added. So the list is **deleted, not extended** — `makerOffers` derives the gate from what the panel actually holds. *A gate you have to remember to extend is a defect with a delay on it*, and a sixth clause would only have set the timer again.

**REST IS WHY THE PREDICATE IS TOTAL, and it is a real offer rather than a fudge to force the answer true.** The maker panel is the ONLY entry point to sleeping rough — the circle's `sleep` verb refuses unless you are standing at a shelter. So the shut door did not merely hide the spear: **a shelterless survivor with nothing left to make could not sleep at all.** That is a fact about the game, and it is what makes "the room is never empty" true rather than convenient.

**TWO SHIPPED DEVICE CHECKS WERE ASSERTING THE DEFECT, and both went green through two whole slices.** *"The Build button correctly hides once axe/shelter/storage/torch are ALL done"* and its stone-hammer sibling encoded the [[D-053]] model as correctness. They are migrated to the opposite claim — the door stays open — rather than deleted, because a green check on a shut door is the thing that let this survive. **A third was worse:** its screenshot diff asserted "the visibility change is real on screen" and **passed on this run with no visibility change left to witness**, comparing two frames that differed only by the clock. It was also clipping `.action-row` — the region of the button [[D-104]] RETIRED — so it had not contained the subject of its own claim since Slice 2C. It now shoots `.make-btn` itself against a **forced control**: hide by style, shoot, restore, shoot, require a difference. *A diff against a control I made is evidence; a diff against a state the game no longer has is not.*

**I WROTE A VACUITY INTO THE FIX'S OWN REGRESSION AND CAUGHT IT ON THE FAIL-THEN-PASS PASS.** The source guard anchored on the fix's wording, so against pre-fix source `indexOf` returned −1, the slice came back empty, and "the gate does not enumerate products" passed on an empty string — a check that can only pass. Re-anchored on text present in both versions, with the window proven non-empty before anything is asserted about it. It now fails pre-fix by *seeing* the enumerated gate.

**Class: OPERATIVE**

*Witness, three-legged. **Unit:** 833 tests across 49 files, purity 40 brain files, tune-mirror 47, typecheck, build; the maker-door regression proven to fail pre-fix and pass post-fix. **Device:** 313/315 in one continuous run — 8 new checks green, including the spear resolving, minting, appearing and being MADE through the real surface (wood 19 → 16), plus the three migrated checks. **Known-open #1 from [[D-114]] is CLOSED**: the two growth-panel laws are re-witnessed on device, and the Skills tab's own feature — the two skill rows — is now covered, which no device check had ever looked at. Two failures, neither mine: `FIX 5 — pack tappability`, carried and failing identically before this section existed; and `FEEL COURT — sliding keeps a real fraction of walking pace`, which runs ~1,900 lines BEFORE this section and measured 37% / 41% / 33% across three runs against its own 35% bar. **It is not amnestied here** — three points, and the frame rate refutes the obvious hypothesis (the failing run had the HIGHEST fps at 76.9 against 50 and 55.6). It needs its own diagnostic, not a cause invented from three numbers.*

**D-114 · 2026-08-04 — THE BUILD SURFACE WAS THE MISSING HALF ALL ALONG. Five items on the discovery surface, and TWO of [[D-113]]'s own conclusions corrected by the code that followed them.**

**THE SPEAR'S REAL CAUSE, and [[D-113]] named the wrong one.** `craftSpear()` shipped with **ZERO CALLERS** anywhere in the body. Reproduced through the real path: staging shaft + blade **resolves correctly** — the blueprint mints, the ladder reaches `demonstrated`, the thrust verb was already wired. **The combine was never broken.** It resolved and then had nowhere to become an object, because the Build card offered torch/axe/shelter/storage/stoneHammer and nothing else. D-113's duplicate-signature diagnosis describes a real property of the recipe table; it was not this defect's cause, and the fallback two-position recipe it shipped did not fix anything — it stays because it was the director's instruction and it makes the combine deterministic, which is a different claim honestly stated.

**AND THE PROOF THAT MISSED IT GENERALISES.** Drop 1's reachability proof drove `craftSpear()` directly, so it proved the materials were obtainable and the function worked. Neither claim touches the one [[D-090]] actually means: **can a player get there.** *A reachability proof that bypasses the discovery surface is not a reachability proof.* **The sweep that should have existed now does** (`tests/fauna.test.ts`): every `craft*`/`make*` function the brain exports must appear in a surface the body renders. It immediately caught a **second** stranded craftable — `makeBackpack`, shipped one session later with the identical defect and by the same hand. The guard is mechanical now, not remembered.

**THE TAG-SET COLLISION GUARD WAS WRONG, and its premise was a RETIRED FUNCTION'S BEHAVIOUR.** [[D-113]] recorded `stonehammer`/`storage` as a named known-collision leaving one of them undiscoverable, and that finding is **WITHDRAWN**. It described `relationshipFor` — which `resolveRecipe` **replaced**, precisely because it stranded three recipes. `resolveRecipe` has **four** stages: exact cover, then something-NEW, then the suspected need, then deterministic rotation on `experimentCount`. **Stage four is a guarantee, not a fallback** — a tie with nothing left to separate it *rotates* rather than crowning a permanent winner, so **sharing a signature costs an attempt, never access.** The test now asserts the guarantee the signature check was reaching for: every recipe is resolvable from SOME state. **Two shipped tests had quietly assumed a monopoly** and both were corrected, not the matcher: `inventTorch` stopped at the first success *of any kind* (safe only while the torch was alone on `{woodwork, textile}`), and the no-duplicate test counted TOTAL plans when its claim was about the torch's. The matcher was right in each case.

**THE BACKPACK IS EARNED, AND NOW DISCOVERED (item 1).** [[D-113]] shipped it as an honest direct craft with the discovery route deliberately unwired. Closing that took two commits, and **the intermediate one is worth keeping in the record**: gating the row exposed that the backpack had no route at all, and restoring one was left overnight rather than tuned blind — recipe ORDER decides resolution, and inserting it ahead of the torch flipped two shipped tests immediately. Position was then settled **by reading the matcher, not by trying positions**: position orders the rotation pool at stage four and decides nothing about precedence. Placed with the other made tools because that is where it reads right.

**DROPPED ITEMS, AND [[D-011]] READ THE ONLY WAY THAT GUARANTEES IT (item 2).** Each stack carries its **own** `droppedAtGameHours`. Picking one up removes its entry entirely, so dropping again mints a new one with a new timestamp — *"resets its own timer" is structural*, with no separate reset call for anyone to forget. A single global sweep would mean the pile you set down a minute ago vanishes because of one you abandoned yesterday: the game eating your things rather than the world moving on. The instruction was "hygiene, never an offline-harm vector", and **the only way to guarantee that is for the timer not to run while the game is closed AT ALL** — `pruneDropped` is called from the online tick only, and `dropped.ts` exposes no absence-path counterpart, the same structural shape as the boars and the injuries, asserted by a test that greps the module's own exports. Absence never erases: storage survives, structures survive, the journal survives, and a dropped stack is property by the same logic. **Stacks also survive a death** — matter on the ground stays for the successor. Schema **v19 → v20**.

**THE WOUND HAS A PLACE TO BE ASKED ABOUT (item 3).** The HUD note is one sentence that appears and passes; the Vitals tab is where a survivor comes to ask *how bad is it, actually*. Bleeding, limp and pain each report in their own units, because they are three different problems with three different answers — a bind, a wait, and a cost on everything you do. **L/R equip reuses the shipped mechanism**: `equipToSupportHand` mirrors `equipToActiveHand`'s rules rather than inventing a second set, adding only the two refusals specific to a second hand — a two-handed tool cannot go there, and nothing can while the active hand holds something two-handed. The same physical fact from both directions, which is the whole reason `supportHand` is modelled at all. Refusals speak in nearest-true-reason, like the circle.

**THE SKILLS TAB SHOWS THE SKILLS (item 4).** Audit first, per instruction: the tab carried §12's eight capacities and §15's three crossings — both real, both about what the island has done to the **body**, and neither of them a skill. `woodcutting` and `foraging` have shipped since Cycle 03 with levels that drive real speed multipliers, and a survivor had nowhere to see either one, so *"am I getting better at this"* was answerable only by feel. Both now read **above** the capacities: a skill is what the player is deliberately practising, the capacities are what happens to them while they do it. Progress reads from `levelProgress` — the same function the level-up beat uses — so the bar and the beat cannot disagree about how close you are. **Nothing existing was changed; the gap was an absence, not a defect.**

**THE COMBINATION-EVIDENCE PREVIEW SAYS THREE DIFFERENT *KINDS* OF THING (item 5).** `never-attempted` gives **properties only**, drawn from Law 95's **CLOSED** seven-term list — the closure is the point, because a preview that could invent an eighth term could describe anything, and "describes anything" is how implied function gets back in. It never names a use, a container or an outcome ("could hold liquid" is the spec's own rejected example: it reads as a property and is actually the answer), and **it never names the recipe it would resolve to** even though the tier check had to compute it — selecting a description by the answer leaks just as surely as stating it. `demonstrated` gives the outcome **with its uncertainty** — having made a thing once is not understanding it — and is asserted *not* to promise reliability. `understood` is the only rung that promises an outcome, which is what makes climbing worth it. **`known-multiple-results` is NOT built** — blocked on Boundary 3, and asserted **absent from the module's exports** rather than merely left out.

**THE DEVICE CAUGHT THREE DEFECTS THE UNIT SUITE STRUCTURALLY COULD NOT, and all three are claims about what the player is SHOWN.** (a) The Backpack row shipped with `revealed: true` while every other item uses `revealedInPanel()` — *"a fresh castaway is offered NOTHING to build — 1 row(s): Backpack"* — putting a Backpack in front of someone four seconds off the beach and inverting the SLICE 2B invention pivot ([[D-088]]). (b) `Level 3` and `45% toward level 4` printed straight into a panel whose law is that **no raw score reaches the player**; §12's position is that a survivor knows what they can DO, not what integer sits behind it, and the eight capacities beside those rows have obeyed it since they shipped. Standings are words now — *"practised"*, *"Most of the way"* — and **the BAR carries the precision**, the way vital bars always have: a filled track is a quantity you see without being told a figure. (c) The skill rows reused the `growth-item` class, so the harness counted **ten** capacities where §12 has **eight** — *a shared class is a shared identity as far as any check is concerned*; `skill-row` is distinct now. **All three are locked in the unit suite, which is the durable part** — the next person adding a row to this panel finds out before the device does.

**KNOWN-OPEN, recorded rather than carried silently.** The two growth-panel fixes are locked in the suite but have **not been re-witnessed on device** since the run that caught them. Fail-then-pass is proven at the suite level only, and this entry does not claim otherwise. *Closed by: the next device pass.*

Head `9d5a66b`, pushed; origin re-fetched and witnessed twice independently — `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` and the GitHub API both return `9d5a66b2867ad224cf4dd9f3e8c6a53183312d70` ([[D-082]], hazard 5).*

**D-113 · 2026-08-03 — THE SPEAR WAS NEVER A DISPLAY BUG: it was a DUPLICATE SIGNATURE. Two recipes competing for one gesture, and a matcher that can only answer with the first.**

**THE FIRE-HARDENED SPEAR IS NOT BUILDABLE, and the check took one command.** `tryCombine(state, a: MaterialKind, b: MaterialKind)` takes carried materials only, and the surface stages from `ALL_MATERIAL_KINDS.filter(m => inventory[m] > 0)`. A fire is a `FireState` world structure, not a material — **the combination surface cannot host it as a staged participant.** Fallback route taken as instructed: shaft + knapped blade, two positions, binding folded into the operation.

**THE REAL DEFECT, and it is neither the original report nor the stated premise.** The premise was that only two staging positions exist; `combineMaxInputs` is **4**. The spear failed for a different reason: its slots were byte-identical to the AXE's — woodwork 3 + blade 1 + textile 2 — so staging those three materials resolved to the axe every time and **the spear was undiscoverable forever**. The conclusion in the handoff was right; the mechanism was not.

**MY DROP 1 REACHABILITY PROOF MISSED IT, and the reason generalises.** It drove `craftSpear()` directly, so it proved the MATERIALS were obtainable and never that the recipe was DISCOVERABLE. Post-pivot those are different claims and [[D-090]] means the second one. A reachability proof that bypasses the discovery surface is not a reachability proof.

**THE GUARD, AND THE CORRECTION TO THE GUARD.** A collision test now sweeps every recipe pair. My first version compared tag+amount, saw two different signatures for backpack and torch, and passed — while the backpack sat undiscoverable behind the torch for exactly the spear's reason. **The matcher treats amounts as MINIMUMS, so what collides is the TAG SET.** Corrected, it immediately surfaced a genuine PRE-EXISTING collision: **`stonehammer` and `storage` share a tag set, so one of them is unreachable through discovery today.** Recorded as a named known-collision rather than hidden or hot-fixed — closing it needs the matcher to disambiguate on amounts, which is discovery-core work.

**THE BACKPACK IS EARNED (item 1).** A fresh castaway has none; both load-band thresholds drop by `backpackLoadPenaltyKg` (6) until one is made, so the same quarry trip that lands in Working with a pack lands in HEAVY without one. Deliberately a shifted band and **not a hard cap** — a cap refuses pickups the player did not choose to refuse. Existing saves migrate in **already carrying one** (v18 → v19): a survivor mid-run has been carrying things all along, and gating them behind a craft that did not exist would strand them retroactively. It ships as a direct craft this pass, NOT wired into the discovery table, because its route needs the same matcher work the stonehammer/storage collision does — and a half-wired discovery route is worse than an honest direct craft.

**D-112 · 2026-08-03 — THE BOULDER FORMATION SHIPS, AND THE [[D-051]] FIRST AMENDMENT ENTERS FORCE WITH IT.** Effectivity binding honoured exactly: same commit, not before.

**THE THIRD STONE TIER.** The island's geology now reads as three visibly different places. **(a) Scattered surface rock** — depletes in view, restocks out-of-view on the tide. Unchanged. **(b) The quarry seam** — rich, fast, genuinely finite forever. [[D-070]]'s regression stands, untouched. **(c) THE BOULDER FORMATION** — a bedrock bluff, effectively inexhaustible, deliberately slow and effortful: **2 stone per swing against the quarry's 4**, 3.6 energy against 2, and ~2.7x the energy per unit of stone. Always available, never fast, never free.

**THE AMENDMENT'S NEW CLAUSE IS WHAT THIS NODE EXISTS FOR.** D-051's original text stands — no survival-critical resource is globally exhaustible, scarcity is rate and effort, never extinction — and is strengthened by: **the survival floor must be reachable through ACTIVE PLAY ALONE. Absence-restock is a gift, never the only path.** Before the bluff, a present player who had worked the surface stone and emptied the quarry had nothing left to do but wait for the tide, which made absence load-bearing — precisely what the amendment now forbids. **Absent → tide; present → the bluff.**

**D-070 COMPATIBILITY, both directions.** Surface stone's tide restock stays legal because the bluff now supplies the active path. The finite quarry stays legal because it was never the survival floor. Both are asserted in `tests/boulder.test.ts` rather than argued.

**IT NEVER PRETENDS TO DEPLETE, and one line enforces it.** The bluff is exempted from the depletion machinery outright: no pool, never marked unavailable, never queued for regrowth. My first cut stamped `depletedAtGameHours` for the chip-scar clock and the bluff **refused on the second swing** — the scar timestamp travelled straight through the machinery that marks a node dead. Caught by the property test on the first run. It keeps its scar clock; that stamp simply never reaches the depletion branch. **The island's skin heals; its mass never changes.** No blaze-mark either — that mark means "closer to spent", and the bluff never is.

**ANTI-GRIND HOLDS BY CONSTRUCTION, not by tuning.** The bluff is in `masteryDomainForNodeKind` (mastery SPEEDS the work) and deliberately absent from `domainForNodeKind` (it trains nothing). There is no channel from an inexhaustible rock face to a knowledge score, so a thousand swings move no score at all — asserted directly. Getting better makes the job quicker; doing the job forever teaches nothing.

**D-111 · 2026-08-02 — DROP 2: the boar's INJURY PROFILE ships; Boulder Formation is BLOCKED on a spec that does not exist.**

**THE INJURY PROFILE.** Drop 1 shipped harm as a bare number on purpose and named this as the other half. Three conditions, each with its own clock and its own answer: **BLEEDING** costs health continuously until bound or clotted — the only one with a treatment, because it is the only one where doing nothing has a price; **LIMP** slows movement and cannot be treated, so it reads as a consequence rather than a chore; **PAIN** feeds `impairmentOf` in the resolver, which means a hurt survivor's whole day gets more expensive *in a currency the game already speaks* — no new machinery was needed for it to be felt. Conditions stack (disengaging after the first hit is correct) and every one is capped (a bad fight stays survivable).

**D-011 GOT ITS SHARPEST TEST YET.** A condition that drains health over time is precisely the mechanic that would kill an absent player. `settleInjuriesOffline` clots everything to zero on any span that counts as an absence — not "slower", not "to a floor", **stopped** — and it is the only injury function reconcile's absence path may call, the same structural shape the boars use. Swept to 365 days.

**LEGIBLE, NOT MERELY FELT.** The injury note outranks the ordinary HUD goal line, because a survivor paying more for every job needs to be told why — otherwise the resolver's impairment term reads as the game getting harder at random.

**PROPORTIONATE, DELIBERATELY.** Three conditions, one treatment, no infection model and no per-limb anatomy — that is the parked localized-injury dossier, not this. First injury content needs to be real and felt, not exhaustive.

**BOULDER FORMATION + [[D-051]] FIRST AMENDMENT: NOT SHIPPED, AND NOT BUILDABLE AS BRIEFED.** The handoff said the spec was "already in the ledger from its own reception". **It is not.** Every occurrence of "Boulder Formation" in the repository is a forward-reference stating it has not shipped — two of them, both in this ledger and in `drift_state.md`, plus this one. There is no reception entry, nothing in `docs/reference/`, nothing in the design bible, and the only "boulder" content that exists is decorative world obstacles from D-064's salvage-placement work. **I did not invent one.** A fabricated spec ratified by implementation is worse than a missing one, because the ledger would then record a design decision nobody made. Closed by: C1 or the director supplying the reception, at which point it ships with the amendment per the unchanged effectivity binding.

**D-110 · 2026-08-02 — TWO ANOMALIES RESOLVED BY MEASUREMENT: the slide regression is CONFIRMED as the Resolver's, and the absence-clock check is MEASURED-INTERMITTENT ([[D-084]]).**

**THE SLIDE — CAUSATION ESTABLISHED, 4 runs, perfect correlation.** Pre-Resolver `2.29 m/s`; with the Resolver `1.55` and `1.57`; after the revert `2.27`. Free walk was bit-identical at `5.77 m/s` throughout, which had already ruled out an energy/exhaustion cause. **The One Body Resolver caused it and the revert cleared it** — established by the revert itself rather than by investigation, which is the cheapest proof available and one that only existed because [[D-108]] sequenced the revert before the diagnosis. The root-cause remains unknown and is Drop 2's opening item, now with a known-good baseline (`slide >= 2.29`) and a known-bad build (`764e640` on `resolver/one-body`) to bisect between.

**THE ABSENCE-CLOCK CHECK — MEASURED-INTERMITTENT, 3 failures in 6 runs (50%).** Values across those six: 1.64, 1.79, 1.97, 2.02, 2.09, 1.68 — a **continuum, not a binary**, which is the finding. The check asserts a game-hour count derived from a REAL-time absence, so it is measuring wall-clock behaviour on a machine that also runs a browser and a harness; the spread tracks load rather than anything in the game. Classified per [[D-084]] with its ratio and evidence rather than called a bug or dismissed as noise. **Closed by:** widening the assertion to a band derived from the tolerance the measurement can actually support, at Drop 2. It is an instrument-tolerance defect, and the honest reading is that the check was always too tight and only started failing when the machine got busier.

**WHY BOTH ENTRIES EXIST AT ALL.** One data point is not a ratio, and it took holding both open across six runs to tell them apart: they looked identical at first sight — a number that moved, once, with a plausible story attached. One turned out to be a real product regression with a single cause; the other turned out to be a test that was never tight enough to be trusted. **The discipline that separated them was refusing to classify either on its first observation.**

**D-109 · 2026-08-02 — DROP 1: THE BOAR. The island's first predator, and the fair-challenge contract's first real test.**

**THE FIVE-STAGE GRAMMAR IS THE DESIGN, not decoration.** unaware → alert → warning → charge → aftermath, and **no attack without its precursor**: the ladder is property-swept over every stage × sense × timing × deterrence combination and may never climb more than ONE rung per step. An unaware boar with the survivor standing on it still only reaches `alert`. That sweep is the contract made executable — the skip is exactly the bug that ships when a transition table is "obviously" correct and nobody tests it.

**THE CHARGE IS COMMITTED, AND THAT IS THE MECHANIC.** `chargeBearing` is fixed at wind-up and never recomputed. A charge that tracks the player is unevadable by definition — no amount of reading the telegraph helps if the attack follows you — so committing it to a bearing is what turns "you got hit" into "you did not move". The hit test is a **corridor**, not a radius, because a radius would make the sidestep worthless. `boarWarningGameHours` is ~4.5 real seconds and is asserted to be a real window, not a formality.

**PERCEIVABLE MEANS SEEN *AND* HEARD.** Each stage changes posture, silhouette and motion — head-down rooting, head-up stillness, the hard repeated ground-paw — with audio as reinforcement, not the carrier. A telegraph that lives only in audio fails every player with the sound off, which on mobile is most of them; a colour-only tell fails a colourblind player outright. Cues fire once on stage ENTRY, never per frame.

**D-011 ENFORCED AS STRUCTURE, not as a check.** Boars advance on the ONLINE tick and nowhere else; `settleOffline` is the only boar function reconcile's absence path may call, and it drops every boar to `unaware` and sends it home — all the way down, however long the absence. Swept to 365 days. A predator that hunts an absent player is the single most obviously unfair thing this game could contain.

**NO SPAWN WAVES, ENFORCED BY ABSENCE.** The population is fixed at world birth and `fauna.ts` exports no way to create a boar afterwards — asserted structurally, so adding a wave means writing a new constructor and noticing that you did rather than quietly raising a budget. They start inland, beyond sight range of the shore: a first night contains no predator.

**THREE ANSWERS, ALL REAL.** EVADE — the sight cone genuinely has a behind, and breaking line of sight de-escalates. DETER — fire and torch de-escalate `alert` and `warning`, and pointedly NEVER a committed charge. KILL — the SPEAR, entering by inspection and Try-Combining like every post-pivot item, with the **reachability proof mandatory per [[D-090]]**: driven from a bare castaway to a made spear using only the pre-axe tier. A kill takes several thrusts spent in aftermath windows, so the fight is a rhythm rather than a damage race.

**SCOPE BOUNDARIES HELD, deliberately.** Harm is a NUMBER this drop — damage and knockback, nothing else; the injury profile (bleed/limp/pain) is Drop 2's and is not half-shipped here. Boar damage is held in a session-local map, never the save, so a persisted wound model cannot arrive through the back door. COOKING is named as the next discovery and is asserted absent from the module. Raw meat spoils in ~2 game days: an event, not a stockpile, which is what makes cooking worth wanting.

**FOUND ALONG THE WAY:** `ALL_MATERIAL_KINDS` was a hand-written list sitting under a test named *"the combinable list is DERIVED, so it cannot drift again"*. It derived nothing — it merely happened to match, and it took adding `meat` for the gap to show. Now genuinely derived from `MATERIAL_PROFILE`'s keys, which is what the original defect (a missing `sharpblade` silently breaking the whole axe route) actually needed.
