**D-087 · 2026-07-30 — SLICE 2, "THE RADIAL CIRCLE", CLOSED** — its own verdict, distinct from [[D-085]]'s. The circle ships under **the Default-Verb Law** ([[D-086]] lineage, C1's constitutional pen): *a tap ALWAYS fires the context's default verb; the circle opens on HOLD, or on tap only where no sensible default exists.* My first cut opened it on tap whenever two options existed, and C1 ruled that a **defect class** rather than a trade-off — a survivor carrying wood tapped their shelter and got a menu instead of sleep, so the single most frequent action in the game silently cost two taps. The law restored tap behaviour at every target and **twelve of the thirteen checks I had listed as needing supersession self-resolved**, which is the tell that the law was right and my design was wrong.

**233/233 device checks pass**, zero failures, build identity confirmed, from `6e12931`. The four Slice 2 checks are green on their own terms: tapping a pond with a flask still drinks (thirst 69.7 → 100.0, no menu); holding it opens the circle (ready `drink, fill-flask`, blocked `fish`); a blocked segment is **shown** greyed carrying its own reason (*"You have no line to fish with."*) rather than hidden; and **ONE-THUMB REACH passes — 0 segments off-screen, lowest edge at 296 px.**

That last check failed three times first, and the reason belongs on the record because it is a reasoning failure, not a geometry one. **The clamp fix was INERT.** I clamped the hub's inline `left`/`top` — which were being ignored, because no CSS existed for `.verb-hub` or `.verb-seg` at all. The hub was statically positioned and the segments laid out in normal flow. Twice I reasoned harder about geometry that was never being applied. What found it was asking *whether the fix ran at all*, which should have been the first question after the first failed run, not the third. Filed against hazard #2: **a fix that cannot be observed to have executed is indistinguishable from no fix.**

**The lesson, stated (C1, this session): THE ARTIFACT UNDER EDIT MUST BE VERIFIED AS THE ARTIFACT THAT RUNS.** Same witness family as *local is not live* ([[D-082]]) and *a 200 is not your build* ([[D-083]]) — a fix in a file the running system never consults is the same failure as a fix on a branch the server never pulled. **No new law is needed**; the Vacuity Law's clause (a) already governs. What was missing was the habit of asking the question early, so it is written down here rather than left as an instinct one acquires by losing three runs.

Three priority hacks retired, one deliberately kept and **named in code** rather than left implicit: fire is not routed through the circle, because lighting a torch from a fire is not a menu decision.

**Class: OPERATIVE** — the law is shipped, and both halves of it are mechanised: the tap path in `defaultVerb`, the hold path in `holdOpensCircle`. *Witness: `tests/verbs.test.ts` (20, including the frequent-verb sweep — no capability, at any target, may ever change what a tap does), `tools/smoke.mjs` 233/233 at `6e12931`.*

**D-084 · 2026-07-29 — Harness vocabulary amendment under the Vacuity Law: MEASURED-INTERMITTENT** adopted as a third check state, between **known-open** (a scheduled defect) and **check** (must always pass). Forcing a flaky check into either one misreports it in opposite directions — filed as known-open it cries wolf on every run where it passes; left as an ordinary check it turns the run red for a cause nobody has diagnosed. It carries an **observed pass/fail ratio and reproduction count from recorded runs only** (never a guess), auto-refreshed each slice close, **plus a hypothesis line or the explicit words "cause unknown"** — a blank field reads as "nobody looked", and that should be visible. **Maximum residence: two slice-closes**, then either a root-cause investigation with a named closer or reclassification on evidence; a ratio worsening past `INTERMITTENT_PROMOTE_TO_DEFECT` **auto-promotes to a real defect** (a flaky check getting flakier is a defect arriving, not noise), and a full slice at zero failures **promotes to a normal check WITH A NOTE** — unexplained-vanishing flakiness is information, not relief. **A measured-intermittent check cannot serve as a regression lock:** where an item's only coverage is intermittent, the **coverage gap itself is recorded**, never papered over with a green tick.

**Class: OPERATIVE** — mechanism named: `measuredIntermittent()` in `tools/smoke.mjs`, shipped this batch, with both promotion rules and the residence clock enforced in the run output and the run summary. *(Class is C2's reading; C1 to confirm or correct.)*

**D-083 · 2026-07-29 — The feel-court press: instrument before diagnosing, and the two causes it found.**

**Class: OPERATIVE** — the mechanism is named and shipped in this same batch: the press trace, the two resolver fixes, ten unit regressions, two device checks, the `preflight()` build-identity refusal, and the corrected known-open register.

**The rule this ratifies.** *After two failed diagnoses of the same symptom, the next action is an instrument, not a third theory.* A defect that has survived multiple root-cause claims is, by that fact, a defect whose evidence is inadequate — and more reasoning over the same inadequate evidence produces more wrong answers, faster. Build the measurement that would have distinguished the failed theories, then diagnose.

**Why.** The feel court's perpendicular-travel gate read 0.38, 0.55 and 0.52 m against a 0.80 m bar across three device runs. Three root causes were published and all three were wrong: collide-and-slide (disproved by its own A/B), slide magnitude (moved the device numbers not at all), and an impassable notch (disproved by its own restaging, which then leaked and cost three more checks). Each was argued from positions sampled ~5×/second through a metric that had itself been wrong twice. From a position sample, "the tangent decayed", "the tangent was fine and it went nowhere" and "it slid and the ruler lied" are indistinguishable. One instrumented press separated all three in a single run.

**The verdict, against C1's own three hypotheses — none of them, stated plainly rather than stretched (the standing order).** The trace matched **H2's signature** (tangent high, no advance) and **none of the three mechanisms**: the tangent never decayed (2.21 m/s throughout, so not H1); the push-out anchored on live geometry every frame, 1.700–1.705 m (so not H2's mechanism); and the full-rate perpendicular was 0.51 m against the gate's 0.50 m, so the ruler was honest to a centimetre (not H3).

**What it was — two causes, stacked, both now measured rather than inferred:**

1. **The resolver judged a transient instead of the request.** "Is this contact dead-on?" is a question about direction, and the dead-on test measured the *current velocity's* angle. For ~0.2 s after any fresh press that is the accelerator still catching up, pointing where neither the old motion nor the new request does. At the reversal: the request was dead-on to **0.1°**, the velocity **31°** off-normal, prior travel 0.167 m/s one way, tangential residual 0.170 m/s the other. At 31° the branch reads "glancing" and does not fire — and the hysteresis that exists to stop exactly this **lives inside that branch**, so `priorTravel` was correct, present and never read.
2. **Contact means penetration, so the direction memory was wiped on every stick release.** A mover coasting along a curved surface leaves it by **2.5 cm** — enough to end contact while the castaway is plainly still leaning on it. This is **C3's NOTE N6**, carried IDENTIFIED-NOT-VERIFIED since the previous pass; the trace verifies it as the *second* cause under the first, not the whole story it was guessed to be.

**The fix.** `stepMovement` is told what the mover is asking for and judges the request's angle (`slideDeflectThreshold` keeps its 0.35 — against a steady velocity both readings agree exactly). The direction memory is gated on *leaning* (`contacted || nearestGapM <= slideMemoryGapM`, 0.1 m) rather than on penetration. A candidate keyed to `walkSpeedMps` was rejected on measurement: the slowest legal walk is 1.59 m/s and a partly-deflected stick is slower, so any floor scaled to walking pace would hijack deliberate slow steering along a wall.

**Device, one variable at a time:** 0.51 m / 6 reversals → 1.10 m / 4 (cause 1) → **1.70 m / 0 reversals** (both). 1.70 m is the geometric maximum for the obstacle, against a 0.80 m bar. Every guard measured unchanged: conveyor 0.925× at 10/45/80°, deliberate steering 4.54→4.56 m full-stick and 1.05→1.05 m light-stick, unbroken hold 1.70 m, the notch 376 reversals / 6.00 m net, the pin 4.38 m.

**Two standing improvements ride with it, both earned the hard way:**

- **`preflight()` now proves the server is serving THIS build**, by comparing the content-hashed entry chunk against the one `dist/index.html` names. D-056 named this gap and recommended the check; it was never actioned, and it cost this slice a run. **Mechanism corrected post-hoc, from the process table rather than by inference** — and the correction matters, because the first version of this entry implied the port was simply unavailable. It was not: a two-day-old `vite preview` from another checkout of this same project held `127.0.0.1:4173`, this worktree's preview was started on the same port **with `--strictPort` and did not refuse**, and it bound `[::1]:4173` instead. Both servers held "port 4173" simultaneously on different stacks; every request went to the stale bundle because the harness addresses `127.0.0.1`. **`--strictPort` is not proof you own a port, and "my server started fine" is not proof anything is talking to it** — the two facts that would normally reassure you both stayed true while the run was measuring the wrong code. The identity check catches it whichever stack answers. A green run against the wrong bundle is worse than no run.
- **"Isolated obstacle" is now read from the resolver's own field**, not computed from the harness's hand-copied radii for the storage box alone — which could not see a decorative tree or rock, and which is how a whole session went to a notch hypothesis.
- **The known-open register was quoting figures nothing checked.** `knownOpen()` reported the notch at 425 reversals per 960 frames (850 pre-hysteresis); re-measured, it is **376 and 845**. The register is what the director reads, and the assertions either side of it spanned `100..600` — wide enough to swallow the drift. Corrected and pinned to ±5, in a test that says to update the register in the same commit if it ever fails. The same re-measurement is this pass's cleanest proof the notch was **not** touched: identical to three decimals on reversals and net travel.

*Status: standing. Supersedes nothing; extends [[D-078]](B) and closes the gate it opened. The notch shake remains this slice's one carried known-open defect, deliberately unchanged.*

**D-082 · 2026-07-28 — Vacuity clause (d): "local is not live."** Any claim that a commit, document, or ledger entry is **live**, **landed**, or **applied** must be witnessed against **ORIGIN** — the public raw URL or the API — **never local HEAD**. Every docs or build session report must cite the **pushed SHA** and an **origin re-fetch confirmation**, not a local verification.

**Why this is a law and not a note.** Gate 0's close was reported as landed across two sessions. Every local check agreed: `git status` clean, `docs-integrity` green at 81 decisions, the ledger reading D-081 at its head. All of it true, and all of it about the wrong machine. Origin was eight commits behind at `57beb2d`, and SON — who reads the public raw URL — could see none of it. C1 caught it independently via two cache-independent paths. **A clean local tree is not evidence of anything reaching anyone.** This is the Vacuity Law's own failure mode moved up a layer: a verification that witnesses something other than its target.

**This entry is witnessed by its own rule.** The recovery push it describes, confirmed against origin before this entry was written:

- **Pushed SHA:** `8da48fcbf46609aaca3d8b8bd47573c3d2c8e4f8` (`57beb2d..8da48fc  main -> main`)
- **`git ls-remote origin main`:** `8da48fcbf46609aaca3d8b8bd47573c3d2c8e4f8	refs/heads/main`
- **Public raw URL** (`raw.githubusercontent.com/karimfz007/drift/main/docs/drift_decisions_log.md`): **HTTP 200**, 166771 bytes, head entry line read back verbatim:
  > `**D-081 · 2026-07-28 — Terminology ruling: three vocabularies, none overloading (C1).**`

Its own landing is witnessed the same way, in the report that ships it — a second push and a second origin re-fetch, quoted, not asserted.

**Amendment, found by applying this clause to itself within the hour it was written.** The raw URL is **CDN-cached and lags origin by minutes**. Immediately after D-082 was pushed as `f7b5b6e`, the three paths disagreed:

| path | saw |
|---|---|
| `git ls-remote origin main` | `f7b5b6e…` — the new commit |
| GitHub **API** (`/contents`, `?ref=main`) | 168981 bytes, **D-082 present** |
| **raw URL** | 166771 bytes — byte-identical to the *previous* fetch, **D-082 absent**, head still D-081 |

So a raw-URL read that disagrees with a push means **"not yet propagated"**, not "not pushed" — and a raw URL alone is therefore **not a sufficient liveness witness**. It must be paired with `ls-remote` or the API, and the three must be reported together when they differ. This cuts the other way too, and that is the half that matters: **C1's finding that origin was at D-078 stands regardless**, because a stale CDN can only ever show something *older* than origin, never something newer. A cached raw URL cannot invent eight commits.

**Practical note for whoever debugs this next:** `curl` on the build machine cannot reach the raw URL at all — `schannel: CRYPT_E_NO_REVOCATION_CHECK (0x80092012)`, exit 35, on every HTTPS host. `git` and PowerShell's stack both work fine. That is a local certificate-revocation setting, **not** a network outage, and not evidence about origin either way.

**Class: OPERATIVE** — mechanism named: **the origin re-fetch itself**, required in every session report and checkable by C3 against the public URL, plus the pushed SHA which C1 or SON can verify independently on any machine. A report that names no SHA and quotes no re-fetch is, under this clause, **making no claim at all**. Ratified as ops **§5 law 9 clause (d)**, ops → **v1.11**. *(Class is C2's reading; C1 to confirm or correct.)*

Resolved by separating the vocabularies:

- **LAWS** — **OPERATIVE** / **DESIGN-BINDING** ([[D-076]]), unchanged.
- **PLAN SECTIONS** — **RATIFIED** (spine binding) / **PROVISIONAL** (converting on witness). Everywhere [[D-077]] and [[D-078]] described plan sections as "OPERATIVE", read **RATIFIED**; PROVISIONAL is unchanged. **Ch.10's own text is amended identically** wherever it describes sections as OPERATIVE.
- **BUILD ARTIFACTS** — the evidence vocabulary (**VERIFIED** / **PARTIAL** / etc.), carried by the comparison sheet.

[[D-077]] and [[D-078]] **themselves, as laws, are OPERATIVE** under [[D-076]]'s law-class vocabulary — their mechanism (the ratified plan text, and the gating process) is live. **C2's self-declarations from the Gate 0 remediation are CONFIRMED in substance** — the instinct to self-declare was right, and the collision it exposed was real — and are corrected only to match this ruling's wording.

**Class: OPERATIVE** — mechanism named: **`tools/check-docs-integrity.mjs`**, which already refuses any decision after [[D-076]] that declares no class, and which accepts **only** the two law classes in a `**Class:**` line — so a section vocabulary can never again be mistaken for a law's. **D-076 · 2026-07-27 — Build law 12, the Effectivity Law** (ratified, applies from ratification, no retroactive audit required). Every ledger law and amendment must declare its **effectivity class**, explicitly, going forward:

**OPERATIVE** — in force against the live game; its satisfying mechanism is **NAMED** (a property test, a code path, an enforced check) and either already exists or lands in the same batch. **A live-game guarantee with no shipped witness is illegal under this law.** *This law's own witness is the docs-integrity check plus C3's audit reading — stated explicitly here so it does not become the first law to violate itself.*

**DESIGN-BINDING** — governs design and future builds; its trigger or slice is **NAMED**; marked explicitly as not-yet-live. The Castaway Cycle's *"nothing implements now"* ([[D-067]]) and the social-economy first law ([[D-054]]) are the canonical precedents.

**The forbidden class is the unmarked middle** — law-shaped prose that claims the present with no witness.

**Rationale:** this law, the Vacuity Law ([[D-066]]), standing hazard #4 ([[D-075]]), and the five-axis evidence status model are one discipline across four domains — **NO CLAIM WITHOUT A WITNESS.** Tests witness code; laws witness mechanisms; reports witness their own audit legs; status witnesses evidence.

**Retroactivity:** applies from ratification forward only — the 74 existing ledger entries are not audited against it, which is what makes ratification against the live ledger safe rather than invalidating.

**D-075 · 2026-07-27 — Standing hazard #4 (build law 11): "A debug hook driving a device check is not proof of a player path."**

Every player-facing capability must be device-verified **through the player's own route**: real input on the real visible control (D-053's `isVisible` standard). **Debug hooks may READ state, never DRIVE path verification.** A one-time audit of the existing harness is required — convert every hook-driven check found to a real interaction path, fail-then-pass per the Vacuity Law.

*(Named because it had already happened twice and shipped both times. D-063's experimentation checks drove `__drift.tryCombine()` directly and passed for two packages while the feature had **no player entry point whatsoever** — found only when the director played it (D-073). The same shape put D-063's loadout checks on a panel the `.inv` row never opened. A hook-driven check proves the brain works; it says nothing about whether a human can reach it.)*

**D-074 · 2026-07-27 — C3 returned FAIL on D-073; FIX 5 reverted, F2 and F4 fixed.**

**The audit was right and the finding was blocking.** Making the worn pack pickable put an opaque, camera-facing collider between the player and everything they walk up to. Both node resolvers use `scene.pick`, which returns the **nearest** pickable mesh — and a pack worn on a body drawn at the centre of a third-person view is nearer than any target. At interaction range it won the ray: `pickNode`'s exact-hit branch got the pack, and its near-miss fallback then measured from the survivor's own body, ~1.7 m out, past `nodeTapSlack` (0.9). **The gather never fired.**

| shipped harness, same machine and session | checks | failures |
|---|---|---|
| pre-fix | 32 | **0** |
| with the pack pickable | 41 | **12** |

Reeds, deadfall, rock, palm, driftwood, shellfish, berries, felling and the pond flask all broke — the game's central verb, on `main`.

**Two lessons, both mine.** My "hardening" commit did not fix it: ordering the pack *after* `pickNode` cannot help when `pickNode` is precisely what the pack broke, and its "only when topmost" safeguard **is** the failure mode rather than a guard against it. And my single-point probe passed only because that one ray happened to miss the pack — a sweep across nine verbs is what caught it. **One green probe is not evidence; C3's A/B was.**

Tapping the worn pack is still wanted. It needs a mechanism that puts **no collider in front of the camera** — a screen-space hit region over the character, or a pick predicate that excludes the body from world picks. **Parked, not abandoned.**

**F2 — the experimentation outcomes were never checked by the compiler.** `onTryCombine` tested `outcome === 'failed'`, which is not one of the five real outcomes, so **`failed-attempt`, `already-known` and `refused` were all announced as successes with the unlock cue**, and a real invention never named its plan because the field is `blueprint`, not `blueprintName`. An `as { outcome: string }` cast hid every bit of it. The cast is gone and the switch is exhaustive.

**F4 — mastery was rewarding no work.** The effect map reached `reed` and `coconutpalm`, which are tap-kind: a yield bonus for a gesture with no hold at all (measured, fibre 2 → 3 on one reed tap), while excluding `crashbox` for no stated reason. Now principled — **mastery rewards effortful work**, hold-kind only.

**Still open from the audit, not fixed here:** F3 (FIX 2 and FIX 5 shipped with no regression test — D-073's "every fix proven fail-then-pass" was **false** for those two, and FIX 5 is the proof of why that matters), F5 (the assertion cited as protecting shelter-sleep passes on a 45% nerf; the property is safe via a different pre-existing test, but the named guard is weak), and **F6 — stone now has zero ONLINE replenishment**: moving `rock` to absence-only restock means a player who never closes the app never sees stone return (measured: 1433 game hours, 0 rocks). That is a direct consequence of D-070's "restock happens out of view" ruling and needs the director's call, not a unilateral fix.

---

**D-073 · 2026-07-27 — FIX package from the live playtest: mastery's reach, experimentation's missing door, stone's depletion category, sleeping rough, and the pack as a target.**

**1. Mastery — TWO bugs, distinguished by measurement before either was fixed.** The ruling asked which of three causes it was; it was two of them. **(a) Wrong map.** Mastery read `domainForNodeKind`, which answers *"what does this verb TRAIN?"* — and Ch.2 deliberately named only felling, quarrying and salvage. Breaking surface rock therefore got **no mastery at all**, measured at `hold 1.50 → 1.50, speedMult 1.000`. A new `masteryDomainForNodeKind` covers every effortful harvesting verb while **training stays exactly as Ch.2 ruled it** — asserted explicitly, so the constitutional rule is not quietly widened by a fix to its neighbour. **(b) No feedback.** Felling's effect was real the whole time (4.00 → 2.50 s) but nothing on screen ever acknowledged it, while forging's knowledge path (experimentation's `successChanceFor`) reports its own outcome plainly. That asymmetry is precisely why a full session could end in *"mastery only affects forging"*. `gatherNode` now returns what it taught and the body says so on whole-point crossings.

| verb | novice → master | |
|---|---|---|
| fell | 4.00s → 2.50s | 37.5% faster, yield ×1.50 |
| mine surface rock | 1.50s → 0.94s | 37.5% faster, yield ×1.50 |
| quarry | 1.50s → 0.94s | 37.5% faster, yield ×1.50 |

**2. Try-Combining had no player entry point, and no cache could have explained it.** Established by reading, not by guessing at build versions: the **only** caller in the entire body layer was `runtime.tryCombine` — the *debug hook* — which is also what every D-063 device check drove. That is a **vacuous device pass** of exactly the kind D-066 exists to catch, and the **third** instance of this failure class after the Build button (D-053) and the loadout panel (D-065). The entry point now lives in the Carried panel, verified through D-065's visibility pattern rather than "the tap succeeded": chip 86×48px, button 186×56px, both `topmostIsSelf`, disabled until exactly two are picked, and a real Blueprint minted through the player path.

**3. Depletion CATEGORY, answered per node kind as the ruling asked.** Reading the render branch showed `rock`, `quarry` and `shellfish` all sharing the living-node shrink/regrow visual. **Surface rock was in the wrong category** — D-070 rules that the sea restocks stone *out of view*, narrated by the morning report, but it sat on a per-node live timer and so popped back to full size in front of the player. It now joins driftwood's absence-only tide branch and **cannot repopulate under the player's eyes**. **Shellfish is correctly on the living-node pattern** (same as bushes) — if it still feels wrong it is a regrow-*speed* TUNE question at 18 game hours, not a bug. **The quarry cannot regrow at all since D-070**, so a visibly regrowing quarry could only have been a pre-D-070 build.

**4. Sleeping rough — anywhere, always worse.** `canSleep` no longer requires a shelter; `isShelteredSleep` carries the distinction that used to be a gate. Recovery is scaled by `groundSleepRecoveryMultiplier` and the weather half needed **no new machinery at all**, because a survivor who is not under a roof already interacts with wet/warmth normally. Measured: **84.0 energy sheltered vs 46.2 rough** over one full sleep. Shelter-sleep's own rates are untouched, asserted explicitly. It needed a door too — Rest sits on the Build card, and a device probe caught it rendering *below the 412 px fold* once the card had grown, so it was moved to the top.

**5. The pack on the survivor's back is a real pick target**, with its own metadata, opening the same panel the HUD icon opens. Additive as asked — the icon still works — and the player capsule itself stays unpickable so the body never eats world taps.

*(Every fix proven fail-then-pass per D-066(b). Two pre-existing tests that encoded the old "sleep requires a shelter" law were rewritten to the new one rather than deleted, so a silent revert would still be caught.)*

**D-072 · 2026-07-27 — Standing hazard #3: bench isolation — "separate ports are not isolation; separate times are."**

The **CPU-contention diagnosis carried since [[D-051]] is formally RETIRED**, refuted by direct measurement: **8% CPU** with **memory/buffer starvation on page navigation** (`ERR_NO_BUFFER_SPACE` raised from inside the page), not contention. Every crash previously blamed on a loaded machine was a `page.goto`/`waitForScene` stall with that signature.

The mechanism was ours all along: `preflight()` ran a blanket `taskkill /F /IM chrome.exe`, so a second harness starting up **silently killed the first one's browser mid-run**. Separate ports never isolated anything.

**`preflight()` is rewritten, not merely documented:** it kills only processes this run owns (the browser it launched, torn down on any exit path), never a blanket kill; and **concurrent harness runs are forbidden** — a live bench lock makes the second run **refuse to start** rather than silently killing the first. Both paths verified by hand: a live PID refuses, a stale lock is cleared and the run proceeds.

**COROLLARY — the amnesty is withdrawn.** The two harness misses previously waved through as "known machine-specific" (**quarry three-taps**, **fast-movement**) **lose that status pending one re-evaluation under the corrected cause.** Do not re-apply the old amnesty without re-confirming it under *this* diagnosis specifically. A wrong cause that excuses a failure is worse than no cause: it retires the question.

**D-070 · 2026-07-27 — GEOLOGY V2: a finite seam, and stone that returns on the tide (Gate 0 item 7).** Verified never previously done — no ledger entry existed, so it was treated as not done, per the ruling's own instruction.

**Two tiers, and the difference between them is the point.** The **quarry is now genuinely finite**: its regrow interval is `Infinity`, reconcile no longer refills its pool, and a seam worked to nothing stays nothing. It visibly empties as you mine it — the pool was already there, it simply never comes back now. **Surface stone stays renewable**, and is reframed as what it physically is: the sea working the shingle over. That gets **its own morning-report line** — *"The sea has turned the shingle over. There is loose stone on the shore again."* — because the island must never silently repopulate itself in view. If a resource came back, the report says so.

**D-051 is not breached, and the law is now stated more precisely.** What may never be globally exhausted is a survival-critical **resource**, not every individual **deposit**. A regression proves it rather than asserting it: mine the seam to nothing, wait a lifetime of game hours, confirm it never returns, then confirm stone is *still* obtainable from surface rock. If that test ever fails, the finite tier has breached the law and must be reverted.

**Fail-then-pass (D-066 b):** with the quarry's regrow interval restored, **3 tests fail**; with the finite tier in place, 16 pass. The old "regrows to full capacity, not partially" assertion was replaced, not deleted — it now asserts the opposite, which is the honest form of a deliberate behaviour change.

*(Scope note: implemented by making the EXISTING quarry the finite tier rather than adding a new `vein` node kind. The spec named either — "the quarry/a rich vein" — and the quarry is already pool-backed, already visibly depletes, and needs no world placement, render path, or save migration. A new kind would have been more code and more risk for the same rule.)*

**D-068 · 2026-07-27 — THE SURVIVOR'S JOURNAL (director's design; the Castaway Cycle's keystone).** Register + Ch.10 slice input. **Nothing implements now.** A craftable journal — materials + a writing tool + light + **TIME [TUNE]**. The player *actively records* what the survivor actually did and knew; **unwritten experience DIES with the survivor.** This is Law 22 (knowledge exists in a carrier) with the player holding the pen: the game never auto-journals mastery, because a journal that writes itself is exactly the account-wide XP screen the Cycle exists to refuse. A successor reads it through the **D-056 found-content channel** — study costs time, and comprehension is gated by the reader's own relevant understanding; below the gate entries are only partially usable and **become goals** rather than instant capability. Re-learning from your ancestor (yourself) is accelerated, never instant. **Journals are mortal carriers:** storage quality, damp, fire and decay govern whether one survives across lives, so "kept somewhere safe" is real play — and the shipped storage/preservation systems are already its substrate. Rails: honest-systems (no monetized memory), D-011 offline protection exactly as for any other secured property, and morning-report material ("you spent the evening writing by the fire").

The player's **Spawn Island persists with everything physical upon it.** The next castaway is **a new arrival, not a resurrection** (v0_11 Law 23), inheriting **matter, not memory** (Law 22). **Privacy is architectural** (Law 24) — the island cannot be entered by another living person, which is why it is safe to make it the only re-entry point. Requalification follows v0_9 §5.4: the world can shorten ignorance through manuals, jigs, seed archives and training rigs; it can never hand over the dead person's hands, eye or judgment. **The D-052 interim respawn stands exactly as-is and is scheduled for replacement in the Cycle's own slice under Ch.10.** Nothing implements this pass.

**D-066 · 2026-07-27 — The Vacuity Law (standing hazard, ratified).** *"A test must witness its target."* **(a)** Every property test must assert that its guarded branch actually fired — either a minimum hit count across the tested corpus, or constructed cases that force it; a branch that never fires **FAILS** the test as vacuous, it does not pass by default. **(b)** Every regression test born from a live, reported defect must first be proven to **FAIL** against the pre-fix tree before it is trusted — D-065's A/B practice, now law. **(c)** Every KEY REPORT states explicitly, per package, which audit legs genuinely ran — static, unit, device — with **no leg implied that did not happen.** Tracked as a standing item alongside the worktree-destruction hazard, not absorbed per-occurrence.

*(Ratified because this project had already shipped three vacuous passes and not noticed: D-063's device-leg loadout checks passed on a panel the `.inv` row never opened (`occluded`), D-064's `!isPlaceablePoint` branch fired **0 times across the 500 seeds its property test swept**, and D-065's own "the game REMAINS RESPONSIVE" check would have passed on the shipped freeze because `realTapDom` hit-tests by selector and `opacity: 0` is still hit-testable. In all three the test was green and the defect was real. Remediation for (a) and (b) shipped with this entry; see the D-066 as-built.)*

**D-065 · 2026-07-27 — The freeze: an invisible panel is not a missing panel. Plus the two hit-test reports, and the entry points the director actually looked for.**
Reported as a total input freeze — nothing responds, Settings included, while the game clock keeps advancing. The ruling required the mechanism confirmed, not assumed, and specifically asked whether the D-063 `beginPanel`/`endPanel` pair was being bypassed or whether the OPEN path was throwing before control transferred. **Neither.** Nothing threw and nothing was bypassed. `.panel` is `opacity: 0` until the `visible` class is added, and every panel added it at its own call site; `showLoadout` was the one that did not. So the panel WAS created — `inset: 0`, `pointer-events: auto`, full-screen — and was **completely transparent**. It swallowed every tap, `panelOpen` made every other panel's guard (Settings included) refuse to open, and the render loop kept ticking. The player sees the world, taps, nothing happens, time passes. Reproduced empirically before any fix: `opacity: 0`, panel present, close button real and in-viewport at top 352 — invisible, so unfindable. After the fix, `opacity: 1`.

**The invariant now has one owner.** The reveal moved into `panel()` itself and the five duplicate call sites were deleted. "Every panel must remember to reveal itself" was exactly the rule that failed, so a per-call-site duty was the wrong shape for it.

**The backstop, because the class is worse than the instance.** `beginPanel` commits the control transfer *before* the panel is proven to be on screen, so anything failing after that line locks the player out with no way back but a reload — on a permanent-death game, a safety failure. `guardPanelLock` now runs each frame: if control is held with no *visible* panel for a continuous second, the game removes the stuck panels, takes control back, and logs loudly (D-049). `runtime.panelRecoveries` counts every time it fires and the harness fails if it is not zero — the recovery keeps the player playing, the counter makes the defect impossible to ship quietly. `openLoadout`'s open path is additionally wrapped so a throw returns control immediately.

**Why the D-063 checks passed over this.** They asserted the panel was in the DOM and that its buttons answered a scripted tap. Both are true of an invisible panel. The new checks assert what a player needs: computed opacity above 0.5, and — after closing — that **Settings still opens**, which is the exact thing the director found dead.

**The storage box: not a hit-test bug.** D-051's nearest-centre-wins sort is intact and storage resolves correctly. The defect was priority. `canRepairStructure` answers "may I mend this?"; it was being used to answer "should mending take this tap?". Repair applies below 90% durability and decay is 1 per game hour from 100, so **ten game hours after building — and forever after — every tap by a player carrying wood repaired**. "+15 durability instead of opening contents" was that, exactly. Same starvation shape as flask-fill hidden behind drink (FIX 2) and Build-fire behind Craft-axe (C03). New `repairIsUrgent` gates pre-emption on the structure actually failing (`structureRepairUrgentFraction`, 0.4); below that, mending is available but never steals the tap.

**The shelter: the interactive area was not the visible object.** Its only pickable mesh was the roof slab — 0.18 m thick, tilted, nearly no screen area from most angles. Both poles, the tall obvious part of the silhouette, were `isPickable = false`, so taps on them passed through to the terrain BEHIND the shelter, outside the forgiveness radius, and resolved to nothing. Sleep could only be triggered from the one patch of ground where the ray happened to land near the centre — "one specific spot", precisely. Every child mesh now carries the shelter's own metadata. Same family as the Build-button visibility gap.

**The entry points the director expected from the start.** Carried items open from a **drawn backpack** with its live load beneath it, not a button lettered "Carried" — the object it stood for was nowhere on screen, which is why it was never found. Storage opens from **tapping the storage box**, and storing, taking and mending are named choices made with the contents in front of you, rather than one verb the game picked for you silently.

**Also folded in:** C3's D-063 finding **A1** — `openDeath` was the one panel that never joined the pair, inlining `panelOpen = true` and omitting `cancelHold()`, so a gather hold survived under the death card. It now goes through `beginPanel`. No death-MODEL change: what dying costs and how respawn works are untouched, per the standing constraint.

**Device harness: 191/193, run unpiped to a file and genuinely completed** — the first completed run in three sessions. Both remaining failures (quarry repeat-mining, fast-movement) were shown to predate this package by checking out the whole pre-D-065 tree, source *and* its contemporary harness, and running it: **168/178 with byte-identical failure strings**. Every URGENT check passes, including the panel being genuinely visible (`opacity 1`, covers screen), the game still responding afterwards, `panelRecoveries 0`, the storage box opening without repairing (`durability 83.2 → 83.2, wood 6`), and the shelter tappable across **17/17 sampled offsets up to 160 px above its base**.

**Harness checks that encoded the old behaviour were rewritten, not deleted** — storage deposit/withdraw now go through the opened box, and the shelter repair check moved to genuinely failing durability, with a new check that a merely *worn* shelter still sleeps. Two long-running property tests (offline-death, knowledge-never-decays) were failing the full suite as 5 s **timeouts**, which reads at a glance as the law breaking; they take ~3.4 s alone, so the budget moved to 30 s and the iteration counts — which are the strength of the properties — did not.

**AUDIT (D-065):** *(C3, 2026-07-27, main checkout at `c04f140` — no filesystem isolation, stated as an accepted tradeoff; tree clean and unmoved throughout. Device-level confirmation is C2's this pass — I did not run the harness.)* **PASS-WITH-NOTES.** **All three diagnoses re-derive and are the real causes.** `.panel` is `inset: 0` + `pointer-events: auto` + `opacity: 0` (`index.html:316-341`, `:358`), and `git show c04f140^:src/body/hud.ts` confirms `showLoadout` was the **only** one of six panels without `classList.add('visible')`; `panel()` (`hud.ts:238-245`) is now the sole creator of `.panel` in the tree, so no call site can skip the reveal, and the two toasts that keep their own (`hud.ts:414`, `:424`) are not `panel()` products. `openDeath` genuinely joins the pair (`game.ts:1476`) — finding **A1** closed — and the whole `src/brain/` diff is **+17 lines, all `repairIsUrgent`**: the death model is untouched. Poles really were `isPickable = false`, `getChildMeshes()` is recursive so it covers them and the grade mark, and `pickHitPoint` (`game.ts:496-499`) collapses any shelter-tagged hit to the shelter's own centre, so tagging children cannot mis-resolve. Storage was never a hit-test bug — crate *and* lid were already pickable (`entities.ts:920-928`). `vitals.test.ts` is untouched except `}, 30_000)`: iteration counts 3000/2000 and every assertion intact. Fresh: typecheck ✓, purity ✓ 16/18, docs-integrity ✓ 65, **343/343 across 19 files**. **Findings.** **C1 (behavioural):** `tryRepair('shelter')` has exactly **one** call site (`game.ts:689`), gated on `repairIsUrgent`, and the shelter got no Mend button — so between 40% and 90% durability the shelter **cannot be mended by any input**, contradicting `state.ts:911-912`'s "above that it stays reachable"; since repair adds +15 and urgency needs <40, a shelter that once decayed past 40 is **permanently capped at ~55/100**. **C2:** the starvation shape survives below 40 — with wood in hand, up to two consecutive taps still repair instead of sleeping. **C3:** `openLoadout`'s `catch` (`game.ts:444-447`) calls `endPanel()` but never removes the panel `showLoadout` may already have appended, and `guardPanelLock` returns on its first line when `panelOpen` is false (`game.ts:353`) — so the backstop is structurally blind to the exact state its own try/catch can create. **C4 (harness):** the new shelter-BODY check (`smoke.mjs:884-897`) reads `__drift.pending()` ~390 ms after tapping a target the player is already ≤1.75 m from; `stepInteraction` nulls the pending on the next frame (`game.ts:1242`, `:691`), so it will read `null` and **fail for a reason unrelated to the fix**. Precision notes: **"nine game hours" is ten** (built at 100, decay 1/h, `durability < 90` strict); `smoke.mjs:1971` "the game REMAINS RESPONSIVE" **would have passed on the shipped bug**, because `realTapDom` hit-tests by selector and `opacity: 0` does not remove an element from hit testing — only the opacity probe at `:1958` catches the defect; 40 px above the base lands ~0.4 m up a 2.1 m pole and the old ray would still have struck terrain inside the 2.8 m forgiveness radius; `panelRecoveries` is reset by every `editSave` reload; `paintBackpackLoad` runs a `querySelector` every frame against the file's own caching discipline. The three harness rewrites judged individually — repair 40→22, storage-through-the-box, fail-loud box→pond — **preserve their originals' intent and two of them strengthen it**; the pond substitute was verified sound down to `thirstMax`/`flaskCapacitySips`. Full reasoning and file:line in `drift_cycle_log.md`.

**D-064 · 2026-07-27 — Reachability, third strike: the CLASS fix, and a diegetic boundary.**
The ruling was explicit that this must not be another coordinate patch — root-cause why D-052's own harness check passes cases a real player cannot reach.

**ROOT CAUSE, two compounding halves, neither a coordinate bug.**

**(a) The spawn validator models the island as a bare disc.** `isWalkablePoint` is only `hypot(x,z) <= WALKABLE_RADIUS`. It is correct about the waterline and knows **nothing** about the ~101 decorative trees and 14 decorative rocks that `island.ts` pushes into `staticObstacles` as real collision bodies. A find could therefore spawn hard against a boulder, be perfectly "walkable" by the arithmetic, and be physically uncollectable — the player's own collision push-out holds them further from it than they can reach.

**This is D-051's quarry arithmetic, never applied to spawns.** D-051 banked `objectCollisionRadius + playerCollisionRadius < interactRadiusM` as a standing constraint and it was only ever enforced for the one object that had already broken. Computed against the real decorative-rock sizes: **4 of the 8 distinct sizes violate it** — a size-2.1 rock has a 2.94 m collision radius, so the nearest legal standing point is 3.34 m from its centre against a 2.5 m reach. Sizes 1.6, 1.7, 1.8 and 2.1 all strand; 1.2–1.5 do not.

**(b) The harness check was a distance assertion, not a reachability test.** It computed `hypot(node) > walkableRadiusM` from a fixed origin and called the result "path-reachable". It never walked, never collected, and was **structurally incapable** of failing for cause (a) no matter how badly a find was stranded. It verified that a path exists on paper — which is exactly the phrase the ruling used.

**THE FIX, at the class rather than the instance.** New `isPlaceablePoint` (`src/data/world.ts`) enforces the D-051 constraint against **every** obstacle, not just the one that broke: a point is placeable only if a player can both stand near it and still reach it. `spawnSalvageNode` now validates against it. A blocked spawn **walks the ring in fixed arc steps** and takes the first genuinely placeable point, rather than the old fallback of collapsing to the island centre — a beach find teleporting inland is its own kind of wrong. Deterministic and seeded throughout. Any future procedural placement gets the same guarantee by calling `isPlaceablePoint` instead of `isWalkablePoint`.

**THE BOUNDARY IS NOW DIEGETIC.** The walkable edge was an invisible wall discovered by walking into it. It is now a band of pale surf drawn at `SURF_LINE_RADIUS` — which **is** `WALKABLE_RADIUS`, exported from the same module so the rule and its picture cannot drift apart. The thing you see is literally the thing that stops you. Drawn as a torus so it reads as water breaking on a shelf rather than a fence, and `isPickable: false` so it can never intercept a tap meant for the beach behind it (D-049's lesson about invisible geometry eating taps).

**THE HARNESS CHECK REWRITTEN TO ACTUALLY DO THE THING.** It now forces a spawn against the largest decorative rock — the exact shape the old check waved through — then **walks to it and collects it**, asserting the castaway ended within reach and that the loot genuinely landed in the inventory. Nothing short of that counts as reachable. A 200-seed placement sweep runs beside it through the real shipped validator (`__drift.spawnSalvage` / `__drift.isPlaceable`), so the harness exercises the shipped rule rather than re-deriving it.

**Regression-locked:** `tests/reachability.test.ts` (12 new) — including the one that demonstrates the bug directly: **a point the OLD check accepts and the NEW one correctly rejects**, sited on the largest decorative rock. Plus the D-051 constraint restated as arithmetic, stranding rocks proven to exist, a 500-seed property test that every spawn is placeable, the ring-walk fallback proven never to dump a find at the island centre, determinism, and `SURF_LINE_RADIUS === WALKABLE_RADIUS`.

**AUDIT (D-064):** *(C3, 2026-07-27, main checkout at `fe34f01` — no filesystem isolation, stated as an accepted tradeoff.)* **PASS-WITH-NOTES.** **The rock arithmetic was re-derived independently before reading the fix and reproduces this entry's figures exactly: 4 of the 8 distinct sizes (1.6/1.7/1.8/2.1) exceed `objectR + playerR < interactR`; size 2.1 gives a 2.94 m collision radius and a 3.34 m nearest standing point against a 2.5 m reach; 4 of the 14 placed rocks strand.** Size 1.5 sits exactly on the constraint (2.10 + 0.40 = 2.50) and clears only by float. `isPlaceablePoint`'s geometry is derived correctly, `island.ts:376` and `world.ts:287` are an exact mirror so collider and validator cannot disagree, `SURF_LINE_RADIUS` is an *alias* of `WALKABLE_RADIUS` so the picture and the rule cannot drift, and the surf torus is `isPickable: false`. The diagnosis and the fix are both right. **The evidence is not.** **B1:** replicating `spawnSalvageNode`'s own numerics, the `!isPlaceablePoint` branch is taken **0 times across seeds 0–499** — the exact range the property test uses — so the ring walk and the centre fallback have **zero coverage** and the test would stay green if the whole block were deleted. Real incidence is 28 blocked spawns in 200,000 seeds (0.014%, first at seed **6384**); only one rock, `[-52, 84, 1.8]`, lies in the 96–105 m spawn annulus at all. **B2:** the harness does **not** force a spawn against the largest rock — `smoke.mjs:1274` calls `spawnSalvage(3)`, which lands at (99, 22), **80.06 m** from that rock; its "landed somewhere placeable" check asserts only `Boolean(node)` and never calls `isPlaceable`; and its walk tolerance (`interactRadiusM + 1.5` = 4.00 m) is wider than the worst stranding standoff (3.34 m) it exists to detect. B3/B4/B5 are precision notes (the tree loop is provably inert; the centre fallback still exists; only static obstacles are covered). Full reasoning and file:line in `drift_cycle_log.md`.

*(**Both the D-063 and D-064 audits are static-plus-unit only.** Two harness attempts on my own port 4191 — never the builder's 4185, identity verified first — crashed on the documented `editSave`/`waitForScene` 60 s timeout under CPU contention, at `smoke.mjs:1238` and `:901` respectively, **neither reaching the D-064 checks at `:1253-1314` nor the D-063 section at `:1834`**. 103/3 and 63/0 before the crashes; the misses were the known wall-clock-sensitive pair plus one of the same class. No device-level confirmation was obtained for either package — see the addendum in `drift_cycle_log.md` for exactly what that leaves unverified.)*

**D-063 · 2026-07-26 — Gate 0 items 1, 2 and 4: embodied inventory, equip/switch, and Try-Combining with Blueprints (v0_7 §9/§10.5/§10.6).**
Built against the verbatim v0_7 sections relayed this pass, which closed the D-046(b) gap that had blocked these two items. **Process note worth recording: this was the fourth occurrence of content being referenced but not travelling** — the batch's own reception instruction, Ch.6's chapter text, v0_11's laws, and these sections. D-060's numbering-hygiene law generalises to exactly this: an artifact that only exists in conversation does not exist. All four were eventually resolved by relaying the text, which is the process working, just late.

**ITEM 1 — EMBODIED INVENTORY (§9).** Six access zones as a real model (`src/brain/loadout.ts`): activeHand / supportHand / belt / pockets / backpack / storage. Hands, belt and pockets hold **specific items by position** — §9's "hotkeys are physical references", not queries against a bag. **The law that follows, and the one regression-locked hardest: a consumed, moved, broken, dropped or stolen item leaves its position EMPTY, and it is never silently refilled from storage.** Enforced at a single point (`syncLoadoutToOwnership`) and wired to the live case — a torch burning out inside `reconcile` now clears the hand or belt slot it occupied, proven through the real burn-down path rather than by hand.

Load is **mass + bulk**, both surfaced. Bulk is genuinely tracked and gates pocket eligibility (the axe and hammer are deliberately too bulky for a pocket; the flask and a knapped blade are not), but it **deliberately does NOT feed the load bands** — §9 is explicit that the bands stay Ch.6's ("UI on top of that, not a new system"), and a test pins that two loads of equal mass but very different bulk band identically. Storage contents are **inspectable in place**, without hauling anything out, which unparks the C05 storage-contents note; carried and stored are never merged in the view, so you can always tell what is on you from what is at home.

**ITEM 2 — EQUIP/SWITCH.** Fell out of §9's hands zone rather than needing a separate system, so it is included here: choosing a tool puts it in the active hand and it is worn on the character (the axe's original carriage precedent, D-046(d)). The **two-handed rule** is real — the axe and stone hammer occupy the support hand as well, displacing whatever was there. Displaced, never destroyed: it returns to general carry and the result reports what moved.

**ITEM 4 — TRY-COMBINING + BLUEPRINTS (§10.6).** **No invented recipes**, exactly as §10.6 demands ("a bounded design question, not arbitrary procedural crafting"): a pair is valid only if the existing Ch.1 tree already says the two materials satisfy **two different slots of one recipe**. Two materials that fill the *same* slot are alternatives, not partners, and invent nothing. Everything else is a null outcome routed through **D-055's own journal** and **Ch.2's own evaluator** — no parallel system anywhere, per the ruling.

An attempt **costs energy, game time, hunger and thirst on every real try, win or lose**, which is what stops brute-force enumeration being free. A pair already journalled as a dead end costs nothing — but only because the answer is already known, never because trying is cheap; that distinction is tested directly. The **confidence curve reads Ch.2's Technique score**, so practice compounds through the one progression the game already has: success chance and speed both climb, and success is capped below certainty so an experiment is never a formality. A success mints a **named Blueprint** recording inputs, authorship, version and workmanship; re-deriving a plan you already hold **bumps its version rather than duplicating it** (§10.5's versioning). Workmanship is recorded as evidence of that prototype and **never inherited** by a later maker — §10.6's "reproduces the relationships, not the original quality for free". Minting is deterministic (the seeded-hash technique `rollGrade`/`deadfallYield` already use), never `Math.random()`.

**INPUT SAFETY (§9), treated as HARD LAW.** The source's own framing is that "permanent death makes input reliability a safety requirement", so this was built as a law and not polish. Every panel now opens and closes through a single `beginPanel`/`endPanel` pair, so no panel added later can forget a step: opening takes pointer/camera ownership, cancels every in-flight gesture, and drops any pending intention or running hold. **The actual gap closed: control is no longer restored on the closing input itself.** The previous code cleared `panelOpen` inside each close handler, which runs while the closing gesture is still in flight — so the close button's own release could fall straight through to the world and fire a tap at whatever sat behind it. `panelOpen` now stays true until that pointer has genuinely released. Every pre-existing panel (cold open, morning report, settings, build card, death) was migrated onto the pair; the only remaining direct write is inside `endPanel` itself.

**Save schema bumped v9→v10** (`migrateV9toV10`): a returning player gets an **empty loadout and no plans**. Every tool stays owned and sits in general carry — exactly where it effectively was before positions existed. Inventing positions would fabricate a history the save has no record of, and granting blueprints for things they already knew how to make would fabricate a discovery they never made.

**Regression-locked:** `tests/loadout.test.ts` (32 new) — the six zones, the two-handed rule, belt/pocket assignment and drawing, pocket bulk gating, one-object-one-position, the consumed-item-empties-its-position law proven through the real reconcile burn-down, mass/bulk both surfaced, bulk NOT driving the bands, storage inspectable without withdrawing, and the v9→v10 migration. `tests/experiment.test.ts` (25 new) — relationships coming only from the Ch.1 tree, costs charged win or lose, the free-only-because-known short-circuit, journal-and-evaluator routing with Understanding-but-not-Technique on a null, named-plan minting with versioning, determinism, and the confidence curve in both its success and speed halves. 328 tests green overall.

**AUDIT (D-063):** *(C3, 2026-07-27, main checkout at `fe34f01` — no filesystem isolation, stated as an accepted tradeoff.)* **PASS-WITH-NOTES.** THE LAW verified the sceptical way and it holds: one enforcement point (`syncLoadoutToOwnership`), wired live at `reconcile.ts:236`, and the regression genuinely drives `reconcile(…)` rather than clearing a slot by hand. So do no-`Math.random`-in-the-brain (zero hits in `src/brain/`), the single progression path (`technique`/`understanding` written at exactly two lines, `knowledge.ts:177-178`), never-inherited workmanship, version-bump-not-duplicate, the v9→v10 migration, and the specific input-safety fix — `runtime.panelOpen = false` occurs **exactly once in the tree**, inside a deferred `endPanel`, and world taps fire from an earlier `pointerup` than any close button's `click`. Five findings, two behavioural: **A1** the death panel never actually joined the pair (`game.ts:1383-1388` inlines `panelOpen = true` and omits `cancelHold()`), contradicting this entry's "every pre-existing panel … migrated" and "the only remaining direct write is inside `endPanel`"; **A2** nothing in `src/` can ever put a tool in `supportHand`, so the two-handed displacement rule is unreachable and its test sets the precondition by hand; **A3** `assignToBelt`/`assignToPocket`/`drawFromBelt` have no UI call sites; **A4** `hash32(0) === 0`, so the first experiment of every new game succeeds with certainty despite the 0.95 cap; **A5** three locks pass for reasons other than the ones they name (notably the bulk-vs-bands test, whose two states differ in mass and which passes under the very counterfactual it excludes). Full reasoning and file:line in `drift_cycle_log.md`.

**D-062 · 2026-07-26 — Final reception: v0_12 "The Living Hunt."** Rails: populations-not-loot ADOPTED; renewability law refined — fauna may be locally exhausted or restored, survival floor guaranteed by method diversity (no single species is survival-critical); offline fauna action vs. player property under D-011 caps; predator content through the fair-challenge contract + five-stage ladder; whole-use processing as honest-systems framing; community ownership/justice sections = Ch.8 input; braids/rosters = coverage taxonomies; implementation order reconciles into the Master Plan. CORPUS CLOSED at nine documents (v0_4-v0_12). From this entry forward, new design enters only as register amendments or parked notes unless the director explicitly reopens the shelf.

*(Applied verbatim as supplied. **Register note — SUPERSEDED 2026-07-27:** when written, none of v0_4–v0_12 were filed in this repo, so the entry recorded the reception while the sources stayed uncommitted. **v0_7 through v0_12 are now filed at `/docs/reference/`, byte-identical to the director's originals**, which lifts the ops v1.7 §4 law (b) build gate for those six. **v0_4, v0_5 and v0_6 remain unfiled and still gated.** The one rail with a live code consequence is the renewability refinement: the existing law (D-051, "no survival-critical resource is globally exhaustible") is **unchanged for flora and minerals** and is narrowed only for fauna, where the survival floor moves from per-species to method diversity — that has no effect on anything currently implemented, since no fauna exist in the build yet.)*

**D-061 · 2026-07-26 — Laws 19-30 (v0_11 §4) RATIFIED as design law, C1 shapings binding:** Law 24 ratified as the ARCHITECTURAL implementation of D-054's first law (private Spawn Island physically unenterable by living players; succession-access design routes to the Castaway Cycle trunk). The §22.2 public-dock nuance LAW-GATED to Ch.8 (world forces within D-011 caps; player-inflicted loss during absence floored at zero in default modes). Law 28 registered as D-011 restated — same law, our number. Doc-series law numbers map through the register; the charter remains supreme.

Law 24's and Law 28's substance is pinned here by their mapping onto D-054 and D-011 respectively, both of which are committed and resolve.)*

**D-060 · 2026-07-26 — Chapter-numbering correction (C1's own process error), and the hygiene law that prevents its recurrence.**
**The correction.** Canon read **Ch.9 = slicing** because C1's Way-Home renumber was decided at the chat layer and **never shipped as a delta**. C1 records this as its own process error, not SON's flag being wrong. **Definitive numbering, now shipped:** **Ch.9 = "The Way Home"** (endings & maritime; source v0_6/v0_7 §15) · **Ch.10 = The Master Plan** (slicing), always last.

**Why it went undetected for three passes:** every artifact that referenced the number — D-054's own ratification line, the ops v1.6 waypoint table, §4's law (c), the state tracker — was internally consistent with the *stale* value, so nothing contradicted anything. Consistency is not correctness when every copy descends from the same unshipped decision. There was no mechanism by which a chat-layer renumber could ever reach the repo on its own.

**The hygiene law (ops v1.6 → v1.7, §2):** (1) `drift_state.md`'s Dossier tracker is the **single source of truth** for chapter numbering — not the charter, not a ledger entry, not anyone's memory; where the tracker and a conversation disagree, the tracker is right by definition. (2) **A renumber is only real once its delta ships in the same breath as the decree** — chat-layer intent counts for nothing. This is §4's law (b) ("a handoff may never reference spec content that isn't committed") generalised from specs to numbering: **an artifact that only exists in conversation does not exist.**

**Applied this pass:** ops → **v1.7** (v1.6 archived byte-identical), with the C1-blocking list now reading "the Master Plan (Ch.10), and Ch.9 'The Way Home'" in both §3's waypoint table and §4's law (c); the state tracker gains explicit Ch.9 and Ch.10 rows; this ledger entry records the correction and its cause. **Nothing about routing or verification changed** — this is a renaming plus a new hygiene rule, and the D-057 waypoint model is otherwise untouched.

**D-059 · 2026-07-26 — Three director-reported FIX items: tree parity, exhaustion with teeth, carry weight that scales.**
FIX-tier long session under D-057's doctrine. All three reports were real; each was root-caused before anything was changed, as the ruling required.

1. **TREE PARITY — the same disease D-051 cured once, still live for trees.** Measured before touching anything rather than guessing a target: **rocks are 3 real nodes against 14 decorative meshes (3:14, 17.6% of all rock objects real); trees were 5 against 101 — 4.7%.** So a tap on any of ~96 visually identical trees fell silently through to the terrain, which is D-051's own root cause (a decorative instance is `isPickable: false`, and the fail-loud law is structurally blind to a pure miss, never seeing a hit on anything unrecognised). **Fixed by promoting 14 authored treeline spots to real nodes — 19 real against 87 decorative, 17.9%**, the closest whole-tree match to the rock ratio (18 would give 17.0%, further off). Promotion walks the spot list on a fixed stride rather than taking a contiguous block, so harvestable trees are spread across all four quadrants and a player never has to learn which arc is the "real" forest. **Content scope, not a new system:** these are ordinary `tree` nodes, inheriting the blaze mark, the axe gate, stump/sapling depletion and 96-hour regrowth exactly as tr1–tr5 always did; a promoted spot is removed from the decorative list so no position is ever drawn twice. **Render cost, reported rather than assumed** (the ruling asked for the number if the impact is real): a decorative tree is one of ~101 thin-instances batching into 2 draw calls total, while a real tree node is 3 individual meshes (trunk, canopy, blaze mark), so promoting 14 costs **+42 meshes and +28 pickable targets** — the latter mattering twice over, since every pickable mesh is work for each interaction raycast. The A3 p95 frame-time budget stays law and is gated by the harness's own pre-existing checks; a live `renderCost()` probe now reports the actual scene counts every run so this can never degrade silently.

2. **EXHAUSTION HAD NO TEETH — root cause confirmed exactly as the ruling suspected, not assumed.** `nodeHoldSeconds` read skill level and axe grade only; `isExhausted` had exactly **three** consumers in the entire codebase, all in `game.ts` — the movement speed scale and two lines of hint text. **Gathering was never wired to energy at all**, so mining at 0 energy was byte-identical to mining at 100. Fixed with `exhaustionHoldMultiplierFor`, applied at the single place hold duration is computed, so no gather verb added later can silently escape it. Reuses the load-band template as instructed — a multiplier on the SAME stat skill and grade already scale, not a second parallel notion of "slower." It is a **gradient, not a cliff**: 1 while energy is healthy, `exhaustedHoldMultiplier` (1.8) at the low threshold, interpolating to `collapsedHoldMultiplier` (2.6) at empty, so "nearly spent" and "utterly spent" differ. Never blocks the action and never shrinks the yield — C05's rule that low energy is a soft debuff, never a wall.

3. **CARRY WEIGHT NOT REGISTERING AT SCALE — root cause identified among the three candidates the ruling named.** **(a) is FALSE:** stone has a real configured mass (2.0 kg/unit), not a near-zero default. **(b) is FALSE as stated:** the thresholds are calibrated fine and easily reachable — 15 stone already hits Working, 16 hits Heavy. **(c) is TRUE, and is compounded by a fourth cause the ruling did not list.** There is **no inventory cap anywhere**, so 100 was always reachable; and **the three bands SATURATE** — `loadHeavyAtKg` is 30, so 16 stone (32 kg) and 100 stone (200 kg) both read `heavy` and were byte-identical, ×0.7 speed and ×1.6 energy. On top of that, **carry weight was not surfaced anywhere in the body layer**, so a player had no readout to notice a band change even when one happened. "100 rock produced no observable effect" was therefore literally true: it behaved exactly as 16 rock, and nothing on screen said otherwise. Fixed by adding a continuous overload term past the Heavy threshold (weight-aware multipliers replacing band-aware ones at both consumers), plus an HUD chip that appears only once genuinely overloaded. **The bands below Heavy are untouched**, so everything Ch.6 tuned and tested under 30 kg behaves exactly as it did. **`loadOverloadSpeedFloor` is a safety rail, not a tuning knob:** a castaway must always be able to walk home and put the load down, so no weight — 200 kg, 1000 kg, 100 tonnes — can ever approach a soft-lock; property-checked directly.

**Save schema bumped v8→v9** (`migrateV8toV9`): the promoted trees are merged into an existing save's node list. Without this a returning player would keep their five-tree island forever, because `hydrate` deliberately preserves a save's own `nodes` (that is what remembers which nodes you depleted). The same "new content is merged in, nothing existing is disturbed" shape v3→v4 used for the quarry — **adds only**: every pre-existing node survives byte-identical, including a tree felled an hour ago, which stays felled and keeps counting down.

**Regression-locked, and proven fail-then-pass.** `tests/parity.test.ts` (new, 19 tests) covers all three items plus the migration; `tests/construction.test.ts` gained the saturation regression and the soft-lock rail; `tests/body.test.ts` gained overload-step and bands-untouched coverage. **All three fixes were then temporarily reverted in place and the suite re-run: 11 tests failed across all three items, then passed again on restore** — so these tests demonstrably catch the reported bugs rather than merely describing the new code. Device harness gained a "D-059" section: the real-tree count, a promoted tree actually felled by a real tap for real timber, the live render-cost readout, a timed rested-versus-spent mining comparison, a 16-versus-100 rock drain comparison, and the overload chip appearing and disappearing honestly.

**D-058 · 2026-07-25 — Ch.6, "The Body Model": carry weight, the rest redesign, and the death cost.**
Brain-layer slice under the new long-session doctrine (D-057) — built, self-verified, C3-audited, and remediated inside one session. Three systems, all soft states, none of them a new death vector.

1. **Carry weight (`src/brain/body.ts`).** Every carried material has a per-unit mass and every owned tool a fixed one (`TUNE.materialMassKg` / `toolMassKg`, keyed by the same `MaterialKind` set the family/tag schema uses, so a future material cannot ship without deciding what it weighs). Total mass sorts the castaway into three bands — Light / Working / Heavy — which scale movement speed and energy cost. **The energy half deliberately REUSES the D-052/D-055 plumbing by multiplying it** (the ambient per-game-hour drain in `reconcile`, and `effortEnergyCostFor`'s per-gather charge) rather than adding a parallel drain that would need keeping in sync forever. **`light` multiplies by exactly 1 on both axes**, so an unencumbered castaway behaves bit-for-bit as before this chapter existed — the system is invisible until it is earned, and that invariant is its own regression test.
2. **The rest redesign.** C05's `sleep()` ran a reconcile span and then set `energy = energyMax` outright — an instant jump to full regardless of how empty the castaway started. **That line is gone.** A new transient `resting` flag is raised for the sleep span, and reconcile recovers energy and warmth at tuned RATES over the elapsed game hours (scaled by `sleepRecoveryMultiplier`), shedding fatigue as it goes. A full 8-hour sleep recovers 84 energy: an ordinarily-tired castaway wakes full, one who ran themselves to empty wakes genuinely short. Recovery is bounded by the same `energyMax`/`warmthMax` ceilings everything else clamps to, so sleeping repeatedly cannot exploit it — tested directly (25 consecutive sleeps stay at or under the ceiling). Sleeping is also never *worse* than being awake in the same spot: the warmth rate takes `Math.max` of the resting rate and the awake rate, so dozing by a roaring fire still gets the fire's much better rate.
3. **Fatigue.** A 0–100 value accruing while ONLINE and in energy debt, shed by rest, read out in three perceivable stages (mild / moderate / severe) with plain status text. **Deliberately absent from the health-drain path entirely** — that is what keeps D-011's offline-death-impossible law structurally intact with a new body state added, the same scope-out energy's own C05 introduction made.
4. **The death cost + lesson.** A death now takes a floored `deathResourceLossFraction` (0.25) of each CARRIED loose resource stack — **and nothing else**. Tools survive, stored goods survive, skills survive, and `KnowledgeState` survives: Ch.2's amendment B is a standing law, and this is simply the second system that has to respect it. `Math.floor` means a stack of 1–3 loses nothing, so rounding never wipes a small stack and the poorest castaway is not punished hardest. **Two judgment calls, stated rather than buried:** (a) the fraction — 0.25 against a realistic 10–20 units carried costs 2–5 units, versus one felled tree yielding 8 wood, so it is minutes of play to recover, a real sting that is never a setback spiral; (b) **"loose/uncarried" was read as CARRIED loose stacks, and storage was deliberately left untouched** — what you were carrying scatters where you fell, what you took the trouble to store at home is exactly the investment a built crate is meant to protect. The ruling's phrasing admits a second reading (storage) and this one is flagged for cheap correction if C1 meant otherwise. Each death also records a cause-specific lesson message and exactly what it cost, in the death log.

**Save schema bumped v7→v8** (`migrateV7toV8`): `fatigue` and `resting` are new; a returning player wakes with zero fatigue and not resting — the honest "we have no record of how tired you were" answer, and the one this chapter's own offline law demands (inventing a fatigue number for a save that predates the system would be absence making the body worse). Carry weight needs no migration at all — it is derived from inventory and tools, which every v7 save already has. Pre-v8 death-log entries keep their original shape, unrewritten, rather than gaining a fabricated message for a death that happened before the lesson existed.

**THE MANDATORY PROPERTY TEST (the Ch.6 analogue of Ch.2's amendment B), and a real bug it caught.** The chapter required that reconcile never let absence turn Fatigue harmful. Implemented **structurally**, by branch order rather than a guard bolted on afterwards: across a qualifying absence fatigue can only fall or hold, never rise — a restful spot (roof, not freezing, not soaked) actively recovers, anywhere else simply holds. Property-tested over 2000 random states × random long absences (up to 30 game-days), alongside the offline-death-impossible law in the same file. **The first draft failed it**: reconcile's 6-decimal `round()` could round a held value UP, raising fatigue by up to ~5e-7 across an offline span (max observed 4.995e-7; an earlier draft of this entry said ~5e-9, corrected by C3) — numerically trivial, but the law is absolute. Rather than widen the assertion's epsilon, the code was fixed (an explicit clamp so the offline result can never exceed the pre-span value) and the test was then TIGHTENED to an exact `<=` with no epsilon at all. The law now holds exactly, not approximately.

**The honest-systems rail, and what was deliberately NOT built.** The chapter names a "perceptual distortion" at severe fatigue, cosmetic only, that must never alter game state the player reasons from. **The status text shipped; the distortion did not.** Reasoning, stated plainly rather than quietly skipped: this is a brain-layer slice, the visual would be body-layer work, and a distortion effect is exactly the kind of thing that is safe as a screen-space vignette (the existing cold-vignette precedent) and unsafe the moment it touches a displayed number. Deferring it costs nothing and removes any chance of shipping something that reads as misleading; the constraint is recorded for whoever builds it. What DID ship is truthful at every stage — a device-harness check verifies that at severe fatigue, warmth, thirst, inventory counts, and fatigue itself all still read exactly true.

**Regression-locked:** `tests/body.test.ts` (new, 33 tests) — weight arithmetic including the torch's owned/spent transition, band boundaries as inclusive-below ceilings, `light === 1` on both multipliers, monotonic band ordering, the ambient-drain scaling through a real reconcile; the rest redesign's curve, its bound under 25 consecutive sleeps, `resting` never left on, and sleep-never-worse-than-awake beside a fire; all three fatigue stages, accrual only in online debt, shedding by sleep, and its absence from the health path; the mandatory offline property test plus the sheltered-recovers and exposed-holds cases; the death cost's flooring, the never-takes-tools/storage/skills/knowledge guarantee, fatigue clearing on respawn, the death-log record, and a lesson for every cause including an unknown one; and the v7→v8 migration. Four pre-existing tests that had encoded the OLD behaviour as correct were updated to assert the new behaviour rather than loosened (`construction.test.ts`'s "refills energy on waking" → the recovery curve; two energy-cost tests → live load multipliers, plus two new tests proving `light` pays exactly the pre-Ch.6 amount and `heavy` pays strictly more; `crafting.test.ts` and `session.test.ts`'s death tests → the resource cost). `tools/smoke.mjs` gained a "Ch.6" section (below).

**Verification:** purity ✓ (14 brain files, 16 modules), docs-integrity ✓ (58 decisions), typecheck ✓, **246 brain tests ✓** (213 + 33 new), production build ✓. Device harness **153/155** — the entire new "Ch.6" section (16 checks) green, including the honest-systems rail verified by comparing rendered HUD vitals against live state at severe fatigue; the 2 misses are the same pre-existing CPU-contention pair every D-05x pass has already root-caused. **Five harness-authoring bugs were found and fixed during this pass's own verification, none in product code** (a hardcoded tap coordinate for a node 80 m away, a death-detection baseline captured after the reload that caused the death, two sleep-tap setup errors, and a TUNE constant missing from the harness's own mirror) — detailed in the cycle log, recorded because this class is now the project's most reliable source of false signal.

**Doc deltas:** D-058 (decisions log); this cycle-log entry and its TUNE ledger rows; `drift_state.md`'s Dossier tracker marks Ch.6 shipped, brain-layer only.

**AUDIT (D-058):** *(C3, 2026-07-26 — run in the MAIN checkout, not a worktree, per the hazard D-056's audit escalated; the environment held and the full harness completed.)* Both parts independently re-derived against the code. **Part A** sound: v1.5 archived byte-identical (verified by `git show | diff`), version strings consistent at all three sites, §3's waypoint table and §4's six triggers match D-057 one for one, law (c) narrowed in place with (a)/(b)/(d) restated, and "routing, never verification" survives a full v1.5→v1.6 diff — the definition-of-done gates and all eight §5 build laws are byte-identical. **Part B** structurally correct: the energy reuse is genuinely two call sites (`reconcile.ts:149`, `state.ts:244`) with no parallel drain, `light` is exactly 1 on both axes, C05's `energy = energyMax` is genuinely gone, fatigue is provably outside the health path (`vitals.ts` is not in the commit at all), the offline law is enforced by branch order, the property test really does assert an exact `<=` with no epsilon, and the rounding clamp is load-bearing (1008/2000 seeded values round up without it). `respawn` touches nothing beyond carried stacks; the honest-systems distortion was deliberately not built and nothing distorts any displayed number. Fresh re-verification matched every claimed number: purity 14 files/16 modules, docs-integrity 58 decisions, typecheck clean, **246/246 tests** (15 files, `body.test.ts` 33), clean build, **device harness 153/155 with all 16 Ch.6 checks green**. **One genuine behavioural finding (B1):** every `sleep()` span qualifies for report (1200 s vs the 120 s threshold), so the `restingNow` disjunct at `reconcile.ts:256` is unreachable and sleeping cold, soaked, or under a damaged shelter sheds **zero** fatigue (measured 90 → 90, versus 90 → 0 when restful) — the harness's own green "sleeping sheds fatigue — 91.14 -> 90.97" line documents the gap rather than catching it. No standing law is broken by it. Plus four cosmetic doc inaccuracies (ops v1.6's changelog omits Ch.9 from C1-blocking; a stale "energy is refilled on waking" line in `session.ts`'s own JSDoc; the rounding bug's magnitude is ~5e-7, not ~5e-9; `MASS_KINDS` is not exhaustiveness-checked). **Verdict: PASS-WITH-NOTES.**

**REMEDIATION (C2, 2026-07-26 — same session, per D-057's long-session doctrine): all five findings closed.** **B1 fixed as a FIX:** the fatigue branch order is inverted so recovery (`restingNow || restfulSpot`) is tested first and growth is guarded explicitly by `!qualifiesForReport` — being asleep is now its own sufficient reason to shed fatigue, and the offline law becomes *more* clearly structural (the only branch that can raise fatigue is unreachable across an absence by its own guard, not by chain position). Confirmed on device: the same harness line that read `91.14 -> 90.97` now reads **`90.87 -> 0.00`**. Two regression tests added that fail against the pre-fix code, and the weak assertion that let it through (a bare `<`, satisfied by 0.17 of drift) now requires at least half the tuned shed. **A structural fix beyond the findings:** tightening that assertion immediately produced a FOURTH false failure from constants missing in the harness's own local TUNE mirror — the third such false red in one pass — so the mirror is now self-checking and refuses the run, naming the key, if any value is missing or non-finite. **A1/B2/B3/B4 fixed as documentation TUNEs**, with B4 fixed by making the claim TRUE (`MASS_KINDS` is now derived from `TUNE.materialMassKg`'s keys and typed `MaterialKind[]`, so the exhaustiveness the comment promised is enforced by the compiler) rather than by softening the wording. Post-remediation: **248/248 tests**, purity/docs-integrity/typecheck/build green, the whole Ch.6 harness section green. *Status: standing.* — C2
