**D-052 · 2026-07-25 — Living Island Track A, the immediate FIX package: energy inversion, the death exploit, salvage reachability, depletion visuals split by nature, and the torch.**
Five items, one pass, shipped ahead of any Living Island dossier chapter reaching CODE, per C1's directive. Root causes and fixes:
1. **Energy inversion.** `gatherNode()` never charged energy at all — the ambient per-game-hour drain in `reconcile()` was the ONLY cost that existed, so a felled tree and an idle minute cost identically nothing extra. Fixed: every `hold`-interaction (effortful) node kind now costs a tuned amount on a successful gather (`effortEnergyCostFor` in `state.ts`); every `tap`-interaction kind stays free. `energyDrainPerGameHour` retuned 5→2 so the ambient/idle rate alone is not harder than before now that action costs exist on top of it. Both directions regression-locked independently (`tests/construction.test.ts`), per the handoff's own instruction not to assume one proves the other.
2. **The death exploit (interim only — the full design is a later dossier chapter, not built here).** `respawn()` set every vital to max — a free-refill loop indistinguishable from genuinely eating/drinking/sleeping. Fixed: thirst/hunger/energy now wake at `respawnVitalFraction` (0.5), health lower still at `respawnHealthFraction` (0.3). Warmth is a deliberate exception, kept at max — it is the acute killer (Rule of Threes, charter §I.6) and stacking a second cold-death on the heels of the first is a tradeoff for the death chapter to make, not this fix. Every death is now logged (`trace.deathLog`: cause + game-hour) for the debug export (D-050's tool).
3. **Salvage reachability.** `spawnSalvageNode`'s radius band ran up to `WORLD.islandRadius - 4` (118 m) — up to 10 m past `WALKABLE_RADIUS` (108 m), landing spawns in the shore falloff, in or at the water. Fixed: the band is bounded by `WALKABLE_RADIUS` (minus a shore margin), and a new general-purpose `isWalkablePoint()` (`world.ts`) validates every candidate before it is committed, with a defensive centre-of-island fallback should the formula itself ever be wrong in the future. A pre-existing brain test had a bound loose enough (`islandRadius - 2`) to never have caught this — tightened alongside a new 500-seed property test.
4. **Depletion visuals split by nature.** `NodeViews.sync()` previously shrunk every depleted node except `crashbox`/`salvage` to a 0.32-scale remnant, including driftwood and deadfall — loose, consumed material with no honest "in-between" state. Fixed: driftwood and deadfall now join `crashbox`/`salvage` in the vanish-until-available-again treatment; trees, bushes, rock outcrops, coconut palms, reeds, shellfish, and the quarry keep the existing shrink/stump/sapling regrow, unchanged. A real behavior change, not just an added branch.
5. **The torch — crafting-tree entry #1.** New: wood + fibre → an unlit torch (`craftTorch`); lit only at an active fire (`lightTorch`/`canLightTorch`, reusing the existing interact-radius reach rather than a new one); burns down over `torchBurnGameHours` while lit (closed-form in `reconcile()`, the same treatment as structure decay); spent, not refuelled, once its fuel reaches zero — a fresh one must be crafted. Carried once owned (no equip step, the same "owning it is wearing it" rule the axe already set, D-046(d)): a haft + flame particles + a small point light on the opposite hip from the axe, intensity scaled by night factor like the fire's own light. Wired into the Build panel as a fourth independently-gated entry (never a shared priority slot — the D-040/D-042 lesson), and lighting a torch takes priority over feeding the fire at a tap on it without ever starving feed-fire (`canLightTorch` is only true in the rare transient window right after crafting an unlit torch, not "almost always true" the way the C03 bug's gating condition was).

**Save schema:** bumped v4→v5 (`migrateV4toV5`, `save.ts`) — the torch is a genuinely new field, and a returning player simply hasn't crafted one yet, the same reasoning v2→v3 used for shelter/storage.

**Regression-locked:** `tests/construction.test.ts` (7 new — FIX-1, both directions), `tests/crafting.test.ts` + `tests/construction.test.ts` + `tests/session.test.ts` (updated — FIX-2, 3 pre-existing tests that had encoded the exploit as correct behavior now assert the fix), `tests/renewability.test.ts` (2 new/tightened — FIX-3), `tests/torch.test.ts` (8 new — FIX-5, craft/light/burn-down/independence-from-feed-fire). FIX-4 has no brain-level state to test (a pure rendering-code change); a device-harness section (`tools/smoke.mjs`, "D-052") covers FIX-1/3/5 with real taps, written and syntax-checked but **not confirmed end-to-end in this session** — see the as-built for the honest verification status.

**TUNE introduced/retuned:** see the ledger — rows marked FIX-1/FIX-2/FIX-3/FIX-5.

**Verification:** purity ✓ (10 brain files, 12 modules), docs-integrity ✓, typecheck ✓, production build ✓, **157 brain tests ✓** (142 + 15 new). Device harness: run twice against a locally built preview (once on this changeset, once on unmodified `c05` for comparison) with a cached Chrome under `--software` (SwiftShader) — both runs crashed at the identical point with an identical `Target closed` error after the identical two touch-timing checks failed, confirming the crash and those two failures are a pre-existing sandbox/software-rendering limitation, not a regression this pass introduced. One real bug this partial run did catch and fix: the harness's own post-respawn assertion (`health === 100`) had gone stale against FIX-2 and would have shipped red. A full clean device run is not confirmed this pass — flagged to C3/the director, consistent with Ops §4 (fresh-context independent audit is the actual completeness gate, not C2's own partial run). *Status: standing.* — C2

**Applied and device-confirmed (2026-07-25, same day, real GPU rendering — not the sandbox's SwiftShader).** The patch applied cleanly (every file's base blob hash matched HEAD exactly, zero conflicts). All 5 root causes independently re-read against the applied code, not re-taken on the sandbox's word — each holds up exactly as described above. Full clean re-verification: purity ✓, docs-integrity ✓ (52 decisions), typecheck ✓, **157/157 brain tests ✓**, production build ✓. The device harness's "D-052" section — genuinely run end to end this time, closing the one gap the sandbox flagged — surfaced **five real bugs in the harness/test-authoring itself, not the product**, root-caused and fixed directly rather than assumed away: (1) the pre-existing "Build panel shows all three items" check never updated for the torch's new fourth entry; (2) the new FIX-1 energy-delta checks asserted an exact `< 0.01` match against the tuned effort cost (and an exact-zero delta for a tap gather) without accounting for the harness's own incidental walk time, during which the ambient per-game-hour drain (correctly) keeps ticking — loosened to a realistic tolerance and teleported the player adjacent to each target first, the same lesson banked earlier this cycle; (3) the same over-strict-tolerance class hit the new FIX-2 health check (`< 0.01` against a value read after a real tap + `sleep(500)`, during which online health regen keeps ticking) — loosened, the identical fix C05's own audit already applied to an analogous `energy === 100` assertion; (4) the new FIX-5 torch-lighting setup tapped `.action` (the primary contextual button) instead of `.secondary-action` (the actual Build-panel opener used everywhere else in this file) — a wrong selector; (5) the torch-lighting check only verified the fire was `built`, not that it was still actually *lit* (`fuel > 0`) — by the point this check runs, deep in one long continuous session that had already been through a 240-real-minute `goAway()`, the fire from earlier in the run had genuinely burned out (built stays true forever, only fuel depletes), so the torch correctly refused to light from a dead fire; fixed by forcing the fire's fuel deterministically before the check, the same "force the precondition, don't trust incidental leftover world state" convention every other precondition in this file already follows. **After all five fixes: 113/115.** The remaining two misses (the quarry's three-real-taps-in-a-row check, and the fast-movement toggle's measured-distance check) are the *same* checks D-051's own audit already root-caused to this machine's confirmed CPU contention interacting with a real, deliberate 100 ms per-frame delta-time clamp (`game.ts`) — re-confirmed here by an isolated diagnostic run immediately against this exact build: 3/3 quarry taps landed cleanly (stone climbing by the tuned amount each time) and fast movement measured a 2.82× ratio, matching the tuned 3×. **The device harness is genuinely green** modulo that one pre-existing, independently-verified, machine-specific caveat — not a new regression and not something D-052 introduced. — C2

**D-051 · 2026-07-23 — C1's full gathering-layer package: the renewability law, the quarry, beach salvage, world truth, migration. D-050's diagnosis was a co-factor, not the root.**
C1's evidence, from the director's own exported debug info: a veteran save at 615.6 game hours, axe owned, trees 3/5 still available — but *every other node kind exhausted* (driftwood 0/5, rock 0/3, palms 0/2, bushes 0/3, deadfall 0/2, shellfish 0/3, the box 0/1), and all 20 recorded taps on visible trees resolved `empty-ground`. A fresh save/fresh browser failed identically. **Settled diagnosis:** the verb was innocent the whole time. A decorative treeline tree is `isPickable: false` — a real tap on one passes straight through to the terrain behind it, and the fail-loud law (D-049) is structurally blind to that: it only ever sees a *hit* on something unrecognised, never a *miss* on something that was never a target at all. Fresh saves share the disease for the identical reason D-050 found — 5 real trees scattered among ~101 visually identical decorative ones — but D-050's "exhaustion" framing was a co-factor, not the root: even on a brand-new save, tapping a decorative tree is silent by design. Two compounding world-truth defects, not one: (1) harvestable is indistinguishable from scenery; (2) the world was finite and exhaustible at all. **Neither D-045's nor D-049's branch fired again — this is a content/legibility and world-design gap, confirmed, not asserted, by the director's own export. The input-layer strike count stays at three.**

**The package, all landed in this one pass:**
- **The renewability law** (new standing rule): *no survival-critical resource is globally exhaustible — scarcity is rate/effort, never extinction.* Every node kind regrows via `reconcile()` after a tuned game-hours duration (`regrowGameHoursFor` in `state.ts`), the identical closed-form pattern already used for structure decay — one exemption, `crashbox`, a one-time story beat (the flask), not a resource. Trees additionally pass through a stump stage, then a sapling stage (`TUNE.treeSaplingAtFraction`), before returning fellable — the only kind with staged regrowth visuals. Driftwood also restocks immediately on *any* qualifying absence regardless of its own timer (the tide), narrated in the morning report alongside general regrowth ("the island came back to life") and new beach salvage.
- **World truth:** every harvestable tree, rock, and the quarry now carries a small, consistent "blaze mark" (`PALETTE.harvestMark`) that decorative scenery never has — the mark *is* the signal, present = real, absent = background, learnable at a glance. Depleted nodes no longer vanish: a stump, a shrinking sapling, or a general scaled-down "remnant" reads as *picked clean, not gone*, correctly grounded (a real bug caught and fixed in this same pass — naively scaling a mesh down around its full-height pivot left it floating at the old centre height instead of sitting on the ground; the fix repositions the pivot by the same scale factor).
- **The stone quarry** (`qr1`, one, inland, `TUNE.quarryStoneCapacity` ≈ 220): repeat-minable — a tap spends from a pool, not the node itself, so it stays available across many taps and only "depletes" (then regrows as a whole) once the pool is fully spent. Scattered rk1-3 also now regrow, like every other kind.
- **Beach salvage** (pulled forward from Phase 2): random finds spawn on the beach ring over time — online, a real-minute interval (`TUNE.salvageSpawnMinutesMin/Max`); offline, the identical elapsed-game-hours math catches up in one `reconcile()` call, capped at `TUNE.salvageMaxActive` unclaimed at once. v1 loot: driftwood, cordage (fibre), stone, or a rare bundle — plain stated odds (`TUNE.salvageBundleOdds`), no loot-box dressing. A claimed find is exempt from regrowth (gone for good; new ones keep spawning elsewhere).
- **Migration v3→v4:** an existing save heals on first load — every depleted node comes back at once ("while you were away, the island came back to life") — and new content (the quarry) merges in from a fresh state, since an old save has no entry for it at all. No wipe.
- **Testing aid:** a "Fast movement (testing)" toggle in Settings (`TUNE.testSpeedMultiplier = 3`), persisted, off by default, multiplying on top of `walkSpeedMps` — the base constant itself never changes.
- **SON addendum, same pass:** the Settings panel's "Look sensitivity" presets (Gentle/Standard/Quick) are removed — confirmed with the director first, since the *open* "Look" button (also on screen) is a separate control and stays, as the entry point to the panel's remaining "Copy debug info" tool.

**Regression-locked** (`tools/smoke.mjs`, section "D-051"): the quarry survives three real taps in a row without going silent, then depletes as a whole and regrows to full capacity; a felled tree, given enough elapsed time, is fellable again by a real tap end to end; a forced beach-salvage find grants its rolled loot on a real tap and never returns; the Fast-movement toggle is a real, reachable control that measurably changes walk speed. New brain-level coverage in `tests/renewability.test.ts` (14 tests): every kind's regrow duration, the tree sapling threshold, driftwood's tide vs. its own timer, the quarry's pool mechanics and blocked-refusal, salvage's determinism and spawn cap, reconcile's continued purity, and the v3→v4 migration's heal-and-merge behaviour.

**Verification:** purity ✓, docs-integrity ✓, typecheck ✓, **142 brain tests ✓** (128 + 14 new), device harness verified against the full new "D-051" section (see the as-built for the exact run count — this session's documented Chrome/Node resource accumulation required more than one retry). **Recommendation for the next cycle spec, not actioned beyond this:** none further — this pass closes the gap D-050 could only partially explain. *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-050 · 2026-07-23 — The 5th live report: an emptied world, not a defect. Neither branch fired; the debug-export tool ships anyway.**
C1 scoped diagnostic ruling on the same live build: axe visibly carried (D-049 confirmed working), but standing as close as possible to a tree and tapping it produced true silence — not even the in-range affordance circle — across every tree tried, the 5th consecutive live failure of this verb. The ruling's own lead was right: the missing affordance circle, which renders every frame from player proximity alone and needs no tap at all, meant the failure was not in tap detection but somewhere upstream of it. **Root cause, confirmed by direct reproduction (not asserted):** the island's 5 standing trees are single-use and never respawn (`world.ts`); the treeline behind them is 110 purely decorative, non-interactive trees rendered as thin instances (`island.ts`, `isPickable: false` by design, for frame-rate reasons its own comment states) that are visually near-identical to the real ones — same materials, same palette, interspersed in the same zone. A scripted repro (fell all 5 real trees, then tap a genuine decorative-tree coordinate) reproduced the director's exact symptom bit-for-bit: no highlight, no pending, zero trace breadcrumb, confirmed against a screenshot showing the decorative tree indistinguishable from a real one. After enough real, cumulative play across today's cycles to exhaust the 5 real trees, *every* later "tree" the director sees and taps genuinely has nothing to react with — correct behaviour, not a regression.
**Neither of the ruling's pre-committed branches fired.** This is not new-system gating (no C05 condition blocks felling), not an ownership-state mismatch (the same `state.tools.axe` field gates the fell verb and the carriage render), and not tap-detection/pointer-routing or post-action interaction state (D-045/D-049's classes) — it is a content/legibility gap: finite, non-respawning resources with zero visual distinction from decorative scenery, and no player-facing signal for "this zone is picked clean." **The input-layer strike count stays at three; no rewrite.**
**Shipped regardless, per the ruling's mandatory harness-fidelity item:** a "Copy debug info" button in settings, exporting the trace, the last 20 tap outcomes, and — the number that would have settled this instantly — how many of each resource kind remain available versus the world's total. This is exactly why 82/82 device-harness runs coexisted with 5 real failures: every harness run starts from a fresh or explicitly-reset save, a state a long real session structurally cannot stay in, so the harness never exhausts a finite resource the way extended real play naturally does. Regression-locked (`tools/smoke.mjs`): felling all 5 trees then tapping a real decorative-tree coordinate reproduces the exact silent symptom; the debug-export text is asserted to report "tree: 0/5" in that state. **Recommendation, not actioned under this ruling's scope:** a future TUNE/content pass should give single-use harvestable nodes a visual tell distinct from decorative scenery (or a passive hint once a resource kind is fully depleted) — flagged to SON/C1 for the next cycle spec, not solved here. *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-049 · 2026-07-23 — Tap-to-fell's FOURTH shape: root-caused, remediated below the strike count, plus the fail-loud law.**
C1 scoped diagnostic ruling on the live C05 build (PERFECT queue): the director's re-test of tap-to-fell failed in a NEW shape — fell one tree, tap a second, unrelated object, zero reaction (no highlight, no sound, no reason). Per the ruling's own order, reproduced first: `tools/smoke.mjs` gained sequential-interaction coverage (fell A → tap B; fell → gather → fell; fell → tap-crate) that the existing 75/75 single-action suite had never exercised, and it failed reliably. **Root cause, confirmed via `window.__driftScene.pick()`:** `NodeViews.sync()` disabled a spent node's mesh for *rendering* (`setEnabled(false)`) but never touched `isPickable` — a separate Babylon flag picking does not infer from enabled state. A felled tree's invisible geometry stayed a live pick target, silently intercepting a ray meant for whatever stood near or behind it (confirmed concretely: a storage crate built near a just-felled tree). **This is post-action interaction state — a stale, disposed-mesh hit-list — not the tap-detection/pointer-routing class of the three priors.** Per the ruling's pre-committed branch: fixed at that layer (`NodeViews.sync()` now clears `isPickable` on a spent node's full mesh hierarchy — trunk *and* canopy, palm *and* fronds/husk, a reed's blade *and* its four extras — general, not special-cased to trees), regression-locked in the harness, and **the input-layer strike count stays at three**; no rewrite, no Opus escalation.
Also shipped per the ruling: **(a) the fail-loud law** — silence is never a legal outcome; a tap that reaches something real but produces no verb now calls the existing `explain()`/`markFailedTap()` path (a player-visible reason plus a `trace.failedInteractionTaps` breadcrumb) instead of vanishing, while a genuinely empty-ground tap still explains nothing (a look-around, not a failure); **(b) visible tool carriage** — the axe, once made, is worn on the castaway (a haft+head parented to the hip, always visible, no equip step — ownership is the only gate) instead of living only in a HUD chip, and the Build panel's axe item now reads "Owned." *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-048 · 2026-07-23 — C05 spec, retroactive C1 audit: APPROVED-WITH-NOTES.**
Scope-true to D-037/D-040/D-044; D-011-compliant. Notes: placement-feel judged at playtest; tap-forgiveness constant → tune sweep; energy→death integration requires re-proving the offline-death property when it lands. *Status: standing.* — C1

**D-047 · 2026-07-23 — ⚑ RESOLVED: crew composition** (open since the library-first audit's Amendment 7).
Standing crew remains all-Claude, tiered per D-044; non-Claude external audits stay an occasional red-team practice with no standing seat and no constitutional pen — independence is their value. Resolved by C1 at the director's request. *Status: standing; resolved.* — C1

**D-046 · 2026-07-23 — Process-gap remediation (C1).**
Laws: (a) **No committed spec, no build** — if the cycle log lacks the target cycle's spec, C2 halts and requests it; a missing OPEN artifact is a stop condition, never an improvisation license. (b) **Handoff packages embed their spec and deltas inline** — never reference uncommitted content. (c) **MAJOR artifacts flagged by SON reach C2 only with C1's explicit "AUDITED — GO"** relayed by the director. (d) **New CI docs-integrity check** — every D-reference in `/docs` must resolve to a ledger entry (this would have caught the dangling D-045 reference mechanically). Ops → v1.5 with these under §4; version strings bumped. *Status: standing.* — C1

**D-045 · 2026-07-23 — Third tap-to-fell root cause: an untracked pointer event corrupting tap-vs-drag detection.**
Regression-locked; shipped as its own commit before the C05 build. **Standing rule: a fourth distinct failure of this interaction rewrites the input layer (class, not instance).** *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-044 · 2026-07-23 — Crew reorganization for token economy (director).**
SON Operator added (Sonnet high, chat) for daily orchestration, triage, and drafting specs/packages/deltas; **C1 becomes Supervisor** (top tier: constitution, forks, MAJOR-artifact audits, cycle-close verdicts). **C2 default drops to Sonnet high** with per-block Opus escalation (the handoff block's tier line) or per-blockage escalation (an architecture-level problem C2 cannot crack in two attempts → tell the Director "flip to Opus for this", then back). C01–C04 ran Opus as architecture-founding cycles — correct spend, that era is closed. **C3 stays Sonnet.** Supervision loop: MAJOR artifacts (cycle specs, handoff packages, constitutional deltas) are audited by C1 pre-handoff when flagged; a KEY REPORT goes to C1 at every CLOSE. Ops → v1.4 (§1 crew table + supervision loop, §6 token economy). *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-043 · 2026-07-23 — Resource legibility: the fiber lesson.**
The director found fiber only by exhausting alternatives — palms were the *designed* source, but the game never taught it, which fails Experience rule 1 even when the design is sound. Fixes: **reed/tall-grass clumps** added near the pond and scattered inland as the visually obvious fiber source (fibrous silhouette); palms keep husk fiber with visible husks at the trunk base; the craft card shows a source hint under any missing material ("Fibre — reeds by the pond, palm husks"); first-time pickups toast their identity. *Status: standing.*

**D-042 · 2026-07-23 — Direct-world interaction replaces the context-button model.**
The Cycle 03 defects (fire buildable at range, fire hidden until night/axe, an axe that did nothing findable) share one root: verbs lived in a prioritized HUD button stack instead of on the objects themselves. New model: **tap the thing to use the thing** — in range it acts; out of range the character walks there and then acts (tap-to-move-and-use, the LDOE flow); every object has an interaction radius and a subtle in-range affordance; every blocked interaction states its reason ("Too far", "Needs an axe", "Not enough wood"). Buttons survive only for placement verbs (Build fire — available whenever wood suffices, day or night), the craft card, and settings. *Status: standing.*

**D-041 · 2026-07-23 — Landscape-first (director's call).**
Two-thumb stick-and-look 3D belongs in landscape; the UI is relaid for it, portrait shows a rotate prompt, and a web-app manifest sets the orientation (fullscreen + orientation lock requested on first interaction where the browser allows). The manifest also plants the deferred PWA seed from D-014 — install/service-worker still later. *Status: standing.*

**D-040 · 2026-07-23 — ⚑ Cycle 03 CLOSED: systems pass, feel fails the LDOE bar. Construction defers one cycle.**
Director's verdict, five defects: (1) camera/look while moving is not smooth; (2) fiber sourcing was opaque (bushes teased, palms hid); (3) fire interactable from far away; (4) fire could not be built until night or until the axe existed — root cause to be identified, suspected context-button priority starvation; (5) the axe apparently did nothing — its verbs were not discoverable. All five are one architecture problem (D-042) plus camera feel and teaching gaps (D-041, D-043). The D-037 escalation order stands, with a feel cycle inserted first: **C04 feel → C05 construction → C06 threats** — building on a janky camera would compound every later cycle. *Status: standing.*

**D-039 · 2026-07-23 — D-025 formally resolved: with real death stakes, the floors are the whole safety.**
D-035 named "the first cycle that gives a vital real consequence" as the moment to confirm or replace the offline-floor-vs-idleness rule. Cycle 03 is that cycle — death is real now — so this is the on-the-record resolution the C3 audit correctly asked for (a code comment claiming "resolved" is not a decision). The rule stands and is now *stronger* than in the warmth-only cycles: online, floors are 0 and a vital can empty and kill; offline (a report-worthy span), every floor is positive and **offline death is mathematically impossible for any state and any elapsed time** — proven by a property test, not asserted (A1). D-025's old desktop-no-auto-lock worry is moot for offline: absence simply cannot reach 0 health. The one narrow case that remains — a *foreground, unfocused* desktop window left untouched for a long stretch, where the frame loop keeps ticking sub-threshold spans and the floor never engages — is by design "presence is agency": the window is open, the player could act. It is documented at `reconcile.ts`'s gate. C04 adds a bed/respawn anchor, which is where any softening of active-play death (a safe place to log off) belongs. *Status: standing; resolved.* — C2

**D-038 · 2026-07-23 — Cycle 03 audit remediation: the purity check judges every import by where it resolves, and body gameplay numbers move to `tune.ts`.**
The C3 audit of Cycle 03 found a **fourth** bypass of the brain/body check — the same family as the three before it (D-026 ×2, D-034): a *relative* path that tunnels into node_modules (`import from '../../node_modules/@babylonjs/core/...'`) slipped past, because the relative branch only checked for a body import, never the real package the path lands in. Rather than special-case a fourth shape, the check was **generalised to close the class**: every import — relative or bare, however written — is now judged by the package that owns the file it resolves to (`owningPackageOfFile`), and a resolved path inside node_modules is never recursed into, only identity-checked. All **five** bypass shapes (direct, transitive, computed-dynamic, npm-alias, relative-tunnel) reproduced and confirmed caught; the clean tree still passes. This is the generalization the checker had been circling for four cycles: stop trusting syntax, always ask where it resolves.
Also fixed, from the audit's notes: the tune law is now honoured in `src/body` — the player's collision radius, the per-kind obstacle footprints, the decorative-instance radii, the pond tap forgiveness, and the tap-vs-drag gesture thresholds moved from hard-coded body literals into `tune.ts` (they gate reachability and interaction, exactly the class D-026 established belongs there); the HUD's "low" vital thresholds now read the same `TUNE.*LowHintAt` constants the hint system uses, so the bar's cue and the hint can never disagree; `save.ts` clamps every vital to its valid range on load, so a corrupt or hand-edited save can no longer carry a negative health that reconcile would hold negative forever (a health of 0 self-heals to a respawn on the next tick); and the device harness takes the death screenshot after confirming the overlay is up. *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-037 · 2026-07-23 — The build-up: escalation order locked (pressure → construction → threats).**
Per Experience Constitution rule 2 (new decisions before tighter margins): **C03** adds survival pressure (thirst, hunger, health/death, first tool, first loot, first mystery) — the island starts making demands; **C04** adds construction (shelter tiers, bed/respawn anchor, energy/sleep, storage, upkeep) — the player starts owning ground; **C05** adds threats (first creature, spear, defense) — the base earns its walls. Each cycle converts learned systems into new decisions. Director's mandate: "build it up as you see" (standing authority on content escalation within the charter). *Status: standing; contents of C04/C05 specified at their OPEN.* — C2 (renumbered from the handoff's D-032; the ledger reached D-035 in Cycle 02.)

**D-036 · 2026-07-23 — ⚑ Cycle 02 CLOSED: gate 3 pass-with-notes. The 3D foundation is accepted.**
Director's verdict on the phone: foundation good, trees read well, no frame-rate complaint (treated as pass; re-raised any time it stutters) — two defects and one direction note. Defects, fixed in C03 Stage 0: (1) the character reads as floating — root causes addressed together: feet-to-terrain grounding **and a blob contact shadow** under the character (the absence of a contact shadow is the classic cause of perceived hovering); (2) trees have no colliders. Direction note: "still very simple" — answered by D-037's escalation, not by iterating the thin slice. *Status: standing.* — C2 (renumbered from the handoff's D-031.)

**D-035 · 2026-07-23 — D-025 re-examined for Cycle 02 and carried forward explicitly.**
The C3 audit noted that D-025's standing commitment — "confirm or replace the offline-floor-vs-idleness rule once warmth carries real stakes" — was carried into Cycle 02 silently rather than re-logged, which is exactly the forgetting D-025 was written to prevent. Re-examined now: **warmth still has no death or damage stakes in Cycle 02** (SCOPE OUT keeps out health, death, and every vital but warmth), so the condition that would force the decision has not arrived, and the rule stands unchanged and correct. It moves to the first cycle that gives a vital real consequence — Cycle 03 at the earliest. The dependency C3 named in D-025 (on the primary device the floor leans on OS auto-lock; no safety net on desktop) travels with it. This entry exists so the deferral is a decision on the record, not an omission. *Status: standing; the revisit condition has still not triggered.* — C2

**D-034 · 2026-07-23 — Cycle 02 audit remediation: the purity check resolves real package identity.**
The C3 audit of Cycle 02 found a *third* bypass of the brain/body check, in the same family as Cycle 01's two: an npm alias (`"x": "npm:@babylonjs/core"`) lets a brain file `import from 'x'` and load a renderer straight past a forbidden-*name* list. Root cause across all three: the check trusted the name the code typed. Fixed at the root — `tools/check-purity.mjs` now resolves every bare specifier to the file it actually loads, walks up to that file's owning `package.json`, and judges the **real declared package name**, so an alias resolves to `@babylonjs/core` and is caught. All four bypass classes (direct import, transitive re-export, computed dynamic import, npm alias) reproduced and confirmed caught (exit 1); the clean tree still passes. Two audit notes also fixed: the `+2 hours` fire-feed feedback now reads `TUNE.fireBurnGameHoursPerWood` live rather than hard-coding it, and the harness's "within seconds" check tightened from 45 s to 8 s. Added beyond the notes: the device harness now **fails** if it finds itself on a software renderer without `--software` having asked for it, so a GPU-less runner can never misread an 8 fps SwiftShader number as a real A3 pass. *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-033 · 2026-07-23 — The frame rate is traced beside the save, not inside it.**
Cycle 02's spec extends the playtest trace with an FPS median, but A1 requires the brain's `TraceState` to stay byte-identical across the pivot — and the brain has no opinion on frame rate anyway. So the body keeps its own numbers (median, 1% low, sample count, device pixel ratio) in its own localStorage key, exposed through the debug hook. The rule this sets: **the brain traces what it decides; the renderer traces what it draws.** *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-032 · 2026-07-23 — The HUD and panels are DOM over the canvas, not engine-drawn; plus a scene debug handle.**
Text in the DOM is crisp at any device pixel ratio, costs the GPU nothing, scales with the player's own font settings, and is reachable by a screen reader — all of which §I.18 rule 7's accessibility baseline asks for and none of which comes free from text drawn into a 3D scene. It also keeps the frame budget for the island. Consequence: every tappable control is a real `<button>`, so the harness clicks them as a user would rather than guessing at pixels.
Alongside it the build exposes `window.__driftScene`. It is more surface than D-022's narrow save accessor, and it is kept deliberately: it turned two silent rendering bugs into five-minute diagnoses this cycle — an entire invisible beach (back-face-culled terrain) and a fire with no flame — where reading the code had produced only plausible wrong answers. Revisit if anything the player could exploit ever depends on it. *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-031 · 2026-07-23 — Havok is not adopted in Cycle 02; the character controller is kinematic.**
The cycle spec named the official Havok plugin in Stage 0. Measured rather than assumed, it costs roughly 1.4 MB of WASM plus an async init against an 8-second cold-load budget (`coldLoadBudgetSeconds`) — and Cycle 02 has nothing that needs it. The island's height is an analytic function shared by the terrain mesh and the player, so ground-following is exact in a single call, and reach is a distance check the brain already owns. Charter §II.10's own rule decides it: a package may not introduce more concepts than the code it removes. **Adoption trigger, named now so this is not quietly forgotten:** the first system that needs real dynamics — Cycle 03's building placement and collision, or the first threat that moves. Declared as a deviation in the as-built note so the audit judges it explicitly rather than discovering it. *Status: standing; revisit at Cycle 03.* — C2

**D-030 · 2026-07-22 — Cycle 02 "Boots on Sand": re-prove the Cycle 01 steel thread in the 3D body.**
Same brain, same tune constants, same tests — a new world. The pivot's central claim ("everything above the renderer survives") is checked mechanically: A1 requires zero diffs under `/src/brain` and `/tests` versus tag `c01`. Rust-loop systems (building, threats, more vitals) begin Cycle 03+ on this foundation. *Status: standing.*

**D-029 · 2026-07-22 — Art bar: low-poly stylized 3D; close third-person camera.**
Muck/Valheim readability, not Rust fidelity — Rust sets the *gameplay* bar, never the visual one. Close third-person default, first-person a later option. CC0-first assets (Kenney, Quaternius) until the Art Director hat activates. Touch model: left stick to move, drag to look, tap the world to interact. *Status: standing; camera/controls `[TUNE]` in playtest.*

**D-028 · 2026-07-22 — 3D stack: Babylon.js + TypeScript, web-first retained (charter v0.4 §II.5).**
Pinned to current stable (9.x line, verified 2026-07-22; exact pin in `DEPENDENCIES.md`), Apache-2.0, Havok WASM physics, glTF, WebGL2 baseline with a WebGPU path. Keeps the brain byte-identical, the whole proven pipeline (Pages, tap-a-link, smoke harness, CI), and the crew's strongest language. Rejected: Unity (editor-centric, poor AI-crew/director-machine fit, weak mobile-web), Three.js (lower-level, more glue), PlayCanvas (cloud-editor dependency), react-three-fiber (D-009's rule). **Godot 4.x is the named native escape hatch** — its 3D web export (Compatibility renderer, 30–100 MB payloads) is wrong for phone browsers, but its native Android path is strong; if mobile-web 3D hits a hard performance ceiling that optimization cycles cannot clear, the brain ports and Godot builds the APK. Only that gate reopens this decision. *Status: standing; reversible at the hatch.*

**D-027 · 2026-07-22 — ⚑ Cycle 01 CLOSED: technical PASS, direction overturned at gate 3. The First Night pivots to 3D.**
The director's playtest verdict: the systems work, but 2D top-down buttons-and-overlays cannot deliver the Rust-like experience the game is for. Under the three-gate rule that verdict is final and the machine worked as designed — the direction was overturned at the cheapest possible moment, one cycle in. Consequences: the 2D body is frozen (no further 2D cycles); the C01 build stays live at `/builds/c01/` as the **simulation laboratory**; everything above the renderer survives untouched (brain, reconcile, saves, tune, tests, pipeline, ops, all decisions). Charter → v0.4, ops → v1.3. *Status: standing.*

**D-026 · 2026-07-22 — Audit remediation: the purity check now walks the import graph, and two hit-test numbers moved into `tune.ts`.**
The C3 audit of Cycle 01 (PASS-WITH-NOTES) demonstrated that `tools/check-purity.mjs` only regexed the brain's own files: a brain module importing Phaser *through* an intermediary in `src/data/` passed the check. The brain/body law is the project's one non-negotiable rule (§II.5) and its enforcement was weaker than it looked, so the check was rewritten to resolve and walk the brain's whole transitive import graph and to print the offending chain; the bypass was reproduced, then confirmed caught (exit 1). Also moved: the fire's tap radius (was a literal `40`) and the wood-node tap slack (was in the presentation table) into `tune.ts` as `fireTapRadius` and `nodeTapSlack` — both widen hit targets, so by D-023's own test they change how the game *plays*, not how it looks. The audit's remaining notes (an over-broad test title, two harness checks that could not fail, and a conditional check that could silently skip) were all fixed rather than accepted.
**Second pass, same audit:** C3 then found a *new* bypass on the rewritten checker — `import(parts.join(''))`, a dynamic import with a computed specifier, is working Phaser-loading code that no static analysis can follow. Closed by treating any non-literal `import()`/`require()` inside the brain's closure as a violation in itself: the brain has no legitimate use for one, so the escape hatch is the defect. Reproduced and confirmed caught. C3 also found that the device harness's fire-burn check was flaky (1 failure in 3 live runs) because it compared fuel against a *nominal* three-minute absence while the harness's own reloads leaked real seconds into it — so the check was measuring the harness's punctuality, not the game's arithmetic. Rewritten to assert the invariant instead (whatever time actually passed, the fire burned it at the tuned rate), which tightened the tolerance from ±0.02 to ±1e-6 and made it exact; three consecutive runs, 30/30. Last bare coefficient (`interactRadius * 0.6`) moved to `TUNE.approachStopFraction`. *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-025 · 2026-07-22 — The offline fairness floor is a rule about ABSENCE, not about elapsed time.**
The C3 audit observed that the D-011 warmth floor is gated on a single `reconcile` call reaching the report threshold, so the frame loop's millisecond spans never trigger it: a player who leaves the app gets the floor, a player who sits idle with the screen on does not. Reviewed and **kept deliberately**, because it is the correct reading of D-011: mercy exists for time the player could not act in. Presence is agency; absence is not. Someone watching the fire go out and choosing to do nothing has made a decision, and the charter's fair-challenge test (§I.18 rule 2) is satisfied — it is telegraphed, understandable, preventable, and recoverable. The behaviour is now documented at the gate in `reconcile.ts` and pinned by a test, so it cannot drift silently. **One dependency named explicitly, at C3's insistence:** on the primary device this rule leans on the phone's OS auto-lock, which typically fires inside two minutes of no touches and takes the correctly-floored `visibilitychange` path — so on Android the exposed window is narrow. On a desktop browser (which the charter permits; Android is primary, not exclusive) there is no auto-lock safety net and the gap is fully real. The decision is sound but it is not self-supporting, and Cycle 02 must weigh it on that basis rather than on "presence is agency" in the abstract.
**Flagged for Cycle 02**, where D-011's frame is already the design topic: once warmth carries real stakes, confirm this or replace it with a floor keyed to time since the last interaction. *Status: standing; revisit in C02.* — C2

**D-024 · 2026-07-22 — Superseded documents are archived in the repo, not deleted.**
`/docs/` holds exactly the six canon documents. Earlier versions of all four living docs plus charter v0.1/v0.2 and ops v1.0/v1.1 sit in `/docs/archive/`, and the two external audits plus the game-research file — library references the director fed in, not canon — sit in `/docs/reference/`, joined on 2026-07-27 by the six design documents v0_7–v0_12 (reference, not canon: they are received design input, and the four living docs remain the only canon). One canonical copy of everything (Ops §2) with nothing lost. *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-023 · 2026-07-22 — `tune.ts` is for gameplay; presentation constants live in `src/body/theme.ts`.**
The tune law (D-005) exists so the director's "that number feels wrong" maps 1:1 to a named constant. Palette, type scale, depth order, and animation durations are not that kind of number — putting them in `tune.ts` would bury the eleven constants that matter under forty that do not. They are still centralised (one file, no magic numbers scattered through scenes), and they move wholesale when the Art Director arrives in Phase 2–3. The rule as applied: **if changing it changes how the game plays, it is in `tune.ts`; if it changes only how it looks, it is in `theme.ts`.** *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-022 · 2026-07-22 — The device acceptance checks are automated (`tools/smoke.mjs`), with a local debug hook.**
"It plays right on the phone" (Charter §II.4) was the one gate nobody could re-run. `tools/smoke.mjs` drives an already-installed Chrome in Android emulation with real touch events and asserts against live game state: 31 checks covering A3, A4, A6 and the observable half of A7, re-runnable against any deployed URL — which is what lets C3 audit a build instead of trusting a claim. It needs a `window.__drift` accessor in the shipped build (read/persist/reset this device's own single-player save). Cost: one global on a local-only game. Benefit: the audit and every future regression run are mechanical. `puppeteer-core` was chosen over `puppeteer`/Playwright precisely because it downloads no browser. Anticipates D-014's Playwright item one cycle early, at a fraction of the weight. *Status: standing; revisit if the hook ever gates anything the player could exploit.* — C2

**D-021 · 2026-07-22 — The placeholder audio cues are synthesised in-repo, not sourced.**
Library-first (§II.10) says rent the plumbing — but every free-sample library carries an attribution trail, and for seven short blips the licence bookkeeping costs more than the code. `tools/gen-audio.mjs` synthesises them from scratch with a seeded noise source, so they are ours, licence-free, and byte-identical on every regeneration (CI asserts exactly that). Playback still rents Phaser's SoundManager. They are placeholders and are replaced wholesale when real audio lands. *Status: standing until the audio pass.* — C2

**D-020 · 2026-07-22 — One input path: every tap routes through a single scene-level handler.**
Found during the build: Phaser's per-Game-Object input fired reliably for mouse but **not** for touch in this configuration — every button in the game was dead under a thumb while working perfectly on a desktop, and nothing in the code looked wrong. Rather than carry two input paths and hope, tappable things register a rectangle with `src/body/ui/Taps.ts` and the scene's one `pointerdown` handler routes to the topmost enabled target. One path, one place to debug, and no button can be silently un-hittable. This class of bug is exactly what the device smoke test (D-022) exists to catch. *Status: standing.* — C2

**D-019 · 2026-07-22 — Fourteen `[TUNE]` constants added during the build, marked `C01+` in the ledger.**
The spec's behaviour could not be expressed without them, so rather than hard-code anything they were added to `tune.ts` and the ledger: `startHourOfDay`/`nightStartHour`/`dayStartHour` (the crash lands at dusk; night is 18:00–06:00), `gameHoursPerDay`, `fireWarmthRadius` (how close "by the fire" is), `fireMaxFuel` (the pit is not a silo), `warmthOfflineFloor` (**the D-011 offline-fairness floor made concrete at 15** — a qualifying absence may sting but cannot bottom you out, and the morning report states the floor rather than hiding it), `warmthLowThreshold`, `hintVisibleSeconds`, `playerSpeed`, `interactRadius`, `tapArriveEpsilon`, `joystickRadius`, `joystickDeadzone`, plus `nodeTapSlack` and `fireTapRadius` (added at audit — see D-026). All are `[TUNE]` and expected to move in playtest. *Status: standing; values provisional.* — C2

**D-018 · 2026-07-22 — Ops v1.2: three experience fields per cycle spec; ceremony capped.**
Every cycle spec now declares PROMISE (what the player should feel), HYPOTHESIS (the experience bet), and PLAYTEST QUESTION (what the director's reaction must answer). The director's gate stays plain-language reactions — no scorecards or forms. Cap: external audits are welcome and triaged like feedback (FIX/TUNE/NEXT/PARK), but no further standing constitutions or ceremony will be added; the machine stays lean. *Status: standing.*

**D-017 · 2026-07-22 — Cycle 01 feel amendment (from the human-enjoyment audit).**
The steel thread was mechanically complete but mute: ~15 s of identical tap-holding before any payoff risked testing patience, not judgment. Amended: split wood interaction (instant driftwood tap `driftwoodTapYield=1` for a first reward in seconds; deadfall hold `deadfallHoldSeconds=1.5` → 2–3 wood); six placeholder audio cues moved IN scope (legibility, not polish — music/final audio stay out); grounded feedback kit on every action; fire-as-sanctuary transition (the relief beat); contextual onboarding (title card, one highlighted next action, idle hint at `idleHintSeconds=10`); local playtest trace (no external analytics). New acceptance A7; all values `[TUNE]`. *Status: standing.*

**D-016 · 2026-07-22 — Charter v0.3: Experience Constitution (§I.18); psychology rebalanced (§I.17); anti-grind reopened (§I.9).**
Seven binding rules (compressed from the audit's fourteen + six): legible actions; challenge = decisions not bigger numbers, with the fair-challenge test; failure teaches and recovery preserves momentum; tension earns relief; three goal horizons + curiosity as a system; progress changes play; respect the player's time and body (primary-player definition + accessibility baseline included). §I.17 now leads with competence/autonomy/curiosity — loss aversion and scarcity demoted to seasoning; arc corrected to vulnerability → capability → earned command, with residual risk. Anti-grind downgraded from "resolved" to a playtest hypothesis with the mastery principle (a mastered action becomes faster, richer, safer, or automatable). *Status: standing.*

**D-015 · 2026-07-22 — Director directives.**
(1) Title: **The First Night**; DRIFT stays the internal codename for files and the repo — zero reference churn, the title lives in-game and on the charter. (2) **Android-first**: Android Chrome is the primary test target until launch-prep; the iOS storage-eviction watch item defers to launch-prep. (3) Codex: tools are also obtainable from boxes/salvage loot; shelter expands to a metal tier (salvaged plane/ship parts) plus natural options — caves and dug-in ground. (4) C1 doubles as the director's candid advisor outside cycle ceremony; replies stay short. *Status: standing.*

**D-014 · 2026-07-22 — Verification and tooling schedule (from the external audit, gated).**
fast-check property tests are mandated on `reconcile` from Cycle 02 (C2 may pull them into C01 if free): no NaN/negative quantities, zero-elapsed changes nothing, determinism, caps respected. Playwright mobile-emulation flows and vite-plugin-pwa (install, cached assets, and a controlled "new build" prompt — assets are never hot-swapped mid-session) are scheduled for Cycles 02–03, once there is something to regress. Ops bumped to v1.1 (dependency law §5.7). *Status: standing.*

**D-013 · 2026-07-22 — The control-feel A/B is folded into Cycle 01, not run as a separate cycle.**
C01 Stage 2 ships both tap-to-move and a virtual joystick behind a settings toggle with identical action timing; the director picks by feel at PLAYTEST and the loser is archived, not blended. This absorbs the audit's "control shootout" at near-zero cost. Grid Engine is not adopted for player movement (grid-stepped locomotion risks the grounded feel); it stays parked for NPC pathfinding and structure occupancy later. *Status: standing.*

**D-012 · 2026-07-22 — Authored-first island (charter §II.10).**
The first island is handcrafted; procedural generation begins only after its pacing and spatial rules are proven in playtest. Formalizes what C01 already does. *Status: standing.*

**D-011 · 2026-07-22 — Offline fairness constitution (charter §II.10; frames the Cycle 02 spec).**
Supersedes the "offline-drain gentleness" open note. In the normal solo game: (1) no offline death; (2) vitals stop at a recoverable floor; (3) the base cannot be fully erased offline; (4) a threat's maximum loss is capped by defenses and phase; (5) escape-critical items are never destroyed by generic offline rolls; (6) a risk forecast is shown before exit once production/threats exist; (7) destructive offline effects are time-capped — excess absence becomes non-destructive narrative time; (8) the report explains causes, not just results; (9) offline randomness is seeded and reproducible; (10) long absences get a recovery path, not a restart. Hardcore mode may tighten all of this; the honest-systems rule (§I.8) still governs. *Status: standing; numbers `[TUNE]`.*

**D-010 · 2026-07-22 — Persistence and validation path: boundary now, heavier tools when earned.**
All save access sits behind a small `SaveRepository` interface from C01, with a localStorage implementation this cycle; Dexie/IndexedDB is adopted at the cycle where the save model outgrows one envelope (target C02–C03), and Zod validates saves and content tables from C02. Keeps the steel thread minimal while making the storage swap a non-event. Watch item: verify on-device that iOS Safari's ~7-day eviction of script-writable storage can't silently delete saves during long absences (mitigation: add-to-home-screen/PWA, later a native wrapper) — tracked in state. *Status: standing.*

**D-009 · 2026-07-22 — React rejected for now; UI is Phaser plus framework-free DOM overlays.**
By the audit's own Rule 5 (a package may not introduce more concepts than the code it removes): at C01–C03 scale React adds a second UI framework, a state-sync boundary the audit itself rates a High risk, and audit surface — for a warmth bar and two overlays. Adoption gate: the first screen that outgrows a simple DOM overlay (likely Phase 2's inventory/crafting/report), revisited then. Consequence: the official template used is `template-vite-ts`, not `template-react-ts`. *Status: reversible at the gate.*

**D-008 · 2026-07-22 — Engine: Phaser 4, pinned at 4.2.1 (charter v0.2 §II.5).**
The external audit's core correction, independently verified 2026-07-22 against phaser.io, GitHub, and npm: Phaser 3 ended at 3.90.0; v4.0.0 went stable April 2026 and v4.2.1 "Giedi" (9 July 2026) is current. Scaffold from the official `template-vite-ts`; upgrades only between cycles per the dependency law. Bonus adopted: the Phaser 4 repo ships official AI-agent `skills/` files (28 subsystem skills plus a v3→v4 migration skill) — C2 reads them in Stage 0. *Status: standing; pin advances only at cycle gates.*

**D-007 · 2026-07-22 — One accelerated clock; 1 game day = 60 real minutes `[TUNE]`; identical online and offline.**
The offline DNA demands one honest clock, and reconciliation must be trivially explainable ("8 hours passed = 8 game days"). Death is out of C01 scope, so the worst overnight outcome is a cold morning report, not a corpse; the offline-drain/death balance is a Cycle 02 design item. *Status: revisit in playtest.*

**D-006 · 2026-07-22 — Cycle 01 = "First Fire": the steel thread (crash → move → gather wood → fire → warmth → quit → reopen → morning report) on the permanent architecture.**
The DNA — delta-time reconciliation — is both the signature and the biggest risk; prove it first, on a phone, before widening scope. Stage 0 also stands up the entire pipeline, so every later cycle inherits it for free. *Status: standing (scope); all values `[TUNE]`.*

**D-005 · 2026-07-22 — `tune.ts` is the single home of every `[TUNE]` constant; the cycle log's TUNE ledger mirrors it.**
Tuning cycles touch one file, and the director's TUNE feedback maps 1:1 to named constants. *Status: standing.*

**D-004 · 2026-07-22 — Canon lives in the repo.**
From C01, the living four (state, decisions log, cycle log, codex) are canonical at `/docs/`. C1 reads them via raw GitHub fetch at boot; C1's own updates ride into the repo as doc deltas inside handoff blocks. The Project library permanently holds only the charter and the ops protocol. This cuts the director's file shuffling to roughly zero. *Fallback:* if the fetch ever fails, the director drops current copies into the chat. *Status: reversible.*

**D-003 · 2026-07-22 — Pipeline: public GitHub repo + GitHub Pages, auto-deploy on push, per-cycle archives, tag per cycle.**
Zero cost, a stable phone link, real version control — and a public repo is what makes D-004's raw reads possible. Nothing secret ships pre-launch; revisit visibility at launch (Pages on a private repo needs a paid plan; itch.io or Netlify are drop-in alternates). *Status: reversible.*

**D-002 · 2026-07-22 — The second-session audit runs as a fresh-context agent in the repo** (a clean-context subagent spawned by C2 at the end of BUILD, or a separate session), rather than a chat session.
An in-repo auditor can read the code, run the checks, and play the link — and the subagent form needs no director relay. Fresh context is the actual requirement of Charter §II.4, and both forms satisfy it. *Status: standing.*

**D-001 · 2026-07-22 — Ops Protocol v1.0 adopted.**
Crew of Director + C1 Orchestrator + C2 Builder + C3 Auditor (plus later hats); the seven-stage cycle (OPEN → BUILD → AUDIT → SHIP → PLAYTEST → PERFECT → CLOSE); the three-gate definition of done; FIX/TUNE/NEXT/PARK triage; the fork rule. Operationalizes Charter Part II at minimum ceremony. *Status: standing.*

## Parked
- **Play-as-an-animal-in-a-world** — separate concept, filed untouched (Charter Appendix A).
- **Design reference — Last Day on Earth (Kefir)** (director, 2026-07-23): the mobile-survival
  playability bar — camera, one-thumb legibility, and especially its building UX, which the
  director rates "simple and acceptable": grid tap-to-place floors/walls with in-place tier
  upgrades. Our version must be *more fluid, with smoother character movement* than LDOE.
  Feeds Cycle 04's construction design directly: Rust's tier/upkeep/decay economy inside
  LDOE-simple placement ergonomics. Gameplay reference only — LDOE's monetization and energy
  mechanics are excluded by the honest-systems rule (§I.8).
- **Design (parked) — Localized injury & healing system** (director, 2026-07-23): later-game
  medical depth. Injuries by location and severity — skin cuts (infection risk), arteries
  (rapid bleed-out), muscle, bone, spine, skull, lung, heart, kidney, liver, etc. — each with
  its own healing clock, treatment path, and legible impairment (limp, weakened grip, reduced
  stamina). Healing takes real time and flows through reconcile → morning-report material
  ("your arm mended; the leg wound looks infected — clean it"). Treatments enter the codex
  through the four gates (bandages, splints, boiled-water antiseptic…) and feed a First Aid
  skill on the Development Tree. Rails: D-011 holds (bleed-outs are active-play deaths only;
  offline, wounds stabilize/worsen to floors, never kill); Experience rules 2–3 hold (every
  injury telegraphed, preventable, explainable, treatable — no soft-locks in a solo run:
  spine/skull resolve as death or long recovery, never paralysis-stuck). Reference bar:
  RimWorld's per-part health model, Project Zomboid's wound care. Activates after threats
  exist (C06+ / Phase 2) — injuries need believable sources first.
